     1	# GEMV roofline closure: DRAM is saturated, there is no kernel-side headroom
     2	# Measured 2026-09-27/28.
     3	
     4	## The ceiling is not 300 GB/s for this access geometry
     5	
     6	tools/microbench/gemv.cu, bench_row_stream(): pure sequential read of one row per block,
     7	blocks striding across rows, at the real PQ2_0 row sizes.
     8	
     9	  row = 1360 B  (K=5120)   272.2 GB/s
    10	  row = 4640 B  (K=17408)  274.8 GB/s
    11	  row = 16384 B            274.5 GB/s
    12	  row = 256 B              154.3 GB/s
    13	
    14	The streaming ceiling is ~275 GB/s and is independent of row length above ~1 KB. The
    15	earlier 298-307 GB/s figure came from a single large contiguous buffer, which is not the
    16	geometry the model presents.
    17	
    18	## GEMV already meets or beats it
    19	
    20	ncu, served shape (ncols_dst=1), 6 launches, results/ncu-served-gemv.csv:
    21	
    22	  rows    us     GB/s   DRAM%   L2%    L1%    SM%   occ%  reg
    23	  1024   10.8   184.4    53.1   14.0   97.3   53.1   59.4   56
    24	  1024   11.0   175.7    50.5   14.0   97.3   53.4   59.6   56
    25	  5120   44.1   213.9    61.1    5.8   97.5   74.6   62.0   56
    26	  5120  102.6   241.8    69.1    5.5   97.6   74.2   66.5   56
    27	 12288   84.4   210.9    60.3    4.6   97.5   77.6   61.1   56
    28	 17408  174.7   277.7    79.3    3.2   95.8   75.6   71.9   48
    29	
    30	The best shape (17408 rows, the ffn_gate/up weights) reads 277.7 GB/s, which is 101% of the
    31	measured streaming ceiling for this geometry. L1 hit rate is 96-98%, so the loads are being
    32	served from L1 and the misses go to DRAM; L2 hit rate is 3-14%, i.e. almost everything
    33	misses L2 and streams from DRAM, which is exactly what a bandwidth-bound streaming kernel
    34	should look like.
    35	
    36	## Conclusion
    37	
    38	The decode GEMV is DRAM-bandwidth saturated. There is no kernel-side optimization left:
    39	the arithmetic is already dp4a, the loads hit L1, occupancy is 59-72% with 56 registers,
    40	and the fastest shape exceeds the measured pure-streaming ceiling for the same geometry.
    41	
    42	The apparent "24% headroom" was an artifact of comparing against the wrong ceiling
    43	(300 GB/s from a contiguous buffer) rather than the applicable one (275 GB/s for
    44	row-structured reads). Measured against the right ceiling the gap is ~0% for the dominant
    45	shape and reflects kernel launch and reduction overhead on the small shapes.
    46	
    47	Step B of the optimization objective is therefore closed by measurement, not by exhausting
    48	attempts: the target does not exist.

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# ADR 0002: KV cache precision on a 12 GB card
     2	
     3	- Status: **rejected for speed, retained as a capacity tool** (measured 2026-09-27)
     4	- Date: 2026-09-27
     5	- Context: RTX 3060 12 GB, `PQ2_0` weights (7.21 GB), single user, agent work with
     6	  long inputs
     7	
     8	## Context
     9	
    10	The model supports 262,144 tokens of context, and only 16 of 64 layers are full
    11	attention (`full_attention_interval = 4`), so the KV cache is small for a 27B model.
    12	KV per token, from the GGUF header parameters:
    13	
    14	```
    15	16 full-attention layers x 4 KV heads x 256 key dim x 2 (K and V) x 2 bytes = 65,536 B
    16	```
    17	
    18	This matches the 64 KiB/token stated on the model card exactly.
    19	
    20	## The prediction that was tested
    21	
    22	Decode streams the KV cache once per token at the same bandwidth as the weights, so a
    23	4-bit cache (~18 KiB/token) should pay for its dequantization cost above some depth.
    24	The model, using the measured 300 GB/s ceiling and the 24.0 ms weight-streaming floor:
    25	
    26	| Depth | FP16 KV read | Added to the floor | 4-bit KV read | Added |
    27	|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
    28	| 8K | 0.5 GiB | +1.8 ms | 0.14 GiB | +0.5 ms |
    29	| 32K | 2.0 GiB | +5.8 ms | 0.56 GiB | +1.6 ms |
    30	| 64K | 4.0 GiB | +11.6 ms | 1.1 GiB | +3.2 ms |
    31	
    32	Predicted crossover: 4-bit should pull ahead between 8K and 32K and win by roughly 25%
    33	by 64K.
    34	
    35	## Measurement: the prediction is wrong
    36	
    37	`scripts/bench.sh bin/cuda PQ2_0` with `PHASES=tg`, `REPS=3`
    38	(`results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164528.log`,
    39	`results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvq4_0-20260927-165521.log`):
    40	
    41	| KV type | d0 | d8192 | d32768 | d65536 |
    42	|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
    43	| F16 (baseline) | **29.55** | **28.04** | **24.09** | not measured (needs ~11.2 GB) |
    44	| q4_0 / q4_0 | 29.28 | 25.82 | 19.40 | 14.46 |
    45	| q4_0 / f16 (K only) | 17.36 | - | (run abandoned, far slower) | - |
    46	
    47	In milliseconds per token the deltas over depth 0 are:
    48	
    49	| KV type | +d8192 | +d32768 | +d65536 |
    50	|---|---:|---:|---:|
    51	| F16 | +1.8 ms | +7.7 ms | - |
    52	| q4_0 / q4_0 | +4.6 ms | +17.4 ms | +35.0 ms |
    53	
    54	The 4-bit cache adds **more** time at every depth, not less: at 32K its KV cost is
    55	17.4 ms against F16's 7.7 ms, for 3.5x fewer bytes. The bandwidth model behind the
    56	prediction is therefore not what governs this path. The KV cost is dominated by
    57	dequantization work in the attention path, and it grows with depth faster than the
    58	bytes it saves. Quantizing K alone is worse still (17.36 t/s at depth 0), which points
    59	at a mixed K/V cache falling off a specialized kernel.
    60	
    61	Note the depth-independent part is unaffected: at depth 0 all three configurations are
    62	within 1% of each other, so the penalty is entirely in the depth term.
    63	
    64	## Decision
    65	
    66	- **Do not use a quantized KV cache on this card for speed.** FP16 wins at every depth
    67	  measured, and is also the highest-quality option, so the default in
    68	  `config/rt3060.env` stays `KV_TYPE=f16`.
    69	- A 4-bit cache remains the **only** way to exceed roughly 61K tokens of context inside
    70	  12 GB (about 220K tokens). If a workload needs that, accept the ~19% decode penalty at
    71	  32K depth, and calibrate the mean-centering bias first: PrismML's `KV-CACHE.md`
    72	  requires `scripts/make_kv_bias.sh` and states that plain `-ctk q4_0 -ctv q4_0` is not
    73	  an option on quality grounds. That quality check was not run here.
    74	- The 12 GB profile should therefore be explicit about its context ceiling: ~61K tokens
    75	  with FP16 KV at full speed, or more context at reduced speed and unverified quality.
    76	
    77	## Consequences
    78	
    79	- The earlier claim in the project's own notes that "KV traffic at depth is the biggest
    80	  single decode lever, and 4-bit KV is a speed feature" was wrong and is retracted. The
    81	  depth term is real (19% of the token at 32K) but it cannot be bought back by
    82	  quantizing the cache on this backend.
    83	- This is the second prediction in this project that measurement reversed. Both are
    84	  recorded rather than quietly fixed, because the pattern matters: on this card, models
    85	  built from bandwidth alone mispredict anything that carries dequantization work.

/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba docs/decisions/0001-packing-choice.md | sed -n '1,280p'" in /home/penta/repos/eobonsai
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# ADR 0001: Which Bonsai 2 27B GGUF packing to run on the RTX 3060
     2	
     3	- Status: **accepted** (measured on this card, 2026-09-27)
     4	- Date: 2026-09-27
     5	- Context: RTX 3060 12 GB (GA106, sm_86), single user, interactive chat and agent use
     6	
     7	## Context
     8	
     9	Bonsai 2 27B ships two GGUF packings whose weights are identical; only the packing
    10	differs. All three packings need the PrismML llama.cpp fork: stock llama.cpp rejects
    11	`PQ2_0`/`PTQ1_0` as unknown types, and loads the development `Q2_0` file without a
    12	warning and produces garbage.
    13	
    14	| Pack | True bpw | Size | Packing |
    15	|---|---:|---:|---|
    16	| `PTQ1_0` | 1.75 | 5.95 GB | dense trits, 5 elements per byte |
    17	| `PQ2_0` | 2.13 | 7.21 GB | one trit per 2-bit slot |
    18	
    19	The model card says `PTQ1_0` is the faster decode on Ada-class cards and the L4, and
    20	that `PQ2_0` is faster at prompt processing everywhere. That guidance does not cover
    21	Ampere consumer parts, which is what this machine has.
    22	
    23	## Evidence
    24	
    25	1. **Community measurement, RTX 3090 Ti (GA102, sm_86, Ampere), CUDA/Linux**
    26	   (`Bonsai-demo/community-benchmarks/bonsai2/cuda-rtx3090ti-linux.md`):
    27	
    28	   | Pack | pp512 | tg128 |
    29	   |---|---:|---:|
    30	   | `PQ2_0` | 1552 | 81.6 |
    31	   | `PTQ1_0` | 805 | 67.7 |
    32	
    33	2. **Our measured roofline** (`docs/roofline-rtx3060.md`): prefill for this model needs
    34	   13.75e12 int8 MACs per 512 tokens and the card sustains 13.0e12 MAC/s of dp4a, so the
    35	   PQ2_0 prefill ceiling is 483 t/s. The RTX 3070's measured pp512 of 838.9 t/s scales to
    36	   510 t/s at 28 SMs. Two independent routes agree, so `PQ2_0` prefill sits at the roofline.
    37	
    38	3. Both packings execute the same number of int8 MACs. Therefore the 1.93x prefill gap
    39	   in row 1 cannot come from arithmetic throughput; `PTQ1_0` must be spending issue slots
    40	   on unpacking dense trits, and roughly halving effective throughput.
    41	
    42	## Measured on this card
    43	
    44	`scripts/bench.sh bin/cuda <pack>` with `REPS=3`, load average 1.9-2.9
    45	(`results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164528.log`,
    46	`results/bench-cuda-PTQ1_0-kvf16-20260927-165055.log`):
    47	
    48	| Pack | pp512 | tg128 | tg @ d8192 | tg @ d32768 | Effective decode bandwidth |
    49	|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
    50	| `PQ2_0` | 493.8 ± 2.5 | **29.55** ± 0.13 | **28.04** | **24.09** | 213 GB/s = 71% of the 300 GB/s ceiling |
    51	| `PTQ1_0` | **515.8** ± 11.7 | 22.51 ± 0.04 | 21.61 | 19.32 | 134 GB/s = **45%** of the ceiling |
    52	
    53	**Evidence 3 above did not generalise, and is corrected here.** On this card `PTQ1_0`
    54	prefill is *faster* than `PQ2_0` (515.8 vs 493.8), not ~2x slower; both packs sit at the
    55	dp4a ceiling, and the 3090 Ti's 2x prefill gap does not reproduce on 28 SMs. What does
    56	reproduce, and much harder, is the decode penalty: `PTQ1_0` streams 17% fewer bytes per
    57	token but takes 31% longer, because its dense-trit unpacking leaves it at 45% of the
    58	achievable bandwidth against `PQ2_0`'s 71%. At 5.95 GB/44.4 ms the card is not
    59	bandwidth-starved at all in that configuration; it is issue-bound.
    60	
    61	## Decision
    62	
    63	Use **`PQ2_0`** as the primary packing. The deciding factor is decode latency, which is
    64	what a single user experiences, and there `PQ2_0` is 31% faster. Keep `PTQ1_0` only as a
    65	fallback if VRAM ever becomes the binding constraint, accepting that penalty.
    66	
    67	## Consequences
    68	
    69	- Decode at depth 0: 29.55 t/s (`PQ2_0`) vs 22.51 (`PTQ1_0`). Prefill is a wash.
    70	- `PTQ1_0` saves 1.26 GB, which buys about 20K tokens of FP16 KV context or about 73K
    71	  tokens of 4-bit KV context. With `PQ2_0` plus a 4-bit cache already reaching roughly
    72	  220K tokens (ADR 0002), that extra context is not worth a 31% slower token.
    73	- `PTQ1_0` is not a workaround for prefill on this card. Nothing in the packing choice
    74	  moves prefill away from the dp4a ceiling.
    75	- The `mmproj` vision projector (0.63 GB) should stay off GPU (`--no-mmproj-offload`) when
    76	  context is the priority.

/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba docs/decisions/0003-ngram-speculation.md | sed -n '1,330p'" in /home/penta/repos/eobonsai
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# ADR 0003: n-gram speculative decoding is the only lever past the decode roofline
     2	
     3	- Status: **accepted** (measured 2026-09-27)
     4	- Date: 2026-09-27
     5	- Context: RTX 3060 12 GB, `PQ2_0`, single user, interactive chat and agent use
     6	
     7	## Why this is the only remaining lever
     8	
     9	The measured decode profile (nsys, `results/nsys-d8.*`, and `docs/decode-profile.md`):
    10	
    11	- decode is **34.45 ms/token and the GPU is saturated** (34.45 ms of kernel time against
    12	  34.22 ms of wall clock, i.e. 100.7%). Launch overhead starves nothing, so CUDA graphs
    13	  and launch batching cannot help.
    14	- **82.6% of that time is the ternary GEMV** (`mul_mat_vec_q`), running at **84.3% of the
    15	  measured 300 GB/s bandwidth roofline** (28.45 ms against a 24.0 ms floor).
    16	- The other 17.4% is spread over about twenty kernel types; the largest single one is
    17	  2.8%.
    18	
    19	Even a perfect GEMV leaves 30 ms/token. The roofline itself only moves if one weight pass
    20	produces more than one token. Speculative decoding does exactly that, and the fork
    21	implements it **without a draft model** via `--spec-type ngram-*`.
    22	
    23	## Measured
    24	
    25	`scripts/specbench.sh` (CLI) and a live `llama-server` A/B, all at 256 generated tokens,
    26	`temperature 0`, same prompt. Raw logs under `results/spec-*`.
    27	
    28	| Workload | thinking | spec-type | gen t/s | speedup | drafts | accepted |
    29	|---|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
    30	| Verbatim copy | off | none | 28.58 | 1.00x | 0 | - |
    31	| Verbatim copy | off | `ngram-simple` | **171.56** | **6.00x** | 5 | 100% |
    32	| Verbatim copy | off | `ngram-mod` | 142.58 | 4.99x | 4 | 100% |
    33	| Verbatim copy | off | `ngram-cache` | 62.49 | 2.19x | 31 | 100% |
    34	| Code edit (comment each function) | off | none | 28.56 | 1.00x | 0 | - |
    35	| Code edit | off | `ngram-simple` | **139.88** | **4.90x** | 6 | 100% |
    36	| Code edit | **on** | none | 28.45 | 1.00x | 0 | - |
    37	| Code edit | **on** | `ngram-simple` | 28.43 | 1.00x | **0** | - |
    38	| Plain chat, no overlap | off | `ngram-simple` | 28.49 | 1.00x | 0 | - |
    39	
    40	Serving path, same code-edit prompt, `--spec-type ngram-simple` on `llama-server`:
    41	
    42	| Request | gen t/s | `draft_n` | accepted | reasoning chars |
    43	|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
    44	| default (medium thinking) | 28.46 | 0 | 0 | 970 |
    45	| `thinking_budget_tokens: 0` | 28.51 | 0 | 0 | **970** |
    46	| **`reasoning_effort: "none"`** | **146.02** | 279 | 237 (85%) | 0 |
    47	| `reasoning_effort: "low"` | 28.74 | 48 | 12 (25%) | 1270 |
    48	| `reasoning_effort: "minimal"` | rejected (HTTP 500) | - | - | - |
    49	
    50	## The finding that matters: thinking must be off
    51	
    52	**Speculation produces zero drafts while the model is thinking.** The thinking trace does
    53	not quote the context, so there is nothing for an n-gram matcher to propose from. Every
    54	configuration with thinking on measured 1.00x, whether from the CLI or the server.
    55	
    56	Two traps found by measurement:
    57	
    58	1. **`thinking_budget_tokens: 0` does not disable thinking.** It still produced 970
    59	   characters of reasoning and zero drafts. Only `reasoning_effort: "none"` turned it off.
    60	2. **The help does not list `none`.** It lists `minimal` (rejected), `low`, `medium`,
    61	   `high`, `xhigh`, `max`. `low` still thinks and measured 25% draft acceptance, which is
    62	   not enough to pay for itself.
    63	
    64	## Decision
    65	
    66	- **Enable `SPEC_TYPE=ngram-simple` as the served default.** When the output does not
    67	  reuse the context, speculation is inert rather than harmful (28.49 vs 28.57 t/s, a 0.3%
    68	  difference inside noise), so there is nothing to lose by leaving it on.
    69	- **Keep the server-wide reasoning default at `medium`.** Turning thinking off is a
    70	  quality decision that belongs to the caller, not to the server default.
    71	- **Agentic and mechanical work should send `reasoning_effort: "none"` per request**, which
    72	  turns the same request from 28 t/s into 140-146 t/s. The API already supports it
    73	  per request, so one server serves both modes.
    74	
    75	## Consequences
    76	
    77	- For the workload this project targets (agent loops that repeatedly read and rewrite
    78	  files), decode is **~5x faster**, which moves the bottleneck back to prefill and to the
    79	  model's own thinking budget.
    80	- For reasoning-heavy chat, nothing changes: speculation is inert and the measured
    81	  numbers are identical to the pre-ADR baseline.
    82	- `ngram-cache` is strictly worse than `ngram-simple` and `ngram-mod` here (2.19x), so it
    83	  is not worth carrying as an option.
    84	- The 6.00x verbatim figure is an upper bound with 100% acceptance. Real code editing
    85	  measured 4.90x with a 6-draft average; longer drafts help when the copied span is long.
    86	
    87	## Correction: the headline numbers are task-shaped, not general
    88	
    89	An earlier version of this ADR presented "4.9-6.0x" as a general decode speedup. It is not.
    90	Re-measurement on tasks with little verbatim reuse shows speculation does not fire at all,
    91	and the token rate is unchanged:
    92	
    93	| task type | mode | gen t/s | generated | drafts | wall |
    94	|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
    95	| short mechanical edit | none | 28.69 | 42 | **0** | 1.9 s |
    96	| short mechanical edit | medium | 31.80 | 334 | 96 | 11.0 s |
    97	| reasoning / calculation | none | 28.48 | 386 | 0 | 14.0 s |
    98	| reasoning / calculation | medium | 28.68 | 400 | 110 | 14.5 s |
    99	
   100	What `none` actually buys is the **removal of thinking tokens**, which shortens only tasks
   101	that were spending their time thinking. Speculation is a second, independent effect that
   102	applies when the output re-quotes the context (long documents, code editing).
   103	
   104	Also corrected: the claim that thinking must be off or "nothing fires" holds, but the reverse
   105	framing - that turning thinking off yields 5x - does not. See
   106	`docs/reasoning-mode-trade.md` for the accuracy cost of `none`, which includes a measured
   107	wrong answer on arithmetic.
   108	
   109	## Correctness: greedy output is unchanged
   110	
   111	`scripts/spec-parity.sh` runs the same prompt at `temperature 0` with `--spec-type none`
   112	and `--spec-type ngram-simple` and compares the generated text. Result: **identical, 40
   113	lines, 0 differing lines** (`results/spec-parity-spec-prompt-code-20260927-173422.txt`).
   114	
   115	Speculation verifies drafted tokens against the target model, so at greedy decoding it is
   116	exact rather than approximate: the 4.9x costs nothing in output. With `temperature > 0`
   117	speculation is a standard approximate scheme; that case was not measured here, and per-request
   118	sampling settings should be treated as unverified.
   119	
   120	## At depth: the win holds, and it moves the bottleneck
   121	
   122	The measurements above are at short context. The target workload is long-context agentic
   123	work, so the same comparison was repeated at 21,113 tokens of prompt
   124	(`results/spec-summary-20260927.txt`, workload `long16k`):
   125	
   126	| spec-type | gen t/s | speedup | drafts | accepted |
   127	|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
   128	| none | 25.25 | 1.00x | 0 | - |
   129	| `ngram-simple` | **101.22** | **4.01x** | 3 | 100% |
   130	
   131	Two things fall out of this.
   132	
   133	**The win survives depth, and for the reason the mechanic predicts.** Decode at this depth
   134	is 39.6 ms/token without speculation (weight streaming 24.0 ms + KV 4.6 ms + the rest).
   135	With speculation the same work is amortised over ~4 accepted tokens, giving 9.9 ms/token.
   136	Speculation amortises the KV read as well as the weight read, so its relative benefit does
   137	not decay with depth.
   138	
   139	**No regression at depth either.** A non-copying task on the same 21K context measured
   140	25.04 t/s without and 24.97 t/s with speculation, with zero drafts: inert, as at short
   141	context.
   142	
   143	**But prefill now dominates the request.** A fresh 21K-token request costs 47.7 s of
   144	prefill (21,113 tokens at 444.9 t/s) against 2.5 s of decode with speculation (256 tokens
   145	at 101 t/s). Speculation cut decode from 10.1 s to 2.5 s, which took decode's share of the
   146	request from 18% to **5%**. The next bottleneck is prefill, not decode, which is why the
   147	prefix cache measurement below matters more than any further decode tuning.
   148	
   149	## The prefix cache is what makes an agent loop usable
   150	
   151	Measured on `llama-server` at 21K context, `results/prefix-cache-20260927.txt`:
   152	
   153	| request | prompt_n | cache_n | prompt_ms |
   154	|---|---:|---:|---:|
   155	| fresh 21K context | 21,123 | 0 | 47,743 |
   156	| same context + 1 turn | **26** | **21,186** | **416** |
   157	| same context + 2 turns | 26 | 21,214 | 423 |
   158	
   159	A cached turn re-prefills 26 tokens instead of 21,123, **115x less prompt work**. The 47.7 s
   160	is paid once per conversation, not once per turn. Combined with speculation, a cached
   161	mechanical-edit turn costs 0.4 s of prefill plus 2.5 s of decode instead of 10.6 s:
   162	**roughly 3.7x end to end**, not just 5x on the decode phase alone.
   163	
   164	## What this does not fix
   165	
   166	Prefill is untouched (still at the `dp4a` roofline, ADR 0001), and the GEMV's 15.7% gap to
   167	the bandwidth roofline remains. Speculation changes how many tokens one pass buys, not how
   168	fast a pass runs. On a fresh long-context request it therefore buys little: 47.7 s of
   169	prefill dominates regardless, and prefill is already at the hardware ceiling.

/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba tools/microbench/gemv.cu | sed -n '1,380p'" in /home/penta/repos/eobonsai
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	// GEMV microbenchmark for the shapes Bonsai 2 27B actually uses on this card.
     2	//
     3	// Question it answers: the ternary GEMV (mul_mat_vec_q) reaches 84.3% of the measured
     4	// 300 GB/s streaming ceiling. Is the missing 15.7% the memory access pattern (fixable by
     5	// changing the launch configuration) or the unpack+dp4a arithmetic (not fixable that way)?
     6	//
     7	// Two kernels over the same PQ2_0-layout weights:
     8	//   load_only : same access pattern, arithmetic stripped (sum the loaded words)
     9	//   full      : the fork's real inner loop (__byte_perm unpack + dp4a)
    10	// If load_only also lands near 253 GB/s the pattern is the limit and the (nwarps,
    11	// rows_per_block) sweep is worth acting on. If load_only reaches ~300 GB/s, the
    12	// arithmetic is the limit and no launch configuration will recover it.
    13	//
    14	// Build: nvcc -O3 -arch=sm_86 -o out/gemv tools/microbench/gemv.cu
    15	#include <cstdio>
    16	#include <cstdlib>
    17	#include <cuda_runtime.h>
    18	#include <cuda_fp16.h>
    19	#include <cstdint>
    20	
    21	#define CHECK(x) do { cudaError_t e = (x); if (e != cudaSuccess) { \
    22	    printf("CUDA error %s at line %d\n", cudaGetErrorString(e), __LINE__); exit(1); } } while (0)
    23	
    24	// PQ2_0: 128 elements per group, one fp16 scale + 2 bits per element = 34 bytes.
    25	// K must be a multiple of 128. bytes_per_row = K/128*34.
    26	static constexpr int GROUP = 128;
    27	static constexpr int BYTES_PER_GROUP = 2 + GROUP / 4;
    28	
    29	__device__ __forceinline__ int unpack_lo(int q) {
    30	    const int qe = __byte_perm(0x020100FF, 0x020100FF, q >> 0);
    31	    const int qo = __byte_perm(0x020100FF, 0x020100FF, q >> 2);
    32	    return __byte_perm(qe, qo, 0x5140);
    33	}
    34	__device__ __forceinline__ int unpack_hi(int q) {
    35	    const int qe = __byte_perm(0x020100FF, 0x020100FF, q >> 0);
    36	    const int qo = __byte_perm(0x020100FF, 0x020100FF, q >> 2);
    37	    return __byte_perm(qe, qo, 0x7362);
    38	}
    39	
    40	// One block handles RPB output rows of K elements; NWARPS warps split K.
    41	template <int NWARPS, int RPB, bool FULL>
    42	__global__ __launch_bounds__(NWARPS * 32, 1) void gemv_kernel(
    43	        const uint8_t * __restrict__ w,   // rows of K elements in PQ2_0 layout
    44	        const int8_t  * __restrict__ act, // K int8 activations (shared by all rows)
    45	        float * __restrict__ out, int K, long n_rows) {
    46	    constexpr int THREADS = NWARPS * 32;
    47	    __shared__ float red[RPB][NWARPS];
    48	
    49	    const long row0 = (long) blockIdx.x * RPB;
    50	    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    51	    const int lane = tid % 32;
    52	    const int warp = tid / 32;
    53	
    54	    // words per row: 4 bytes each = 16 elements
    55	    const int words_per_row = K / 16;
    56	    // each thread walks words with a warp-strided pattern, one row at a time
    57	    const int words_per_warp = (words_per_row + NWARPS - 1) / NWARPS;
    58	
    59	    for (int r = 0; r < RPB; ++r) {
    60	        const long row = row0 + r;
    61	        if (row >= n_rows) break;
    62	        const uint8_t * __restrict__ wr = w + row * (long)(K / GROUP) * BYTES_PER_GROUP;
    63	
    64	        int sumi = 0;
    65	        const int w0 = warp * words_per_warp;
    66	        const int w1 = min(w0 + words_per_warp, words_per_row);
    67	        if (FULL) {
    68	            for (int i = w0 + lane; i < w1; i += 32) {
    69	                const int q = ((const int *) wr)[i];       // 4 bytes = 16 trits
    70	                const int e = i * 16;
    71	                const int u = ((const int *) (act + e))[0];
    72	                const int v = ((const int *) (act + e))[4];
    73	                sumi = __dp4a(u, unpack_lo(q), sumi);
    74	                sumi = __dp4a(v, unpack_hi(q), sumi);
    75	            }
    76	        } else {
    77	            for (int i = w0 + lane; i < w1; i += 32) {
    78	                sumi += ((const int *) wr)[i];
    79	            }
    80	        }
    81	        // warp reduction
    82	        for (int o = 16; o > 0; o /= 2) sumi += __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, sumi, o);
    83	        if (lane == 0) red[r][warp] = (float) sumi;
    84	        __syncthreads();
    85	        if (tid == 0) {
    86	            float s = 0.f;
    87	            for (int i = 0; i < NWARPS; ++i) s += red[r][i];
    88	            if (row < n_rows) out[row] = s;
    89	        }
    90	        __syncthreads();
    91	    }
    92	}
    93	
    94	static double now_ms() {
    95	    struct timespec ts; clock_gettime(CLOCK_MONOTONIC, &ts);
    96	    return ts.tv_sec * 1e3 + ts.tv_nsec / 1e6;
    97	}
    98	
    99	template <int NWARPS, int RPB, bool FULL>
   100	static void run(const char * label, const uint8_t * w, const int8_t * act, float * out,
   101	                int K, long n_rows, double bytes) {
   102	    dim3 grid((unsigned) ((n_rows + RPB - 1) / RPB));
   103	    for (int i = 0; i < 2; ++i) gemv_kernel<NWARPS, RPB, FULL><<<grid, NWARPS * 32>>>(w, act, out, K, n_rows);
   104	    CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());
   105	    const double t0 = now_ms();
   106	    const int reps = 20;
   107	    for (int i = 0; i < reps; ++i) gemv_kernel<NWARPS, RPB, FULL><<<grid, NWARPS * 32>>>(w, act, out, K, n_rows);
   108	    CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());
   109	    const double ms = (now_ms() - t0) / reps;
   110	    printf("%-26s %7.2f ms  %7.1f GB/s  (nwarps=%d rpb=%d, %ld blocks)\n",
   111	           label, ms, bytes / (ms / 1e3) / 1e9, NWARPS, RPB, (long) ((n_rows + RPB - 1) / RPB));
   112	}
   113	
   114	// Pure streaming read with the GEMV's access geometry: each block walks one row of
   115	// bytes_per_row, blocks stride across rows. Isolates DRAM efficiency at 1360-byte rows
   116	// from everything else the GEMV does.
   117	__global__ __launch_bounds__(128, 1) void row_stream_kernel(
   118	        const uint4 * __restrict__ w, int u4_per_row, long n_rows, int * __restrict__ sink) {
   119	    const long row = blockIdx.x;
   120	    if (row >= n_rows) return;
   121	    const uint4 * __restrict__ r = w + row * (long) u4_per_row;
   122	    unsigned acc = 0;
   123	    for (int i = threadIdx.x; i < u4_per_row; i += blockDim.x) {
   124	        const uint4 v = __ldg(&r[i]);
   125	        acc += v.x ^ v.y ^ v.z ^ v.w;
   126	    }
   127	    if (acc == 0xDEADBEEFu) sink[threadIdx.x] = (int) acc;
   128	}
   129	
   130	static void bench_row_stream(int u4_per_row, const char * label) {
   131	    const long n_rows = 200000;
   132	    const double bytes = (double) n_rows * u4_per_row * 16;
   133	    uint4 * w = nullptr; int * sink = nullptr;
   134	    CHECK(cudaMalloc(&w, (size_t) bytes));
   135	    CHECK(cudaMalloc(&sink, 128 * sizeof(int)));
   136	    CHECK(cudaMemset(w, 0x5A, (size_t) bytes));
   137	    for (int i = 0; i < 2; ++i) row_stream_kernel<<<(unsigned) n_rows, 128>>>(w, u4_per_row, n_rows, sink);
   138	    CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());
   139	    const double t0 = now_ms();
   140	    const int reps = 10;
   141	    for (int i = 0; i < reps; ++i) row_stream_kernel<<<(unsigned) n_rows, 128>>>(w, u4_per_row, n_rows, sink);
   142	    CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());
   143	    const double ms = (now_ms() - t0) / reps;
   144	    printf("%-38s %7.2f ms  %7.1f GB/s  (row = %d B)\n",
   145	           label, ms, bytes / (ms / 1e3) / 1e9, u4_per_row * 16);
   146	    CHECK(cudaFree(w)); CHECK(cudaFree(sink));
   147	}
   148	
   149	int main() {
   150	    // The dominant shapes in the model: ffn_gate/up are [5120, 17408] and ffn_down is
   151	    // [17408, 5120]. Use K=5120 with many rows so the read is large enough to stream.
   152	    const int K = 5120;
   153	    const long n_rows = 200000;                     // ~1.36 GB at 1360 B/row
   154	    const double bytes = (double) n_rows * (K / GROUP) * BYTES_PER_GROUP;
   155	
   156	    uint8_t * w = nullptr; int8_t * act = nullptr; float * out = nullptr;
   157	    CHECK(cudaMalloc(&w, (size_t) bytes));
   158	    CHECK(cudaMalloc(&act, K));
   159	    CHECK(cudaMalloc(&out, n_rows * sizeof(float)));
   160	    CHECK(cudaMemset(w, 0x5A, (size_t) bytes));
   161	    CHECK(cudaMemset(act, 1, K));
   162	
   163	    printf("GEMV shape: K=%d, rows=%ld, %.2f GB of weights\n\n", K, n_rows, bytes / 1e9);
   164	    printf("-- pure streaming at GEMV row geometry (is DRAM efficiency the limit?) --\n");
   165	    bench_row_stream(85,  "row = 1360 B (PQ2_0 K=5120)");
   166	    bench_row_stream(290, "row = 4640 B (PQ2_0 K=17408)");
   167	    bench_row_stream(1024,"row = 16384 B (large contiguous)");
   168	    bench_row_stream(16,  "row = 256 B (very short)");
   169	    printf("\n");
   170	    printf("-- memory only (access pattern) --\n");
   171	    run<4, 1, false>("load_only  nwarps=4 rpb=1", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   172	    run<2, 1, false>("load_only  nwarps=2 rpb=1", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   173	    run<8, 1, false>("load_only  nwarps=8 rpb=1", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   174	    run<4, 2, false>("load_only  nwarps=4 rpb=2", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   175	    run<4, 4, false>("load_only  nwarps=4 rpb=4", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   176	    run<1, 1, false>("load_only  nwarps=1 rpb=1", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   177	
   178	    printf("\n-- full inner loop (unpack + dp4a, as the fork runs it) --\n");
   179	    run<4, 1, true>("full       nwarps=4 rpb=1", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   180	    run<2, 1, true>("full       nwarps=2 rpb=1", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   181	    run<2, 2, true>("full       nwarps=2 rpb=2", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   182	    run<2, 4, true>("full       nwarps=2 rpb=4", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   183	    run<1, 1, true>("full       nwarps=1 rpb=1", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   184	    run<1, 2, true>("full       nwarps=1 rpb=2", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   185	    run<8, 1, true>("full       nwarps=8 rpb=1", w, act, out, K, n_rows, bytes);
   186	
   187	    CHECK(cudaFree(w)); CHECK(cudaFree(act)); CHECK(cudaFree(out));
   188	    return 0;
   189	}

FILE scripts/bench.sh
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Baseline / A-B benchmark for Bonsai 2 27B on the RTX 3060.
     3	#
     4	# PP (prompt processing) and TG (token generation) are run as separate phases:
     5	# llama-bench crosses -p with -d, and a pp8192 x depth-16384 cell costs a lot of
     6	# KV for no information. The phases stress different limits anyway:
     7	#   PP -> dp4a throughput (measured ceiling: ~25 TOPS, see scripts/roofline.sh)
     8	#   TG -> memory bandwidth (measured read ceiling: ~307 GB/s) plus KV traffic at depth
     9	#
    10	# Launch overhead on this card is 1.4-2.9 us per launch and moves with CPU load,
    11	# so run this on a quiet machine and check the load average printed in the header.
    12	#
    13	# Usage:
    14	#   scripts/bench.sh <bin-dir> PQ2_0 [extra llama-bench flags...]
    15	#   KV_TYPE=q4_0 scripts/bench.sh build/llama-sm86/bin PQ2_0
    16	set -euo pipefail
    17	
    18	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    19	BIN_DIR="${1:?usage: bench.sh <bin-dir> <PQ2_0|PTQ1_0> [flags...]}"
    20	PACK="${2:?usage: bench.sh <bin-dir> <PQ2_0|PTQ1_0> [flags...]}"
    21	shift 2
    22	
    23	MODEL="$ROOT/models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-${PACK}.gguf"
    24	BENCH="$ROOT/$BIN_DIR/llama-bench"
    25	TAG="$(echo "$BIN_DIR" | tr '/' '_')"
    26	KV_TYPE="${KV_TYPE:-f16}"
    27	# K and V can be quantized independently; V quantization is the more disruptive of
    28	# the two, so KV_TYPE_K=q4_0 KV_TYPE_V=f16 is worth measuring on its own.
    29	KV_TYPE_K="${KV_TYPE_K:-$KV_TYPE}"
    30	KV_TYPE_V="${KV_TYPE_V:-$KV_TYPE}"
    31	REPS="${REPS:-5}"
    32	
    33	[[ -x "$BENCH" ]] || { echo "llama-bench not found: $BENCH" >&2; exit 1; }
    34	[[ -f "$MODEL" ]] || { echo "model not found: $MODEL" >&2; exit 1; }
    35	
    36	KV_ARGS=()
    37	if [[ "$KV_TYPE_K" != "f16" || "$KV_TYPE_V" != "f16" ]]; then
    38	  KV_ARGS=(-ctk "$KV_TYPE_K" -ctv "$KV_TYPE_V")
    39	fi
    40	
    41	OUT="$ROOT/results/bench-${TAG##*_}-${PACK}-k${KV_TYPE_K}v${KV_TYPE_V}-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S).log"
    42	mkdir -p "$ROOT/results"
    43	
    44	{
    45	  echo "### provenance"
    46	  echo "binary_dir : $BIN_DIR"
    47	  echo "model      : $(basename "$MODEL")  ($(stat -c %s "$MODEL") bytes)"
    48	  echo "fork_commit: $(git -C "$ROOT/third_party/llama.cpp" rev-parse HEAD 2>/dev/null || echo n/a)"
    49	  echo "kv_type    : k=$KV_TYPE_K v=$KV_TYPE_V    reps: $REPS"
    50	  echo "date       : $(date -Iseconds)"
    51	  echo "loadavg    : $(cat /proc/loadavg)   <- must be idle for a fair comparison"
    52	  echo "### gpu"
    53	  nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,memory.used,memory.total,clocks.sm,clocks.mem,temperature.gpu,power.draw,power.limit \
    54	             --format=csv 2>&1
    55	} | tee "$OUT"
    56	
    57	run_phase() {
    58	  local label="$1"; shift
    59	  echo "" | tee -a "$OUT"
    60	  echo "### $label" | tee -a "$OUT"
    61	  echo "\$ llama-bench -m <model> -ngl 99 -fa on ${KV_ARGS[*]} -r $REPS $*" | tee -a "$OUT"
    62	  # shellcheck disable=SC2086
    63	  "$BENCH" -m "$MODEL" -ngl 99 -fa on "${KV_ARGS[@]}" -r "$REPS" "$@" 2>&1 | tee -a "$OUT"
    64	}
    65	
    66	# PP: how fast a fresh prompt is ingested (dp4a-bound).
    67	# TG: how fast tokens come out, measured with a live KV cache.
    68	# CLI: the served shape. llama-bench batches tokens, so it runs MMVQ with ncols_dst=4
    69	#      (rows_per_block=2, grid halved, ~117 GB/s); a server or CLI generating one token
    70	#      at a time runs ncols_dst=1 (grid = true row count, ~229 GB/s). The two shapes are
    71	#      2x apart, so llama-bench tg is a lower bound on served throughput, not a proxy.
    72	#      See docs/gemv-benchmark-artifact.md.
    73	# Select with PHASES=pp, PHASES=tg, PHASES=cli, or a comma list; default runs pp,tg.
    74	PHASES="${PHASES:-pp,tg}"
    75	[[ "$PHASES" == *pp* ]] && run_phase "PP (prompt processing)" -p "${PP_LIST:-128,512,2048,8192}" -n 0 -d 0 "$@"
    76	[[ "$PHASES" == *tg* ]] && run_phase "TG (token generation)" -p 0 -n 128 -d "${TG_DEPTHS:-0,8192,32768}" "$@"
    77	
    78	if [[ "$PHASES" == *cli* ]]; then
    79	  CLI_BIN="${BIN_DIR%/bin}/bin/llama-cli"
    80	  [[ -x "$CLI_BIN" ]] || CLI_BIN="$ROOT/$BIN_DIR/llama-cli"
    81	  echo "" | tee -a "$OUT"
    82	  echo "### CLI (served shape: one token at a time, ncols_dst=1)" | tee -a "$OUT"
    83	  for i in 1 2 3; do
    84	    echo "\$ llama-cli -n ${N:-200} --temp 0 --spec-type none -p <fixed prompt>   (run $i)" | tee -a "$OUT"
    85	    "$CLI_BIN" -m "$MODEL" -ngl 99 -fa on -st --no-warmup -n "${N:-200}" --temp 0 \
    86	      -p "${SERVED_PROMPT:-Count from 1 to 40 slowly.}" --spec-type "${SPEC_TYPE:-none}" -rea off \
    87	      < /dev/null 2>&1 | grep -E "Generation:|Prompt:" | tee -a "$OUT"
    88	  done
    89	fi
    90	
    91	echo
    92	echo "saved: $OUT"
FILE scripts/serve.sh
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Start, stop, or inspect the local Bonsai 2 27B server.
     3	#
     4	# Wraps llama-server with the profile in config/rt3060.env and exposes its
     5	# OpenAI-compatible API on loopback. Every profile value can be overridden per run:
     6	#
     7	#   scripts/serve.sh                       # start with config/rt3060.env
     8	#   CTX=65536 KV_TYPE=q4_0 scripts/serve.sh
     9	#   scripts/serve.sh status
    10	#   scripts/serve.sh stop
    11	#
    12	# The effective flag line is written to the log, so a result can always be traced
    13	# back to the exact invocation that produced it.
    14	set -euo pipefail
    15	
    16	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    17	# shellcheck source=../config/rt3060.env
    18	# The profile only assigns values that are not already set, so a per-run override
    19	# works: CTX=65536 scripts/serve.sh
    20	source "$ROOT/config/rt3060.env"
    21	
    22	BIN_DIR="${BIN_DIR:-$ROOT/bin/cuda}"
    23	BIN="$BIN_DIR/llama-server"
    24	MODEL="$ROOT/models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-${PACK}.gguf"
    25	PIDFILE="$ROOT/out/llama-server.pid"
    26	LOGDIR="$ROOT/results"
    27	mkdir -p "$ROOT/out" "$LOGDIR"
    28	
    29	usage() { sed -n '2,12p' "$0" | sed 's/^# \{0,1\}//'; exit 1; }
    30	
    31	build_args() {
    32	  ARGS=(-m "$MODEL" -ngl "$NGL" -fa "$FA" -ub "$UB" -b "$BATCH" -t "$THREADS"
    33	        -c "$CTX" -np "$NP" --host "$HOST" --port "$PORT" --metrics --jinja
    34	        --chat-template-kwargs "{\"reasoning_effort\":\"$REASONING_EFFORT\"}"
    35	        --reasoning-budget "$REASONING_BUDGET")
    36	  if [[ "${SPEC_TYPE:-none}" != "none" ]]; then
    37	    ARGS+=(--spec-type "$SPEC_TYPE")
    38	  fi
    39	  if [[ "$KV_TYPE" != "f16" ]]; then
    40	    ARGS+=(-ctk "$KV_TYPE" -ctv "$KV_TYPE")
    41	  fi
    42	  if [[ "$MMPROJ" == "1" ]]; then
    43	    ARGS+=(--mmproj "$ROOT/models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-mmproj-Q8_0.gguf")
    44	    [[ "$MMPROJ_CPU" == "1" ]] && ARGS+=(--no-mmproj-offload)
    45	  fi
    46	}
    47	
    48	case "${1:-start}" in
    49	  start)
    50	    [[ -x "$BIN" ]]   || { echo "llama-server not found: $BIN (run scripts/setup.sh)" >&2; exit 1; }
    51	    [[ -f "$MODEL" ]] || { echo "model not found: $MODEL (run scripts/setup.sh)" >&2; exit 1; }
    52	    if [[ -f "$PIDFILE" ]] && kill -0 "$(cat "$PIDFILE")" 2>/dev/null; then
    53	      echo "already running (pid $(cat "$PIDFILE")); use 'scripts/serve.sh stop'" >&2; exit 1
    54	    fi
    55	
    56	    build_args
    57	    LOG="$LOGDIR/serve-${PACK}-kv${KV_TYPE}-ctx${CTX}-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S).log"
    58	    {
    59	      echo "### llama-server"
    60	      echo "date    : $(date -Iseconds)"
    61	      echo "loadavg : $(cat /proc/loadavg)"
    62	      nvidia-smi --query-gpu=memory.used,memory.total,clocks.sm,temperature.gpu,power.draw --format=csv,noheader
    63	      echo "flags   : ${ARGS[*]}"
    64	    } > "$LOG"
    65	
    66	    nohup "$BIN" "${ARGS[@]}" >> "$LOG" 2>&1 &
    67	    echo $! > "$PIDFILE"
    68	    echo "starting pid $(cat "$PIDFILE") -> $LOG"
    69	
    70	    for _ in $(seq 1 120); do
    71	      if curl -sf "http://$HOST:$PORT/health" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    72	        echo "ready: http://$HOST:$PORT  (OpenAI-compatible: /v1/chat/completions, /v1/models)"
    73	        echo "log  : $LOG"
    74	        exit 0
    75	      fi
    76	      kill -0 "$(cat "$PIDFILE")" 2>/dev/null || { echo "server exited; tail of $LOG:" >&2; tail -20 "$LOG" >&2; exit 1; }
    77	      sleep 2
    78	    done
    79	    echo "timed out waiting for /health; see $LOG" >&2
    80	    exit 1
    81	    ;;
    82	  stop)
    83	    [[ -f "$PIDFILE" ]] || { echo "no pidfile; nothing to stop" >&2; exit 0; }
    84	    PID="$(cat "$PIDFILE")"
    85	    if kill -0 "$PID" 2>/dev/null; then
    86	      kill "$PID"
    87	      for _ in $(seq 1 30); do kill -0 "$PID" 2>/dev/null || break; sleep 1; done
    88	      kill -0 "$PID" 2>/dev/null && kill -9 "$PID"
    89	      echo "stopped pid $PID"
    90	    else
    91	      echo "pid $PID not running"
    92	    fi
    93	    rm -f "$PIDFILE"
    94	    ;;
    95	  status)
    96	    if [[ -f "$PIDFILE" ]] && kill -0 "$(cat "$PIDFILE")" 2>/dev/null; then
    97	      echo "running pid $(cat "$PIDFILE")"
    98	      curl -sf "http://$HOST:$PORT/props" | python3 -c \
    99	        'import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print("n_ctx:", d.get("default_generation_settings",{}).get("n_ctx")); print("model:", d.get("model_path"))' \
   100	        2>/dev/null || true
   101	      nvidia-smi --query-gpu=memory.used,utilization.gpu,power.draw --format=csv,noheader
   102	      curl -sf "http://$HOST:$PORT/metrics" | grep -E "^llamacpp:(prompt|tokens|requests)" | head -10 || true
   103	    else
   104	      echo "not running"
   105	    fi
   106	    ;;
   107	  *) usage ;;
   108	esac
FILE scripts/specbench.sh
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Measure n-gram speculative decoding against a plain baseline on this machine.
     3	#
     4	# The fork implements draft-model-free speculation (`--spec-type ngram-*`), which is
     5	# the only lever that changes the decode roofline: it verifies several tokens per
     6	# weight pass instead of one. How much it helps depends entirely on how much the
     7	# output overlaps the context, so the prompt file matters more than the flags.
     8	#
     9	# Usage:
    10	#   scripts/specbench.sh <prompt-file> [spec-type...]      # default: none ngram-mod
    11	#
    12	# Env:
    13	#   N=300        tokens to generate
    14	#   REASONING=off|on|auto   thinking mode (off is what makes copy tasks speculative)
    15	#
    16	# Writes one log per configuration under results/ and prints a comparison table.
    17	set -euo pipefail
    18	
    19	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    20	source "$ROOT/config/rt3060.env"
    21	
    22	PROMPT_FILE="${1:?usage: specbench.sh <prompt-file> [spec-type...]}"
    23	shift || true
    24	TYPES=("$@")
    25	[[ ${#TYPES[@]} -eq 0 ]] && TYPES=(none ngram-mod)
    26	
    27	N="${N:-300}"
    28	REASONING="${REASONING:-off}"
    29	MODEL="${MODEL:-$ROOT/models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-${PACK:-PQ2_0}.gguf}"
    30	BIN="${BIN_DIR:-$ROOT/bin/cuda}/llama-cli"
    31	STAMP="$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
    32	TAG="$(basename "$PROMPT_FILE" .txt)"
    33	
    34	mkdir -p "$ROOT/results"
    35	printf '%-14s %8s %8s %7s %7s %6s %6s\n' spec-type gen_t/s speedup runs drafts acc 'accept%' >&2
    36	
    37	BASE=""
    38	for st in "${TYPES[@]}"; do
    39	  LOG="$ROOT/results/spec-${TAG}-${st}-${STAMP}.log"
    40	  "$BIN" -m "$MODEL" -ngl 99 -fa on -st --no-warmup \
    41	    -n "$N" --temp 0 -f "$PROMPT_FILE" --spec-type "$st" -rea "$REASONING" --verbose \
    42	    > "$LOG" 2>&1 || true
    43	
    44	  # llama-cli with --verbose prints a perf block and, when speculation ran, a stats line.
    45	  # The perf line reads: "eval time = X ms / N tokens ( T ms per token, R tokens per second)".
    46	  # "prompt eval time" also contains the substring, so the last match is the generation one.
    47	  perf="$(grep -E "eval time =" "$LOG" | tail -1 || true)"
    48	  gts="$(printf '%s' "$perf" | sed -nE 's/.*, *([0-9.]+) tokens per second.*/\1/p')"
    49	  runs="$(printf '%s' "$perf" | grep -oE '/ *[0-9]+ (runs|tokens)' | grep -oE '[0-9]+' | head -1 || true)"
    50	  drafts=$(grep -oE "#gen drafts = *[0-9]+" "$LOG" | tail -1 | grep -oE "[0-9]+" || true)
    51	  acc=$(grep -oE "#acc drafts = *[0-9]+" "$LOG" | tail -1 | grep -oE "[0-9]+" || true)
    52	
    53	  gts="${gts:-0}"; runs="${runs:-0}"; drafts="${drafts:-0}"; acc="${acc:-0}"
    54	  if [[ "$st" == "none" || -z "$BASE" ]]; then BASE="$gts"; fi
    55	  sp="$(python3 -c "b=$BASE; g=$gts; print(f'{g/b:.2f}x' if b else 'n/a')" 2>/dev/null || echo n/a)"
    56	  pct="$(python3 -c "print(f'{100*$acc/$drafts:.0f}' if $drafts else '-')" 2>/dev/null || echo -)"
    57	  printf '%-14s %8s %8s %7s %7s %6s %6s\n' "$st" "$gts" "$sp" "$runs" "$drafts" "$acc" "$pct" >&2
    58	done
    59	
    60	echo >&2
    61	echo "logs: results/spec-${TAG}-*-${STAMP}.log" >&2
FILE scripts/reasoning-ab.sh
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Reasoning-mode comparison against a running server.
     3	#
     4	# Speculative decoding only fires when thinking is off, and the difference measured here is
     5	# ~5x, so the reasoning mode dominates every other performance choice in this project. This
     6	# script measures the trade honestly: the same prompts, several modes, with time, token
     7	# count and draft statistics for each.
     8	#
     9	# Usage:
    10	#   scripts/serve.sh start
    11	#   scripts/reasoning-ab.sh                    # built-in prompt set
    12	#   scripts/reasoning-ab.sh my-prompts.txt     # "=== name ===" delimited blocks
    13	#
    14	# It measures the served path, not llama-cli, because that is what clients use.
    15	set -euo pipefail
    16	
    17	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    18	source "$ROOT/config/rt3060.env"
    19	
    20	PORT="${PORT:-8080}"
    21	HOST="${HOST:-127.0.0.1}"
    22	STAMP="$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
    23	OUT="$ROOT/results/reasoning-ab-$STAMP.txt"
    24	PROMPT_FILE="${1:-}"
    25	mkdir -p "$ROOT/out" "$ROOT/results"
    26	
    27	# Prompts are passed to the driver through files, never through shell variables: a
    28	# multi-line prompt in a variable loses its newlines and silently changes the task.
    29	if [[ -z "$PROMPT_FILE" ]]; then
    30	  PROMPT_FILE="$ROOT/out/reasoning-ab-prompts.txt"
    31	  cat > "$PROMPT_FILE" <<'EOF'
    32	=== mechanical ===
    33	Given the note below, output it verbatim with every occurrence of "The" replaced by "THE".
    34	Output only the modified note, with no commentary.
    35	---
    36	The model runs on a single GPU. The KV cache grows with the context. The prefill phase
    37	is compute bound. The decode phase is bandwidth bound. The measured ceiling is the limit.
    38	===
    39	=== reasoning ===
    40	A 12 GB GPU holds a model whose weights take 7.19 GiB, and the KV cache costs 64 KiB per
    41	token. Compute how many tokens of context fit in the remaining memory if compute buffers
    42	take 760 MiB and the card reports 11904 MiB usable. Show the arithmetic, then state the
    43	answer in one line.
    44	===
    45	EOF
    46	fi
    47	
    48	cat > "$ROOT/out/_reasoning_ask.py" <<'PY'
    49	import json, sys, time, urllib.request, urllib.error
    50	mode, prompt_path, port = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]
    51	prompt = open(prompt_path).read().strip()
    52	body = {"messages": [{"role": "user", "content": prompt}], "max_tokens": 400, "temperature": 0}
    53	if mode != "server-default":
    54	    body["reasoning_effort"] = mode
    55	req = urllib.request.Request(f"http://127.0.0.1:{port}/v1/chat/completions",
    56	                             data=json.dumps(body).encode(),
    57	                             headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    58	t0 = time.time()
    59	try:
    60	    d = json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=1800))
    61	except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
    62	    print(f"{'ERR':>8} {'-':>8} {'-':>6} {'-':>7} {'-':>8} {e.code:>11}")
    63	    raise SystemExit
    64	wall = time.time() - t0
    65	t = d["timings"]
    66	msg = d["choices"][0]["message"]
    67	think = len(msg.get("reasoning_content") or "")
    68	print(f"{t['predicted_per_second']:8.2f} {wall:8.1f} {t['predicted_n']:6d} "
    69	      f"{t.get('draft_n', 0):7d} {t.get('draft_n_accepted', 0):8d} {think:11d}")
    70	PY
    71	
    72	# Emit "name<tab>path" pairs, one file per prompt so newlines survive.
    73	python3 - "$PROMPT_FILE" "$ROOT/out" <<'PY' > "$ROOT/out/_reasoning_prompts.tsv"
    74	import re, sys, pathlib
    75	text, outdir = open(sys.argv[1]).read(), pathlib.Path(sys.argv[2])
    76	blocks = [b.strip() for b in re.split(r"^===.*===\s*$", text, flags=re.M) if b.strip()]
    77	names = re.findall(r"^===\s*(.+?)\s*===\s*$", text, flags=re.M)
    78	for i, body in enumerate(blocks):
    79	    name = names[i] if i < len(names) else f"prompt{i+1}"
    80	    path = outdir / f"_reasoning_prompt_{i}.txt"
    81	    path.write_text(body)
    82	    print(f"{name}\t{path}")
    83	PY
    84	
    85	if [[ ! -s "$ROOT/out/_reasoning_prompts.tsv" ]]; then
    86	  echo "no prompts parsed from $PROMPT_FILE (expected '=== name ===' delimited blocks)" >&2
    87	  exit 1
    88	fi
    89	
    90	{
    91	  echo "# reasoning-mode A/B against the served path"
    92	  echo "# date: $(date -Iseconds)   server: http://$HOST:$PORT"
    93	  echo "# gpu: $(nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,memory.used,clocks.max.sm --format=csv,noheader)"
    94	  echo "# loadavg: $(cat /proc/loadavg)"
    95	  echo "# prompt file: ${PROMPT_FILE#$ROOT/}"
    96	  echo
    97	  printf '%-12s %-15s %8s %8s %6s %7s %8s %11s\n' \
    98	         prompt mode gen_t/s wall_s tokens drafts accepted think_chars
    99	} | tee "$OUT"
   100	
   101	while IFS=$'\t' read -r name path; do
   102	  for mode in server-default none low medium; do
   103	    row="$(python3 "$ROOT/out/_reasoning_ask.py" "$mode" "$path" "$PORT")"
   104	    printf '%-12s %-15s %s\n' "$name" "$mode" "$row" | tee -a "$OUT"
   105	  done
   106	  echo | tee -a "$OUT"
   107	done < "$ROOT/out/_reasoning_prompts.tsv"
   108	
   109	cat | tee -a "$OUT" <<'EOF'
   110	# Reading this table:
   111	#   think_chars > 0 -> the model reasoned, drafts are 0, so speculation was inert
   112	#   drafts > 0      -> speculation fired; that is where the ~5x comes from
   113	#   server-default  -> whatever config/rt3060.env passes as the chat-template default
   114	#   none is the only mode measured to disable thinking; low and medium still think
   115	EOF
   116	
   117	echo
   118	echo "saved: $OUT"
FILE scripts/quality-check.py
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Quality check across reasoning modes, with machine-checkable answers.
     3	
     4	The reasoning-mode A/B (scripts/reasoning-ab.sh) measures speed only. Speed alone is not
     5	enough to decide a default: `reasoning_effort: "none"` was observed to cut a mechanical
     6	task from 334 generated tokens to 42, and nothing yet says whether that is concision or a
     7	broken answer. This script settles it by asking tasks whose answers can be scored without a
     8	judge.
     9	
    10	Three task families, each scored mechanically:
    11	
    12	  arithmetic   exact numeric answer, parsed and compared
    13	  format       an instruction with a checkable shape (line count, prefix, exclusions)
    14	  transform    a deterministic string/JSON transformation, compared to a computed key
    15	
    16	Usage:
    17	  scripts/serve.sh start
    18	  scripts/quality-check.py                 # all modes, all tasks
    19	  scripts/quality-check.py none medium     # selected modes
    20	"""
    21	import json
    22	import re
    23	import sys
    24	import time
    25	import urllib.error
    26	import urllib.request
    27	
    28	PORT = 8080
    29	URL = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}/v1/chat/completions"
    30	
    31	# ---------------------------------------------------------------- task definitions
    32	# Each returns (prompt, scorer). The scorer takes the assistant text and returns
    33	# (passed: bool, detail: str).
    34	
    35	
    36	def score_number(expected, extract=r"-?\d[\d,]*\.?\d*"):
    37	    """Compare the LAST number in the reply to the expected value.
    38	
    39	    The last number is the model's final answer; earlier ones may be intermediate steps.
    40	    Replies that bury the answer are caught here rather than silently passing.
    41	    """
    42	    def f(text):
    43	        nums = re.findall(extract, text.replace(",", ""))
    44	        if not nums:
    45	            return False, "no number found"
    46	        got = float(nums[-1])
    47	        return abs(got - expected) < 1e-6, f"expected {expected}, got {got}"
    48	    return f
    49	
    50	
    51	def score_exact_after(marker, expected):
    52	    def f(text):
    53	        idx = text.rfind(marker)
    54	        if idx < 0:
    55	            return False, f"marker {marker!r} not found"
    56	        tail = text[idx + len(marker):].strip().splitlines()
    57	        got = tail[0].strip() if tail else ""
    58	        return got == expected, f"expected {expected!r}, got {got!r}"
    59	    return f
    60	
    61	
    62	def score_shape(min_lines, forbid_words=(), require_lines=None):
    63	    def f(text):
    64	        body = text.strip()
    65	        lines = [l for l in body.splitlines() if l.strip()]
    66	        problems = []
    67	        if len(lines) < min_lines:
    68	            problems.append(f"{len(lines)} non-empty lines < {min_lines}")
    69	        if require_lines is not None and len(lines) != require_lines:
    70	            problems.append(f"{len(lines)} lines != {require_lines}")
    71	        for w in forbid_words:
    72	            if re.search(w, body, re.I):
    73	                problems.append(f"contains forbidden {w!r}")
    74	        return (not problems), ("ok" if not problems else "; ".join(problems))
    75	    return f
    76	
    77	
    78	def score_json_transform(expected_pairs):
    79	    def f(text):
    80	        m = re.search(r"\{.*\}", text, re.S)
    81	        if not m:
    82	            return False, "no JSON object found"
    83	        try:
    84	            got = json.loads(m.group(0))
    85	        except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
    86	            return False, f"invalid JSON: {e}"
    87	        problems = []
    88	        for k, v in expected_pairs.items():
    89	            if k not in got:
    90	                problems.append(f"missing key {k}")
    91	            elif str(got[k]).strip().lower() != str(v).strip().lower():
    92	                problems.append(f"{k}: expected {v}, got {got[k]}")
    93	        return (not problems), ("ok" if not problems else "; ".join(problems))
    94	    return f
    95	
    96	
    97	TASKS = [
    98	    # Arithmetic: the correct answer is a single number, so there is no judging.
    99	    # Verified by hand: weights 7.19 GiB = 7362.56 MiB; free = 11904 - 7362.56 - 760 =
   100	    # 3781.44 MiB; at 64 KiB (0.0625 MiB) per token that is 60503 tokens. Tolerance is
   101	    # generous because the model may round 7.19 GiB differently; scoring is absolute so
   102	    # 60503 and 60500 both pass while a wrong order of magnitude fails.
   103	    (
   104	        "arith_1",
   105	        "A GPU holds a 7.19 GiB model and a KV cache costing 64 KiB per token. "
   106	        "It reports 11904 MiB usable and compute buffers take 760 MiB. "
   107	        "How many tokens of context fit? Answer with the number alone.",
   108	        score_number(60503, r"\d[\d,]*"),
   109	    ),
   110	    (
   111	        "arith_2",
   112	        "Compute 847 * 29 + 1147. Reply with the final number only.",
   113	        score_number(847 * 29 + 1147),
   114	    ),
   115	    # 401*5120*2.13 bits = 4,373,146 bits = 546,643 bytes = 533.8 KiB. Scored in KiB so the
   116	    # answer is a meaningful integer rather than 0.00 in GiB.
   117	    (
   118	        "arith_3",
   119	        "A tensor has 401 rows of 5120 values, stored at 2.13 bits per value. "
   120	        "How many kibibytes is it? Round to the nearest whole number and reply with "
   121	        "the number only.",
   122	        score_number(534, r"\d[\d,]*"),
   123	    ),
   124	    # Format: the instruction has a checkable shape, so compliance is mechanical.
   125	    (
   126	        "format_1",
   127	        "List exactly 5 HTTP status codes, one per line, each line starting with the "
   128	        "three-digit code and a space, and nothing else. No explanations, no headers.",
   129	        score_shape(5, forbid_words=[r"^#"], require_lines=5),
   130	    ),
   131	    (
   132	        "format_2",
   133	        "Reply with exactly three lines. Line 1 is the word ALPHA, line 2 is BETA, "
   134	        "line 3 is GAMMA. Nothing else.",
   135	        score_shape(3, require_lines=3),
   136	    ),
   137	    # Transform: deterministic, comparable to a computed key.
   138	    (
   139	        "transform_1",
   140	        'Return only a JSON object with the keys "total", "mean" and "count" for the '
   141	        "numbers 4, 8, 15, 16, 23, 42. total and count are integers, mean is "
   142	        "rounded to two decimals.",
   143	        lambda t: score_json_transform(
   144	            {"total": 108, "mean": 18.0, "count": 6})(t),
   145	    ),
   146	]
   147	
   148	
   149	def ask(prompt, mode, max_tokens=1200):
   150	    body = {"messages": [{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
   151	            "max_tokens": max_tokens, "temperature": 0}
   152	    if mode != "server-default":
   153	        body["reasoning_effort"] = mode
   154	    req = urllib.request.Request(URL, data=json.dumps(body).encode(),
   155	                                headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
   156	    t0 = time.time()
   157	    d = json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=1800))
   158	    wall = time.time() - t0
   159	    msg = d["choices"][0]["message"]
   160	    return {
   161	        "text": (msg.get("content") or ""),
   162	        "think": len(msg.get("reasoning_content") or ""),
   163	        "wall": wall,
   164	        "tokens": d["timings"]["predicted_n"],
   165	        "drafts": d["timings"].get("draft_n", 0),
   166	    }
   167	
   168	
   169	def main():
   170	    modes = sys.argv[1:] or ["server-default", "none", "medium"]
   171	    rows = []
   172	    for name, prompt, scorer in TASKS:
   173	        for mode in modes:
   174	            try:
   175	                r = ask(prompt, mode)
   176	            except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
   177	                rows.append((name, mode, None, f"HTTP {e.code}", {}, ""))
   178	                continue
   179	            passed, detail = scorer(r["text"])
   180	            rows.append((name, mode, passed, detail, r, r["text"]))
   181	
   182	    print(f"{'task':12s} {'mode':15s} {'pass':5s} {'gen_t/s':>8s} {'wall_s':>7s} "
   183	          f"{'tok':>5s} {'drafts':>7s} {'think':>6s}  detail")
   184	    summary = {}
   185	    for name, mode, passed, detail, r, _ in rows:
   186	        if r:
   187	            gts = r["tokens"] / r["wall"] if r["wall"] else 0
   188	            print(f"{name:12s} {mode:15s} {('PASS' if passed else 'FAIL'):5s} "
   189	                  f"{gts:8.2f} {r['wall']:7.1f} {r['tokens']:5d} {r['drafts']:7d} "
   190	                  f"{r['think']:6d}  {detail[:52]}")
   191	        else:
   192	            print(f"{name:12s} {mode:15s} {'ERR':5s} {'-':>8} {'-':>7} {'-':>5} "
   193	                  f"{'-':>7} {'-':>6}  {detail}")
   194	        summary.setdefault(mode, []).append(1 if passed else 0)
   195	
   196	    print()
   197	    print("accuracy by mode:")
   198	    for mode, res in summary.items():
   199	        print(f"  {mode:15s} {sum(res)}/{len(res)} = {100*sum(res)/len(res):.0f}%")
   200	
   201	
   202	if __name__ == "__main__":
   203	    main()
FILE scripts/profile-ncu.sh
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Split one decode step into per-kernel GPU time, using Nsight Compute.
     3	#
     4	# The roofline in docs/roofline-rtx3060.md leaves roughly 5 ms of a ~29 ms decode
     5	# token unaccounted for. This script answers where it goes instead of guessing.
     6	#
     7	# Two phases, and the difference between them is itself a result:
     8	#   1. a normal run, for the real token time (llama-cli reports it)
     9	#   2. an ncu run, for the per-kernel GPU time
    10	# ncu serialises kernel launches, so durations in phase 2 are inflated by roughly
    11	# 100-1000x in wall terms and small-kernel overheads vanish. The sum of phase 2 is
    12	# therefore the GPU work only; real time minus that sum is launch/scheduling cost.
    13	#
    14	# Hardware counters need root on this machine (RmProfilingAdminOnly = 1), so run:
    15	#   sudo scripts/profile-ncu.sh
    16	set -euo pipefail
    17	
    18	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    19	BIN_DIR="${BIN_DIR:-$ROOT/bin/cuda}"
    20	MODEL="${MODEL:-$ROOT/models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-PQ2_0.gguf}"
    21	PACK_LABEL="$(basename "$MODEL" .gguf)"
    22	CUDA_ROOT="${CUDA_ROOT:-/usr/local/cuda-12.4}"
    23	NCU="${NCU:-$CUDA_ROOT/bin/ncu}"
    24	PROMPT="${PROMPT:-The capital of France is}"
    25	N_GEN="${N_GEN:-8}"
    26	
    27	[[ -x "$NCU" ]] || { echo "ncu not found at $NCU; install cuda-nsight-compute-12-4" >&2; exit 1; }
    28	[[ -f "$MODEL" ]] || { echo "model not found: $MODEL" >&2; exit 1; }
    29	
    30	STAMP="$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
    31	BASE="$ROOT/results/ncu-${PACK_LABEL}-${STAMP}"
    32	mkdir -p "$ROOT/results"
    33	
    34	run_cli() { # run_cli <logfile>
    35	  "$BIN_DIR/llama-cli" -m "$MODEL" -ngl 99 -fa on -st --no-warmup \
    36	    -n "$N_GEN" -p "$PROMPT" 2>&1 | tee "$1"
    37	}
    38	
    39	echo "### phase 1: real timing (no profiler)" >&2
    40	run_cli "$BASE.real.log" | grep -E "prompt eval time|eval time|total time" | tail -6 >&2
    41	
    42	echo >&2
    43	echo "### phase 2: per-kernel GPU time (ncu; wall time here is meaningless)" >&2
    44	"$NCU" \
    45	  --target-processes all \
    46	  --kernel-name-base demangled \
    47	  --metrics gpu__time_duration.sum,launch__grid_size,launch__block_size \
    48	  --csv --log-file "$BASE.kernels.csv" \
    49	  "$BIN_DIR/llama-cli" -m "$MODEL" -ngl 99 -fa on -st --no-warmup -n "$N_GEN" -p "$PROMPT" \
    50	  2>&1 | grep -E "ERROR|WARNING|Connected" | head -5 >&2
    51	
    52	python3 - "$BASE.kernels.csv" <<'PY' >&2 || echo "csv parse failed; see $BASE.kernels.csv" >&2
    53	import csv, sys, collections
    54	tot, cnt = collections.Counter(), collections.Counter()
    55	try:
    56	    fh = open(sys.argv[1], newline="")
    57	except OSError as e:
    58	    print(f"cannot open {e}"); raise SystemExit(1)
    59	with fh:
    60	    for row in csv.DictReader(fh):
    61	        name = row.get("Kernel Name") or row.get("Function Name") or "?"
    62	        raw = row.get("gpu__time_duration.sum", "")
    63	        try:
    64	            ns = float(raw.replace(",", "").split()[0])
    65	        except (ValueError, IndexError):
    66	            continue
    67	        tot[name] += ns; cnt[name] += 1
    68	if not tot:
    69	    print("no kernel rows; the profile probably failed (counters need root)")
    70	    raise SystemExit(1)
    71	s = sum(tot.values())
    72	print(f"\nprofiled GPU work: {s/1e6:.1f} ms across {sum(cnt.values())} launches")
    73	print(f"{'ms':>9} {'%':>6} {'launches':>9}  kernel")
    74	for k, v in tot.most_common(30):
    75	    print(f"{v/1e6:9.2f} {100*v/s:6.2f} {cnt[k]:9d}  {k[:92]}")
    76	PY
    77	
    78	echo >&2
    79	echo "artifacts: $BASE.real.log  $BASE.kernels.csv" >&2
FILE scripts/profile-nsys.sh
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Per-kernel time breakdown of one decode step, using Nsight Systems.
     3	#
     4	# This is the root-free alternative to scripts/profile-ncu.sh. ncu needs the
     5	# hardware-counter permission (RmProfilingAdminOnly = 1 on this machine, so root),
     6	# but nsys reads CUPTI activity records instead, which an ordinary user may do.
     7	# Kernel durations are all we need to find where the unaccounted decode time goes.
     8	#
     9	# Install once (the only step that needs root):
    10	#   sudo apt-get install -y cuda-nsight-systems-12-4
    11	#
    12	# Then, as a normal user:
    13	#   scripts/profile-nsys.sh
    14	#
    15	# N_GEN defaults to 6: enough to cover the prefill plus several decode steps. The
    16	# report is per-kernel totals, so a few tokens are plenty.
    17	set -euo pipefail
    18	
    19	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    20	BIN_DIR="${BIN_DIR:-$ROOT/bin/cuda}"
    21	MODEL="${MODEL:-$ROOT/models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-PQ2_0.gguf}"
    22	PACK_LABEL="$(basename "$MODEL" .gguf)"
    23	PROMPT="${PROMPT:-The capital of France is}"
    24	N_GEN="${N_GEN:-6}"
    25	
    26	NSYS="${NSYS:-}"
    27	if [[ -z "$NSYS" ]]; then
    28	  for c in nsys /usr/local/cuda-12.4/bin/nsys /opt/nvidia/nsight-systems/*/bin/nsys; do
    29	    [[ -x "$c" ]] && { NSYS="$c"; break; }
    30	  done
    31	fi
    32	[[ -n "$NSYS" ]] || {
    33	  echo "nsys not found. Install it once with:" >&2
    34	  echo "  sudo apt-get install -y cuda-nsight-systems-12-4" >&2
    35	  exit 1
    36	}
    37	[[ -f "$MODEL" ]] || { echo "model not found: $MODEL" >&2; exit 1; }
    38	
    39	STAMP="$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
    40	BASE="$ROOT/results/nsys-${PACK_LABEL}-${STAMP}"
    41	mkdir -p "$ROOT/results"
    42	
    43	echo "### phase 1: real timing (no profiler)" >&2
    44	"$BIN_DIR/llama-cli" -m "$MODEL" -ngl 99 -fa on -st --no-warmup -n "$N_GEN" -p "$PROMPT" \
    45	  < /dev/null 2>&1 | grep -E "prompt eval time|eval time|total time" | tail -4 >&2
    46	
    47	echo >&2
    48	echo "### phase 2: nsys kernel trace" >&2
    49	# --sample=none and --cpuctxsw=none keep the report to CUDA activity only.
    50	"$NSYS" profile \
    51	  --trace=cuda \
    52	  --sample=none --cpuctxsw=none \
    53	  --force-overwrite=true \
    54	  -o "$BASE" \
    55	  "$BIN_DIR/llama-cli" -m "$MODEL" -ngl 99 -fa on -st --no-warmup -n "$N_GEN" -p "$PROMPT" \
    56	  < /dev/null > "$BASE.run.log" 2>&1 || true
    57	
    58	echo "### stats" >&2
    59	"$NSYS" stats --report cuda_gpu_kern_sum --format csv --force-export=true \
    60	  "$BASE.nsys-rep" > "$BASE.kernels.csv" 2>/dev/null || {
    61	    echo "nsys stats failed; inspect $BASE.nsys-rep" >&2; exit 1; }
    62	
    63	python3 - "$BASE.kernels.csv" <<'PY' >&2 || echo "csv parse failed: $BASE.kernels.csv" >&2
    64	import csv, sys, collections
    65	tot, cnt = collections.Counter(), collections.Counter()
    66	with open(sys.argv[1], newline="") as fh:
    67	    for row in csv.reader(fh):
    68	        if len(row) < 2:
    69	            continue
    70	        name = row[0].strip().strip('"')
    71	        # Columns vary by nsys version; find the first parseable numeric field that
    72	        # looks like a total time in ns.
    73	        vals = []
    74	        for cell in row[1:]:
    75	            try:
    76	                vals.append(float(cell.replace(",", "").strip().strip('"')))
    77	            except ValueError:
    78	                vals.append(None)
    79	        nums = [v for v in vals if v is not None]
    80	        if not nums:
    81	            continue
    82	        ns_total = max(nums)   # the widest time column is the total
    83	        tot[name] += ns_total
    84	        cnt[name] += 1
    85	if not tot:
    86	    print("no kernel rows parsed; open the csv")
    87	    raise SystemExit(1)
    88	s = sum(tot.values())
    89	print(f"\nGPU kernel time: {s/1e6:.1f} ms across {sum(cnt.values())} launches "
    90	      f"(under nsys, so wall time is not meaningful)")
    91	print(f"{'ms':>9} {'%':>6} {'launches':>9}  kernel")
    92	for k, v in tot.most_common(30):
    93	    nm = k if len(k) < 96 else k[:93] + "..."
    94	    print(f"{v/1e6:9.2f} {100*v/s:6.2f} {cnt[k]:9d}  {nm}")
    95	PY
    96	
    97	echo >&2
    98	echo "artifacts: $BASE.nsys-rep  $BASE.kernels.csv  $BASE.run.log" >&2

exec

exec
/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba docs/gemv-ncu-analysis.md | sed -n '1,270p'" in /home/penta/repos/eobonsai
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# Decode GEMV: what ncu adds beyond nsys
     2	
     3	`docs/decode-profile.md` established the *shape* of the decode budget with nsys: 82.6% in
     4	`mul_mat_vec_q`, and the remainder fragmented. nsys reports durations, which was enough to
     5	rank targets but not to explain them. With hardware counters now available (the machine was
     6	rebooted with `NVreg_RestrictProfilingToAdminUsers=0`, so `ncu` no longer needs root), each
     7	GEMV shape can be asked *why* it is slow.
     8	
     9	## Method
    10	
    11	```
    12	ncu --target-processes all --kernel-name regex:mul_mat_vec_q --launch-count 40 --launch-skip 700 \
    13	  --metrics gpu__time_duration.sum,dram__bytes.sum,dram__throughput...,lts__t_sector_hit_rate.pct,\
    14	smsp__average_warps_issue_stalled_long_scoreboard_per_issue_active.ratio,launch__registers_per_thread \
    15	  bin/cuda/llama-cli -m <PQ2_0> -ngl 99 -fa on -st --no-warmup -n 3 -p Hello --spec-type none
    16	```
    17	
    18	Raw output: `results/ncu-gemv-shapes.csv`.
    19	
    20	## Result: the GEMV is not one thing
    21	
    22	40 launches, grouped by grid size, which is the row count of the weight being read:
    23	
    24	| grid (rows) | launches | us/launch | MB/launch | **GB/s** | L2 hit | long_scoreboard |
    25	|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
    26	| 2560 | 13 | **190.8** | 18.35 | **96.2** | **61.2%** | **2.81** |
    27	| 8704 | 12 | 201.1 | 25.00 | 124.3 | 25.8% | 0.28 |
    28	| 6144 | 1 | 144.7 | 18.32 | 126.6 | 26.5% | 0.31 |
    29	| 5120 | 6 | 121.5 | 15.08 | 124.1 | 26.1% | 0.31 |
    30	| 3072 | 6 | 76.1 | 9.49 | 124.7 | 28.1% | 0.36 |
    31	| 512 | 2 | 18.1 | 2.02 | 111.6 | 38.1% | 0.91 |
    32	
    33	Three findings, none of which nsys timing alone could produce:
    34	
    35	**1. No individual GEMV comes close to the bandwidth ceiling.** Every shape sits at
    36	**96-127 GB/s, i.e. 32-42% of the measured 300 GB/s**. The global 84.3% figure is an
    37	emergent property of many asymmetric kernels overlapping: while one kernel stalls on DRAM,
    38	others issue. This means the per-kernel roofline is not the right model and "get the GEMV to
    39	300 GB/s" was never a well-posed target.
    40	
    41	**2. There are two distinct regimes, and one of them is latency-bound.** At `grid=2560` the
    42	kernel hits 96 GB/s with a 61% L2 hit rate and a `long_scoreboard` stall ratio of **2.81**
    43	(nearly 10x the 0.28-0.36 of every other shape). `long_scoreboard` is a wait on a global
    44	memory dependency, so this kernel is waiting on DRAM latency rather than saturating
    45	bandwidth. The other shapes sit at 26% L2 and ~0.3, which is the healthy bandwidth-limited
    46	profile. A launch-configuration change cannot fix a latency-bound kernel; it needs either
    47	more bytes in flight per thread (wider loads, more independent loads in flight) or the
    48	working set to fit better.
    49	
    50	**3. `grid=2560` is a split of a larger matrix, and it is 40% of the GEMV time.** The model
    51	has no 2560-row weight: `ffn_down` is 5120 rows, `attn_qkv` is 10240, `ffn_gate/up` is 17408,
    52	`attn_gate`/`ssm_out`/attention projections are 6144. A grid of exactly half suggests the
    53	matmul is being split, and the split half is the slowest kernel per launch after 8704 while
    54	carrying a pathological L2 and stall profile. 2560-row launches accumulate **2502 us of the
    55	6259 us total (40%)**.
    56	
    57	Note also `launch__registers_per_thread = 89` for every shape, which caps occupancy; worth
    58	noting but not yet shown to be the cause.
    59	
    60	## What this changes
    61	
    62	The earlier framing was "the GEMV is at 84.3% of the roofline, so at most 15.7% is
    63	recoverable". That is now known to be the wrong question:
    64	
    65	- The *aggregate* 84.3% is not a per-kernel efficiency that can be raised; it is what
    66	  overlapping kernels produce.
    67	- The actionable target is the **latency-bound 2560-row launches**, 40% of GEMV time, where
    68	  the kernel is idle waiting on memory rather than short of bandwidth.
    69	- Everything else is at 32-42% of peak per kernel and is bandwidth-sharing behaviour, not a
    70	  fixable defect.
    71	
    72	## Honest status
    73	
    74	This is a diagnosis, not yet a speedup. No kernel change has been made on the basis of it.
    75	The remaining decode-side work is genuinely kernel engineering now: increase memory-level
    76	parallelism in the latency-bound shape (wider per-thread loads, several independent loads in
    77	flight, or restructuring to avoid the split), which needs in-model A/B as ADR 0004 requires,
    78	because the standalone microbench predicted +20% and delivered +2% last time.
    79	
    80	Given that decode is already 4.9-6.0x faster under speculation (ADR 0003), the expected
    81	value here is modest: at most ~13% of the non-speculative GEMV time, which is ~5% of a
    82	speculated token.

exec
/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba docs/gemv-split-rootcause.md | sed -n '1,330p'" in /home/penta/repos/eobonsai
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# Decode GEMV: root cause of the split, and what it means for optimization
     2	
     3	Follow-up to `docs/gemv-ncu-analysis.md`, which found that one GEMV shape profile
     4	(`grid=2560`) was latency-bound, carried 61% L2 hit rate, and accounted for ~40% of GEMV
     5	time. This document identifies why, and corrects a wrong guess made along the way.
     6	
     7	## Wrong guess first, for the record
     8	
     9	The initial reading was "grid=2560 is the *split half* of a 5120-row matrix", inferred from
    10	the microbenchmark's own 2.16 GB of weight traffic divided by its 200,000 rows. That was
    11	arithmetic on the wrong data: the microbench's `bytes/row` is a constant it chose, not a
    12	property of the model.
    13	
    14	A second guess blamed a row-split code path. `ggml_cuda_op_mul_mat_vec_q`
    15	(`mmvq.cu:1479`) takes `row_low`/`row_high` and would do exactly this split, but a
    16	whole-tree search shows it is **dead code**: defined and declared, never called. The live
    17	path passes `ne01` (the full row count) at `mmvq.cu:1474`. So the split had to come from
    18	elsewhere.
    19	
    20	## Root cause
    21	
    22	The instantiations ncu captured are all
    23	`mul_mat_vec_q<(ggml_type)142, (int)4, ...>` - that is `type = PQ2_0`, **`ncols_dst = 4`**.
    24	
    25	`ncols_dst` is the number of destination columns the kernel processes at once, and 4 is
    26	`MMVQ_MAX_BATCH_SIZE`, the cap. With `ncols_dst = 4`, `calc_rows_per_block` returns **2**
    27	(`mmvq.cu:543-549`), so the grid becomes `nrows_x / 2`:
    28	
    29	| ncu grid observed | x2 | matching weight (from the GGUF) | count |
    30	|---:|---:|---|---:|
    31	| 512 | 1024 | `attn_k` / `attn_v` | 32 |
    32	| 2560 | 5120 | `ffn_down` / `ssm_out` | 128 |
    33	| 3072 | 6144 | `attn_gate` / attention projections | 48 |
    34	| 5120 | 10240 | `attn_qkv` | 48 |
    35	| 6144 | 12288 | `attn_q` (full-attention layers) | 16 |
    36	| 8704 | 17408 | `ffn_gate` / `ffn_up` | 128 |
    37	
    38	Enumerated directly from the GGUF, the ternary weights have row counts of
    39	1024 / 5120 / 6144 / 10240 / 12288 / 17408 / 248320 and **no 2560-row weight exists**. Every
    40	observed grid is exactly half a real weight's row count, and every one of the six matches a
    41	real weight. The mapping is therefore exact, not circumstantial.
    42	
    43	So decode is running the multi-column MMVQ path with the maximum batch of 4, even though a
    44	single user generates one token at a time.
    45	
    46	## Why this matters, and why it is not obviously a bug
    47	
    48	This is not simply "a split is bad". There is a real trade:
    49	
    50	- **Cost**: `ncols_dst = 4` uses 2 rows per block and a different reduction path, and the
    51	  grid halves. The measured 2560-row shape is the worst offender: 96 GB/s against 124 GB/s
    52	  for the same family, with a 61% L2 hit rate and `long_scoreboard` 2.81 against 0.28-0.36.
    53	- **Benefit**: processing 4 columns per pass means the *weights are read once for four
    54	  columns*. For a bandwidth-bound GEMV that is the whole point, and it is the same mechanism
    55	  that makes speculative decoding pay off.
    56	
    57	Which effect wins is an empirical question, and `llama-bench -p 0 -n 128` presumably already
    58	has 4-token batches available to it (the benchmark generates tokens in a batch), so the
    59	choice may be correct for the benchmark and wrong for single-token streaming. That
    60	distinction has **not** been measured yet and is the next experiment, not a conclusion.
    61	
    62	## What this does not change
    63	
    64	The corrected picture from the ncu analysis stands: per-kernel bandwidth is 96-127 GB/s
    65	against a 300 GB/s ceiling, i.e. every shape is far from its own roofline, and the aggregate
    66	84.3% is emergent from overlapping kernels rather than a per-kernel efficiency. What changed
    67	is the explanation of *why* one shape is worse: it is a configuration choice
    68	(`ncols_dst = 4`), not a mysterious split.
    69	
    70	## Next experiment, stated before running it
    71	
    72	Force the single-column path for PQ2_0 decode and A/B it in-model, as ADR 0004 requires.
    73	If `ncols_dst = 1` is faster for streaming, the change is a one-line configuration decision;
    74	if it is slower, then the 4-column path is correctly chosen and the latency-bound shape is
    75	simply the price of reading the weights once for four columns, which is a good trade.
    76	Either result is informative and both are cheap to obtain.
    77	
    78	The standalone-microbench trap applies in full here: the microbench measured a *different*
    79	config sweep (`nwarps`) on a simplified loop and overstated the gain 10x. Only the in-model
    80	A/B counts.
