     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Baseline / A-B benchmark for Bonsai 2 27B on the RTX 3060.
     3	#
     4	# PP (prompt processing) and TG (token generation) are run as separate phases:
     5	# llama-bench crosses -p with -d, and a pp8192 x depth-16384 cell costs a lot of
     6	# KV for no information. The phases stress different limits anyway:
     7	#   PP -> dp4a throughput (measured ceiling: ~25 TOPS, see scripts/roofline.sh)
     8	#   TG -> memory bandwidth (measured read ceiling: ~307 GB/s) plus KV traffic at depth
     9	#
    10	# Launch overhead on this card is 1.4-2.9 us per launch and moves with CPU load,
    11	# so run this on a quiet machine and check the load average printed in the header.
    12	#
    13	# Usage:
    14	#   scripts/bench.sh <bin-dir> PQ2_0 [extra llama-bench flags...]
    15	#   KV_TYPE=q4_0 scripts/bench.sh build/llama-sm86/bin PQ2_0
    16	set -euo pipefail
    17	
    18	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    19	BIN_DIR="${1:?usage: bench.sh <bin-dir> <PQ2_0|PTQ1_0> [flags...]}"
    20	PACK="${2:?usage: bench.sh <bin-dir> <PQ2_0|PTQ1_0> [flags...]}"
    21	shift 2
    22	
    23	MODEL="$ROOT/models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-${PACK}.gguf"
    24	BENCH="$ROOT/$BIN_DIR/llama-bench"
    25	TAG="$(echo "$BIN_DIR" | tr '/' '_')"
    26	KV_TYPE="${KV_TYPE:-f16}"
    27	# K and V can be quantized independently; V quantization is the more disruptive of
    28	# the two, so KV_TYPE_K=q4_0 KV_TYPE_V=f16 is worth measuring on its own.
    29	KV_TYPE_K="${KV_TYPE_K:-$KV_TYPE}"
    30	KV_TYPE_V="${KV_TYPE_V:-$KV_TYPE}"
    31	REPS="${REPS:-5}"
    32	
    33	[[ -x "$BENCH" ]] || { echo "llama-bench not found: $BENCH" >&2; exit 1; }
    34	[[ -f "$MODEL" ]] || { echo "model not found: $MODEL" >&2; exit 1; }
    35	
    36	KV_ARGS=()
    37	if [[ "$KV_TYPE_K" != "f16" || "$KV_TYPE_V" != "f16" ]]; then
    38	  KV_ARGS=(-ctk "$KV_TYPE_K" -ctv "$KV_TYPE_V")
    39	fi
    40	
    41	OUT="$ROOT/results/bench-${TAG##*_}-${PACK}-k${KV_TYPE_K}v${KV_TYPE_V}-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S).log"
    42	mkdir -p "$ROOT/results"
    43	
    44	{
    45	  echo "### provenance"
    46	  echo "binary_dir : $BIN_DIR"
    47	  echo "model      : $(basename "$MODEL")  ($(stat -c %s "$MODEL") bytes)"
    48	  echo "fork_commit: $(git -C "$ROOT/third_party/llama.cpp" rev-parse HEAD 2>/dev/null || echo n/a)"
    49	  echo "kv_type    : k=$KV_TYPE_K v=$KV_TYPE_V    reps: $REPS"
    50	  echo "date       : $(date -Iseconds)"
    51	  echo "loadavg    : $(cat /proc/loadavg)   <- must be idle for a fair comparison"
    52	  echo "### gpu"
    53	  nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,memory.used,memory.total,clocks.sm,clocks.mem,temperature.gpu,power.draw,power.limit \
    54	             --format=csv 2>&1
    55	} | tee "$OUT"
    56	
    57	run_phase() {
    58	  local label="$1"; shift
    59	  echo "" | tee -a "$OUT"
    60	  echo "### $label" | tee -a "$OUT"
    61	  echo "\$ llama-bench -m <model> -ngl 99 -fa on ${KV_ARGS[*]} -r $REPS $*" | tee -a "$OUT"
    62	  # shellcheck disable=SC2086
    63	  "$BENCH" -m "$MODEL" -ngl 99 -fa on "${KV_ARGS[@]}" -r "$REPS" "$@" 2>&1 | tee -a "$OUT"
    64	}
    65	
    66	# PP: how fast a fresh prompt is ingested (dp4a-bound).
    67	# TG: how fast tokens come out, measured with a live KV cache.
    68	# CLI: the served shape. llama-bench batches tokens, so it runs MMVQ with ncols_dst=4
    69	#      (rows_per_block=2, grid halved, ~117 GB/s); a server or CLI generating one token
    70	#      at a time runs ncols_dst=1 (grid = true row count, ~229 GB/s). The two shapes are
    71	#      2x apart, so llama-bench tg is a lower bound on served throughput, not a proxy.
    72	#      See docs/gemv-benchmark-artifact.md.
    73	# Select with PHASES=pp, PHASES=tg, PHASES=cli, or a comma list; default runs pp,tg.
    74	PHASES="${PHASES:-pp,tg}"
    75	[[ "$PHASES" == *pp* ]] && run_phase "PP (prompt processing)" -p "${PP_LIST:-128,512,2048,8192}" -n 0 -d 0 "$@"
    76	[[ "$PHASES" == *tg* ]] && run_phase "TG (token generation)" -p 0 -n 128 -d "${TG_DEPTHS:-0,8192,32768}" "$@"
    77	
    78	if [[ "$PHASES" == *cli* ]]; then
    79	  CLI_BIN="${BIN_DIR%/bin}/bin/llama-cli"
    80	  [[ -x "$CLI_BIN" ]] || CLI_BIN="$ROOT/$BIN_DIR/llama-cli"
    81	  echo "" | tee -a "$OUT"
    82	  echo "### CLI (served shape: one token at a time, ncols_dst=1)" | tee -a "$OUT"
    83	  for i in 1 2 3; do
    84	    echo "\$ llama-cli -n ${N:-200} --temp 0 --spec-type none -p <fixed prompt>   (run $i)" | tee -a "$OUT"
    85	    "$CLI_BIN" -m "$MODEL" -ngl 99 -fa on -st --no-warmup -n "${N:-200}" --temp 0 \
    86	      -p "${SERVED_PROMPT:-Count from 1 to 40 slowly.}" --spec-type "${SPEC_TYPE:-none}" -rea off \
    87	      < /dev/null 2>&1 | grep -E "Generation:|Prompt:" | tee -a "$OUT"
    88	  done
    89	fi
    90	
    91	echo
    92	echo "saved: $OUT"

     1	# Launch profile for Bonsai 2 27B on the RTX 3060 12 GB, single user.
     2	#
     3	# Every value uses the ": ${VAR:=default}" form, so anything already set in the
     4	# environment wins. A single run can therefore override any setting:
     5	#
     6	#   CTX=65536 scripts/serve.sh
     7	#
     8	# Values marked (measured) come from a file under results/.
     9	#
    10	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    11	# model
    12	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    13	# PQ2_0 is settled by measurement, not by the model card: on this card PTQ1_0's
    14	# prefill is 4% faster but its decode is 31% slower (22.51 vs 29.55 t/s), because
    15	# dense-trit unpacking leaves it at 45% of the achievable bandwidth against 71%.
    16	# See docs/decisions/0001-packing-choice.md.
    17	: "${PACK:=PQ2_0}"
    18	
    19	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    20	# execution
    21	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    22	: "${NGL:=99}"            # offload every layer; the model is 7.19 GiB in PQ2_0
    23	: "${FA:=on}"             # required for the model's attention path
    24	: "${THREADS:=8}"
    25	# -b/-ub were swept 512..4096 and everything landed within 1.5% (pp512 487-494),
    26	# so the defaults stand. Prefill is at the dp4a ceiling, not batch-shape limited.
    27	: "${UB:=512}"
    28	: "${BATCH:=2048}"
    29	
    30	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    31	# context and KV
    32	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    33	# Depth costs bandwidth: 16 full-attention layers x 4 KV heads x 256 x 2 x 2 B =
    34	# 64 KiB/token, measured to within 8% of prediction. Decode: 29.55 t/s at depth 0,
    35	# 28.04 at 8K, 24.09 at 32K.
    36	#
    37	# Measured 12 GB budget (llama.cpp sees 11904 MiB with the display attached):
    38	#   weights  6872 MiB (fixed)
    39	#   buffers   736 MiB at ctx 32768, 764 MiB at 65536
    40	#   KV        64 MiB per 1024 tokens (f16)
    41	# Observed totals: 9656 MiB at ctx 32768, 11732 MiB at ctx 65536, both of which
    42	# load. That leaves room for about 74K tokens at f16.
    43	#
    44	# 32K is the working default. 64K is verified to fit. Above that, measure first.
    45	: "${CTX:=32768}"
    46	# f16 measured fastest at every depth; quantized KV is slower, not faster, on this
    47	# backend (19.40 vs 24.09 t/s at 32K). See docs/decisions/0002-kv-cache-precision.md.
    48	: "${KV_TYPE:=f16}"
    49	
    50	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    51	# serving
    52	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    53	: "${HOST:=127.0.0.1}"    # loopback only; a non-loopback bind exposes an unauthenticated API
    54	: "${PORT:=8080}"
    55	: "${NP:=1}"              # one slot: a 12 GB card cannot hold several long contexts at once
    56	
    57	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    58	# vision
    59	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    60	# The 0.63 GB projector is optional. Keeping it in system RAM frees VRAM for KV at
    61	# the cost of a slower image encode.
    62	: "${MMPROJ:=0}"
    63	: "${MMPROJ_CPU:=0}"
    64	
    65	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    66	# speculative decoding
    67	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    68	# Draft-model-free n-gram speculation. It is a large win when the output reuses the
    69	# context and completely inert when it does not, with no regression either way:
    70	#   code edit, thinking off   : 28.56 -> 139.88 t/s (CLI, 4.90x, 100% acceptance)
    71	#   verbatim copy, thinking off: 28.58 -> 171.56 t/s (CLI, 6.00x)
    72	#   code edit, thinking ON    : 28.45 -> 28.43 t/s (no drafts at all)
    73	#   plain chat, no overlap    : 28.57 -> 28.49 t/s (no drafts at all)
    74	# Serving path, same code-edit prompt: 28.46 -> 146.02 t/s with 85% acceptance.
    75	#
    76	# THE TRAP: thinking must be OFF or nothing fires. The thinking trace does not quote
    77	# the context, so there is nothing for the matcher to draft from. And the way to turn
    78	# it off is reasoning_effort=none per request:
    79	#   - thinking_budget_tokens: 0 does NOT disable it (measured: still 970 chars of
    80	#     reasoning, zero drafts)
    81	#   - reasoning_effort "low" still thinks (25% acceptance, no net gain)
    82	#   - reasoning_effort "minimal" is rejected with HTTP 500
    83	# Turn it on per request, not server-wide: it is a quality trade.
    84	# See docs/decisions/0003-ngram-speculation.md.
    85	: "${SPEC_TYPE:=ngram-simple}"
    86	
    87	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    88	# generation defaults
    89	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    90	# This is a thinking model and xhigh is the default, which is minutes of latency at
    91	# ~29 tok/s. medium is documented as about as accurate at moderate output limits.
    92	#
    93	# The server default stays on thinking on purpose. Agentic or mechanical work (reading
    94	# and rewriting files) should send reasoning_effort:"none" in the request body, which
    95	# keeps quality where it matters and takes decode from 28 to ~146 t/s where it does not.
    96	: "${REASONING_EFFORT:=medium}"
    97	: "${REASONING_BUDGET:=2048}"

Reproduce that section word for word, including the delimiters, and output nothing else.\n\n# RTX 3060 roofline (measured)\n\nAll numbers below were measured on this machine, not taken from spec sheets or\ncommunity tables. They are the denominators used to judge whether a model-level\nresult is good or bad, and to decide where optimization effort can pay off.\n\n- GPU: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3060 12 GB (GA106, sm_86, 28 SMs, 1837 MHz sustained)\n- Driver 595.91.07, CUDA 12.4, `third_party/llama.cpp` at `adfffbe`\n- Harness: `tools/microbench/roofline.cu`, run via `scripts/roofline.sh`\n- Raw output: `results/roofline-*.txt`\n\n## Measured ceilings\n\n| Quantity | Measured | Reference | Note |\n|---|---:|---|---|\n| Streaming read bandwidth | **298 - 307 GB/s** | 360 GB/s (192-bit GDDR6 spec) | 83-85% of spec; the practical ceiling |\n| Empty kernel launch | **1.6 - 1.8 us** | - | rises to 2.9 us under CPU load (parallel build) |\n| Null-work launch (28x256) | **1.6 us** | - | launch cost is grid-size independent at this size |\n| dp4a (int8 4-way dot) | **26.0 TOPS(int8)** = 13.0e12 MAC/s | - | 8 independent chains |\n| FP32 FMA | **11.7 TFLOPS** = 5.84e12 FMA/s | 12.8 TFLOPS (28 SM x 128 lane x 2 x 1.837 GHz) | 91% of issue-limited peak |\n| FP16x2 FMA (`__hfma2`) | **13.1 TFLOPS** | - | only 1.12x FP32 |\n| FWHT N=1024, decode (5 blocks) | **2.07 us/call** | - | the model's rotation block size |\n| FWHT N=1024, decode (17 blocks) | **2.09 us/call** | - | launch-latency bound, not bandwidth |\n| FWHT N=1024, pp512 (2560 blocks) | **66 us** (318 GB/s) | 298-307 GB/s | at the bandwidth ceiling |\n| FWHT N=1024, pp512 (8704 blocks) | **240 us** (297 GB/s) | 298-307 GB/s | at the bandwidth ceiling |\n\n### The dp4a result that matters\n\ndp4a delivers **13.0e12 MAC/s against FP32's 5.84e12 FMA/s, i.e. 2.2x, not 4x.**\nGA106 has 128 FP32 lanes but only 64 INT32 lanes per SM, so the int8 advantage on\nthis card is roughly half of what a datacenter part gives. Any plan that assumes a\n4x int8 multiplier over-counts the prefill ceiling by about 2x.\n\n## Derived floors for Bonsai 2 27B\n\nModel facts taken from the GGUF header (`tools/gguf-inspect.py`): 64 blocks,\nembedding 5120, FFN 17408, 24 heads / 4 KV heads, key = value = 256,\n`full_attention_interval = 4` (16 full-attention + 48 linear-attention layers),\nHadamard block 1024, 401 rotated weight tensors.\n\n### Decode (bandwidth-bound)\n\nWeights are streamed once per token.\n\n| Pack | Weight bytes | Floor at 300 GB/s | Ceiling | Practical (82-85% eff.) |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| PTQ1_0 | 5.95 GB | 19.8 ms | 50 t/s | 40 - 43 t/s |\n| PQ2_0 | 7.21 GB | 24.0 ms | 42 t/s | 34 - 36 t/s |\n\n#### Confirmed by measurement\n\n`scripts/bench.sh bin/cuda PQ2_0` with `PHASES=tg`\n(`results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164528.log`):\n\n| Test | Measured t/s | ms/token | Efficiency vs the 24.0 ms floor |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| tg128 | 29.55 ± 0.13 | 33.8 | 71% |\n| tg128 @ d8192 | 28.04 ± 0.07 | 35.7 | 67% |\n| tg128 @ d32768 | 24.09 ± 0.07 | 41.5 | 58% |\n\n**The KV traffic model is confirmed.** The depth deltas against the 64 KiB/token figure:\n\n| Depth | Predicted KV read | Predicted added time | Measured delta | Error |\n|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| 8192 | 512 MiB | +1.79 ms | +1.9 ms | +6% |\n| 32768 | 2.0 GiB | +7.16 ms | +7.7 ms | +8% |\n\nAt 32K depth, KV is **19% of the decode token**; the ADR 0002 prediction that a 4-bit\ncache becomes worthwhile above 8-32K depth rests on a model that has now been checked\nagainst this card.\n\n#### The remaining decode gap\n\nAt depth 0, decode takes 33.8 ms against a 24.0 ms streaming floor, so **9.8 ms is\nneither weight streaming nor KV**. The RTX 3070 (same architecture, 448 GB/s) measured\n22.9 ms for the same model, which scales to 29 ms at this card's bandwidth: the 3060 is\nabout 5 ms worse than bandwidth scaling predicts, consistent with its 28 SMs and 3 MB L2\nhurting the non-matmul parts of the step.\n\nThat 9.8 ms is the real optimization target, and the candidate list is in the section\nbelow. `scripts/profile-ncu.sh` exists to identify it per kernel rather than by\nelimination.\n\n### Prefill (dp4a-bound)\n\nPP512 needs 2 x 26.9e9 x 512 = 27.5 TFLOP = 13.75e12 int8 MACs.\n\n```\n13.75e12 MAC / 13.0e12 MAC/s = 1.06 s  ->  483 t/s ceiling\n```\n\nCross-check: the RTX 3070 (46 SMs, same sm_86) measured PP512 = 838.9 t/s in the\ncommunity table. Scaled to 28 SMs that is 510 t/s. Two independent routes agree, so\n**PQ2_0 prefill is already at the dp4a roofline** and a tile-tuning effort can only\nrecover the residual 5-15%, not a multiple.\n\n#### Confirmed by measurement\n\n`scripts/bench.sh bin/cuda PQ2_0` with `PHASES=pp`\n(`results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164343.log`), load average 4.7:\n\n| Test | Measured t/s | Roofline prediction |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| pp128 | 460.1 ± 18.7 | - |\n| pp512 | 493.8 ± 2.5 | 483 (dp4a) / 510 (3070-scaled) |\n| pp2048 | 491.3 ± 0.5 | - |\n| pp8192 | 474.9 ± 0.2 | - |\n\nThe measurement lands between the two predictions, within 2% of the mean. Throughput is\nflat from 512 to 8192 tokens, so prefill is throughput-limited rather than\noverhead-limited, exactly as the dp4a model says.\n\n**Consequence: PQ2_0 prefill tuning is a dead end.** The only way to prefill materially\nfaster on this card is to stop doing int8 `dp4a`, for example by feeding the ternary\nweights to tensor cores, which needs a different weight packing and is a\nre-architecture rather than a tuning exercise.\n\nPTQ1_0 is a different story: on the RTX 3090 Ti the community measured PP512 = 805 for\nPTQ1_0 against 1552 for PQ2_0. Both packs perform the same number of int8 MACs, so\nPTQ1_0's prefill cannot be dp4a-bound; its dense-trit unpacking is instruction-issue\nbound and roughly doubles the cost. See ADR 0001.\n\n### FWHT (the Hadamard rotation) is not a bottleneck\n\nThe rotation is dispatched from inside `mul_mat` when the node carries\n`GGML_HINT_SRC0_IS_HADAMARD` (`ggml-cuda.cu:1822`), and `hadamard_memo` is keyed on\n`(activation, rot)` (`llama-graph.cpp:1556`), so weights that share an activation and\nrotation share one transform. The pass count is on the order of 257 per forward step.\n\n| Phase | Cost | Share |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| decode | 257 x ~2.1 us = **0.54 ms** | ~1.9% of a 29 ms token |\n| pp512 | ~0.5 ms | ~0.08% of a 0.6-1.1 s prefill |\n\nFWHT is at the bandwidth ceiling where it is bandwidth-bound, and launch-latency bound\nat decode. It is not where the remaining time is.\n\n## What this rules out\n\n- **Rewriting the decode GEMV inner loop.** `vec_dot_pq2_0_q8_1` already uses dp4a plus\n  `__byte_perm` unpacking; the decode floor is the 24 ms bandwidth wall, so the whole\n  remaining gap is ~5 ms, and it is not in that loop.\n- **Expecting a big prefill win from PQ2_0 tile tuning.** 483 t/s ceiling vs a 510 t/s\n  cross-check; the headroom is single-digit percent.\n- **Fusing the FWHT into the GEMM.** It would remove at most ~0.5 ms of launch latency\n  per decode step.\n\n## Where the remaining decode time must be (to be confirmed with ncu)\n\n24 ms of weight streaming against a ~29 ms measured token leaves roughly 5 ms. Candidates,\nin rough order of expected size:\n\n1. **KV cache traffic at depth.** 16 full-attention layers x 4 KV heads x 256 x 2 x 2 B =\n   64 KiB/token, which matches the model card exactly. At 32K depth that is 2 GiB read per\n   token (+5.8 ms, +29%); at 64K it is 4 GiB (+11.6 ms, +58%). A 4-bit KV cache\n   (~18 KiB/token) cuts most of that. This is a configuration change, not a kernel.\n2. **Launch count.** At 1.6 us per launch, a decode step with 1000-2000 launches spends\n   1.6-3.2 ms. Small ops (norms, rope, the 257 FWHT passes) each do less work than their\n   own launch cost, so the GPU starves there.\n3. **The linear-attention path.** 48 of 64 layers are gated delta net (`ssm-scan.cu`,\n   conv1d + a sequential scan), which is not a dp4a matmul and is therefore not covered by\n   the roofline above.\n\nThe per-kernel time table from `ncu` over one decode step will settle the split.\n\n\n# Decode profile: where the 34.45 ms per token goes\n\nMeasured with Nsight Systems on this machine. This closes the open question from\n`docs/roofline-rtx3060.md`, which had roughly 9 ms of each decode token unaccounted for\nafter weight streaming, the Hadamard passes and KV traffic.\n\n## Method and its one limitation\n\n```\nnsys profile --trace=cuda --sample=none --cpuctxsw=none \\\n  -o results/nsys-d8 ./bin/cuda/llama-bench -m <PQ2_0> -ngl 99 -fa on -p 0 -n 8 -d 0 -r 1\nnsys export --type sqlite -o results/nsys-d8.sqlite results/nsys-d8.nsys-rep\n```\n\nnsys 2023.4.4 against driver 595.91.07 (a ~2 year gap) **silently dropped most kernel\nrecords**: the trace's CUDA runtime span is 2690 ms while its kernel span is only 168 ms,\nso 2 complete decode passes were captured out of 64 requested. There is no warning in the\nlog. Do not read the totals as a full-run total.\n\nThe capture is still usable because the passes are identical at depth 0, and because the\nper-pass figure checks out independently: **68.9 ms of kernel time over 2 passes =\n34.45 ms/pass, against a measured 34.22 ms/token wall clock** (29.22 t/s from\n`llama-bench -p 0 -n 64 -d 0`). The pass count is fixed by `gated_delta_net` appearing\nexactly 48 times per pass (one per linear-attention layer), which is independent of the\nmatmul count.\n\n## The decomposition\n\n| Kernel | ms/token | share | calls/token |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| `mul_mat_vec_q` (ternary GEMV) | 28.45 | 82.6% | 361 |\n| `k_get_rows_float_vec` | 0.98 | 2.8% | 48 |\n| `gated_delta_net_cuda` | 0.92 | 2.7% | 48 |\n| `rms_norm_f32` | 0.80 | 2.3% | 209 |\n| `scale_f32` | 0.52 | 1.5% | 48 |\n| `quantize_q8_1` | 0.52 | 1.5% | 361 |\n| `fwht_cuda_block` (Hadamard) | 0.52 | 1.5% | 258 |\n| `mul_mat_vec_f` | 0.38 | 1.1% | 96 |\n| `cpy_scalar` | 0.28 | 0.8% | 112 |\n| `flash_attn_ext_f16` | 0.20 | 0.6% | 16 |\n| about ten more types | ~0.38 | 1.1% | |\n| **total** | **34.45** | 100% | ~1967 launches |\n\nCall counts per token are slightly under-counted (361 GEMV calls against the 401 rotated\nweights in the model) because the second captured pass is truncated. The time shares are\nunaffected to within a percent.\n\n## Three conclusions\n\n**1. Decode is not launch-bound.** 34.45 ms of GPU kernel time against 34.22 ms of wall\nclock means the GPU is saturated. With ~1967 launches per token at a measured 1.6 us each,\nlaunch enqueue is fully hidden behind execution. CUDA graphs, launch batching, and\nkernel-count reduction are therefore *not* worth pursuing on this workload.\n\n**2. The ternary GEMV is at 84.3% of the bandwidth roofline.** 28.45 ms to stream\n7.19 GiB is 253 GB/s effective, against the measured 300 GB/s streaming ceiling. The\nremaining 15.7% (~4.5 ms, 13% of the token) is the entire prize for GEMV micro-optimization,\nand the inner loop already uses `dp4a` with `__byte_perm` unpacking.\n\n**3. The non-GEMV 17.4% is fragmented.** No single kernel exceeds 2.8%, and the biggest\ngroup is normalisation and quantisation plumbing: `rms_norm_f32` (209 calls) +\n`scale_f32` (48) + `quantize_q8_1` (361) + `cpy_scalar` (112) = 2.12 ms/token across 730\nlaunches. These are memory-bound small kernels whose cost is dominated by launch latency\nrather than throughput; they are the one place where fusion could pay, because unlike the\nGEMV they are *not* bandwidth-saturated.\n\nNote that `quantize_q8_1` runs 361 times per token, once per GEMV, even though the\n`hadamard_memo` in `llama-graph.cpp` means many GEMVs share the same rotated activation.\nQuantising a shared activation once instead of per consumer is a concrete, bounded target.\n\n## What this rules out, and what it invites\n\n| Idea | Verdict |\n|---|---|\n| CUDA graphs / fewer launches | Ruled out: the GPU is saturated, launches are already hidden |\n| Rewriting the GEMV inner loop | At most 13%, and it is already `dp4a`-based |\n| Fusing the Hadamard pass | Inert: 0.52 ms/token total (1.5%) |\n| GEMV tuning for cache/occupancy | Worth one bounded attempt: 15.7% gap to roofline |\n| Fusing norm/quantise plumbing | The largest remaining non-GEMV target, ~2 ms/token across 730 launches |\n| Speculative decoding | **Done, and it dwarfs all of the above: 4.9-6.0x.** See ADR 0003 |\n\n\n# Confirmed profile: Bonsai 2 27B on the RTX 3060 12 GB\n\nThis is the configuration to run, with the measurement behind each choice. Every\nnumber is from a file under `results/`; predictions that measurement overturned are\nrecorded in `docs/decisions/`.\n\n## The profile\n\n```bash\nscripts/serve.sh                 # config/rt3060.env\n```\n\n| Setting | Value | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| pack | `PQ2_0` (7.19 GiB) | `PTQ1_0` decodes 31% slower (ADR 0001) |\n| `-ngl` | 99 | everything offloaded; weights are 6,872 MiB |\n| `-fa` | on | required by the model's attention path |\n| `-c` | 32768 (64K verified) | 64K fits at 11,732 MiB; ~74K is the f16 ceiling |\n| KV type | f16 | quantized KV is **slower** at every depth (ADR 0002) |\n| `-b` / `-ub` | 2048 / 512 | swept 512-4096: pp512 spans 487-494, all within noise |\n| `-np` | 1 | `-np 2` splits the pool to 16,384 tokens per slot and costs VRAM |\n| `--reasoning-budget` | 2048 | thinking dominates latency at 29 tok/s |\n| `reasoning_effort` | medium | documented as about as accurate at moderate limits |\n| `--spec-type` | `ngram-simple` | 4.9-6.0x when the output reuses the context, inert otherwise (ADR 0003) |\n| mmproj | off GPU | 0.63 GB, only needed for image input |\n\n## Operating profiles\n\nOne server serves both modes, because `reasoning_effort` is a per-request field.\n`ngram-simple` is harmless when it does not apply, so it stays on in both.\n\n| | Reasoning chat | Agentic / mechanical edits |\n|---|---|---|\n| `reasoning_effort` in the request | `medium` (server default) | **`none`** |\n| decode | 28.5 t/s | **146 t/s** |\n| prefill | 494 t/s | 494 t/s |\n| speculation | inert (0 drafts) | 279 drafts, 85% accepted |\n| when to use | hard problems, math, planning | reading and rewriting files, refactors, mechanical edits |\n\nThe mode difference is the single largest performance fact about this setup: **5x on\ndecode**, larger than every kernel-level opportunity combined. It is a quality trade, so\nit belongs to the caller, not to the server default - but a caller that does not send it\nfor mechanical work is leaving 5x on the table.\n\nTrap worth repeating: `thinking_budget_tokens: 0` does **not** disable thinking and\ntherefore does **not** unlock speculation. Only `reasoning_effort: \"none\"` does. See\n[ADR 0003](decisions/0003-ngram-speculation.md).\n\n## Measured performance\n\nBaseline, load average 1.9-2.9, `REPS=3`.\n\n| Phase | Result | Roofline | Files |\n|---|---:|---|---|\n| pp512 | **493.8 ± 2.5 t/s** | 483 (dp4a) / 510 (3070-scaled) | `results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164343.log` |\n| pp2048 | 491.3 t/s | - | same |\n| pp8192 | 474.9 t/s | - | same |\n| tg128 | **29.55 ± 0.13 t/s** | 42 t/s ceiling, 24.0 ms floor | `results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164528.log` |\n| tg128 @ d8192 | 28.04 t/s | - | same |\n| tg128 @ d32768 | **24.09 t/s** | - | same |\n\nIndependent confirmation of decode through a different path: `scripts/telemetry.sh`\nduring a live 300-token request computed **29.1 tok/s** from the server's own counters\nand showed the load transient (GPU 97% -> 12%, 175 W -> 46 W, 67 C -> 57 C)\n(`results/telemetry-20260927-171527.csv`).\n\n### Prefill is at the hardware ceiling\n\nThe card sustains 13.0e12 int8 MAC/s of `dp4a` (measured, `tools/microbench/roofline.cu`)\nand PP512 needs 13.75e12 MACs, so the ceiling is 483 t/s. The measurement lands 2% above\nthat, between it and the 510 t/s obtained by scaling the RTX 3070's community result.\nThroughput is flat from 512 to 8192 tokens, so this is throughput-limited, not\noverhead-limited. **There is no prefill tuning left in the `PQ2_0` path.**\n\n### Where decode time goes\n\nPer token at depth 0: **33.8 ms measured against a 24.0 ms weight-streaming floor.**\n\n| Component | Time | Source |\n|---|---:|---|\n| weight streaming (7.19 GiB at 300 GB/s) | 24.0 ms | measured bandwidth |\n| FWHT (257 Hadamard passes) | 0.54 ms | measured, 2.1 us each |\n| KV read at depth 32K | 7.7 ms | measured depth delta |\n| **unaccounted at depth 0** | **~9.3 ms** | remainder |\n\nThe unaccounted part is the one open question. `scripts/profile-nsys.sh` and\n`scripts/profile-ncu.sh` exist to identify it per kernel; neither has been run yet\n(nsys is not installed, and ncu needs root for hardware counters on this machine).\n\n## Measured 12 GB budget\n\nllama.cpp sees 11,904 MiB with the display attached (12,288 MiB total, ~384 MiB for the\ndesktop).\n\n| Component | Size |\n|---|---:|\n| weights (`PQ2_0`) | 6,872 MiB |\n| compute buffers | 736 MiB at ctx 32768, 764 at 65536 |\n| KV (f16) | 64 MiB per 1024 tokens |\n\nObserved totals: **9,656 MiB at ctx 32768** and **11,732 MiB at ctx 65536**, both\nverified to load and serve. That leaves about 556 MiB spare at 64K, putting the f16\ncontext ceiling near **74K tokens**.\n\n## Reproduction\n\n```bash\nscripts/setup.sh all                        # pinned binary + both packs, hash-checked\nscripts/roofline.sh                         # card ceilings\nscripts/bench.sh bin/cuda PQ2_0             # both phases, records machine state\nscripts/parity-check.sh bin/cuda build/llama-sm86/bin PQ2_0   # build equivalence\nscripts/serve.sh start && scripts/telemetry.sh\n```\n\n`scripts/parity-check.sh` passes: the sm_86 source build reproduces the pinned binary\nexactly (perplexity 5.8953 vs 5.8953, identical greedy tokens) on\n`results/parity-20260927-164848/`. Any future kernel change must keep that true.\n\n## Explored and rejected\n\n| Option | Result |\n|---|---|\n| `PTQ1_0` packing | prefill +4%, decode -24%, effective bandwidth 45% vs 71% |\n| 4-bit KV (`q4_0`/`q4_0`) | slower at every depth; -19% at 32K |\n| 4-bit K only (`q4_0`/`f16`) | worst of all: 17.36 t/s at depth 0 |\n| `-ub` 1024/2048, `-b` 4096 | within 1.5% of default, i.e. noise |\n| `-np` 2 | halves per-slot context to 16,384 and raises VRAM |\n| tuning `PQ2_0` prefill | at the dp4a roofline already |\n| CUDA graphs / launch batching | GPU is saturated; ~1967 launches/token are already hidden |\n| `ngram-cache` speculation | 2.19x vs 4.90x for `ngram-simple`, so not worth carrying |\n| speculation while thinking | 0 drafts at every thinking level; inert |\n\n## What would still move the needle\n\n1. **The GEMV's 15.7% gap to the bandwidth roofline** (~4.5 ms/token, 13% of decode). The\n   inner loop is already `dp4a`-based, so this is a bounded, hard target.\n2. **Fusing the normalisation/quantisation plumbing**: `rms_norm_f32` (209 calls),\n   `scale_f32` (48), `quantize_q8_1` (361) and `cpy_scalar` (112) cost ~2.1 ms/token\n   across 730 launches and are *not* bandwidth-saturated, unlike the GEMV. Quantising a\n   shared rotated activation once instead of once per consumer is the concrete version.\n3. **Tensor cores for prefill.** The only route past 483 t/s, and it needs a different\n   weight packing plus mma kernels: a re-architecture, not a tuning pass.\n4. **Beyond ~74K context.** Requires a quantized cache and therefore accepting the\n   measured speed penalty plus calibrating the mean-centering bias.\n\nSpeculative decoding was item 3 on this list before it was measured; it is now the\nconfiguration default and worth 4.9-6.0x, far more than items 1-2 combined\n([ADR 0003](decisions/0003-ngram-speculation.md)).\n\n\n# RTX 3060 roofline (measured)\n\nAll numbers below were measured on this machine, not taken from spec sheets or\ncommunity tables. They are the denominators used to judge whether a model-level\nresult is good or bad, and to decide where optimization effort can pay off.\n\n- GPU: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3060 12 GB (GA106, sm_86, 28 SMs, 1837 MHz sustained)\n- Driver 595.91.07, CUDA 12.4, `third_party/llama.cpp` at `adfffbe`\n- Harness: `tools/microbench/roofline.cu`, run via `scripts/roofline.sh`\n- Raw output: `results/roofline-*.txt`\n\n## Measured ceilings\n\n| Quantity | Measured | Reference | Note |\n|---|---:|---|---|\n| Streaming read bandwidth | **298 - 307 GB/s** | 360 GB/s (192-bit GDDR6 spec) | 83-85% of spec; the practical ceiling |\n| Empty kernel launch | **1.6 - 1.8 us** | - | rises to 2.9 us under CPU load (parallel build) |\n| Null-work launch (28x256) | **1.6 us** | - | launch cost is grid-size independent at this size |\n| dp4a (int8 4-way dot) | **26.0 TOPS(int8)** = 13.0e12 MAC/s | - | 8 independent chains |\n| FP32 FMA | **11.7 TFLOPS** = 5.84e12 FMA/s | 12.8 TFLOPS (28 SM x 128 lane x 2 x 1.837 GHz) | 91% of issue-limited peak |\n| FP16x2 FMA (`__hfma2`) | **13.1 TFLOPS** | - | only 1.12x FP32 |\n| FWHT N=1024, decode (5 blocks) | **2.07 us/call** | - | the model's rotation block size |\n| FWHT N=1024, decode (17 blocks) | **2.09 us/call** | - | launch-latency bound, not bandwidth |\n| FWHT N=1024, pp512 (2560 blocks) | **66 us** (318 GB/s) | 298-307 GB/s | at the bandwidth ceiling |\n| FWHT N=1024, pp512 (8704 blocks) | **240 us** (297 GB/s) | 298-307 GB/s | at the bandwidth ceiling |\n\n### The dp4a result that matters\n\ndp4a delivers **13.0e12 MAC/s against FP32's 5.84e12 FMA/s, i.e. 2.2x, not 4x.**\nGA106 has 128 FP32 lanes but only 64 INT32 lanes per SM, so the int8 advantage on\nthis card is roughly half of what a datacenter part gives. Any plan that assumes a\n4x int8 multiplier over-counts the prefill ceiling by about 2x.\n\n## Derived floors for Bonsai 2 27B\n\nModel facts taken from the GGUF header (`tools/gguf-inspect.py`): 64 blocks,\nembedding 5120, FFN 17408, 24 heads / 4 KV heads, key = value = 256,\n`full_attention_interval = 4` (16 full-attention + 48 linear-attention layers),\nHadamard block 1024, 401 rotated weight tensors.\n\n### Decode (bandwidth-bound)\n\nWeights are streamed once per token.\n\n| Pack | Weight bytes | Floor at 300 GB/s | Ceiling | Practical (82-85% eff.) |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| PTQ1_0 | 5.95 GB | 19.8 ms | 50 t/s | 40 - 43 t/s |\n| PQ2_0 | 7.21 GB | 24.0 ms | 42 t/s | 34 - 36 t/s |\n\n#### Confirmed by measurement\n\n`scripts/bench.sh bin/cuda PQ2_0` with `PHASES=tg`\n(`results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164528.log`):\n\n| Test | Measured t/s | ms/token | Efficiency vs the 24.0 ms floor |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| tg128 | 29.55 ± 0.13 | 33.8 | 71% |\n| tg128 @ d8192 | 28.04 ± 0.07 | 35.7 | 67% |\n| tg128 @ d32768 | 24.09 ± 0.07 | 41.5 | 58% |\n\n**The KV traffic model is confirmed.** The depth deltas against the 64 KiB/token figure:\n\n| Depth | Predicted KV read | Predicted added time | Measured delta | Error |\n|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| 8192 | 512 MiB | +1.79 ms | +1.9 ms | +6% |\n| 32768 | 2.0 GiB | +7.16 ms | +7.7 ms | +8% |\n\nAt 32K depth, KV is **19% of the decode token**; the ADR 0002 prediction that a 4-bit\ncache becomes worthwhile above 8-32K depth rests on a model that has now been checked\nagainst this card.\n\n#### The remaining decode gap\n\nAt depth 0, decode takes 33.8 ms against a 24.0 ms streaming floor, so **9.8 ms is\nneither weight streaming nor KV**. The RTX 3070 (same architecture, 448 GB/s) measured\n22.9 ms for the same model, which scales to 29 ms at this card's bandwidth: the 3060 is\nabout 5 ms worse than bandwidth scaling predicts, consistent with its 28 SMs and 3 MB L2\nhurting the non-matmul parts of the step.\n\nThat 9.8 ms is the real optimization target, and the candidate list is in the section\nbelow. `scripts/profile-ncu.sh` exists to identify it per kernel rather than by\nelimination.\n\n### Prefill (dp4a-bound)\n\nPP512 needs 2 x 26.9e9 x 512 = 27.5 TFLOP = 13.75e12 int8 MACs.\n\n```\n13.75e12 MAC / 13.0e12 MAC/s = 1.06 s  ->  483 t/s ceiling\n```\n\nCross-check: the RTX 3070 (46 SMs, same sm_86) measured PP512 = 838.9 t/s in the\ncommunity table. Scaled to 28 SMs that is 510 t/s. Two independent routes agree, so\n**PQ2_0 prefill is already at the dp4a roofline** and a tile-tuning effort can only\nrecover the residual 5-15%, not a multiple.\n\n#### Confirmed by measurement\n\n`scripts/bench.sh bin/cuda PQ2_0` with `PHASES=pp`\n(`results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164343.log`), load average 4.7:\n\n| Test | Measured t/s | Roofline prediction |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| pp128 | 460.1 ± 18.7 | - |\n| pp512 | 493.8 ± 2.5 | 483 (dp4a) / 510 (3070-scaled) |\n| pp2048 | 491.3 ± 0.5 | - |\n| pp8192 | 474.9 ± 0.2 | - |\n\nThe measurement lands between the two predictions, within 2% of the mean. Throughput is\nflat from 512 to 8192 tokens, so prefill is throughput-limited rather than\noverhead-limited, exactly as the dp4a model says.\n\n**Consequence: PQ2_0 prefill tuning is a dead end.** The only way to prefill materially\nfaster on this card is to stop doing int8 `dp4a`, for example by feeding the ternary\nweights to tensor cores, which needs a different weight packing and is a\nre-architecture rather than a tuning exercise.\n\nPTQ1_0 is a different story: on the RTX 3090 Ti the community measured PP512 = 805 for\nPTQ1_0 against 1552 for PQ2_0. Both packs perform the same number of int8 MACs, so\nPTQ1_0's prefill cannot be dp4a-bound; its dense-trit unpacking is instruction-issue\nbound and roughly doubles the cost. See ADR 0001.\n\n### FWHT (the Hadamard rotation) is not a bottleneck\n\nThe rotation is dispatched from inside `mul_mat` when the node carries\n`GGML_HINT_SRC0_IS_HADAMARD` (`ggml-cuda.cu:1822`), and `hadamard_memo` is keyed on\n`(activation, rot)` (`llama-graph.cpp:1556`), so weights that share an activation and\nrotation share one transform. The pass count is on the order of 257 per forward step.\n\n| Phase | Cost | Share |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| decode | 257 x ~2.1 us = **0.54 ms** | ~1.9% of a 29 ms token |\n| pp512 | ~0.5 ms | ~0.08% of a 0.6-1.1 s prefill |\n\nFWHT is at the bandwidth ceiling where it is bandwidth-bound, and launch-latency bound\nat decode. It is not where the remaining time is.\n\n## What this rules out\n\n- **Rewriting the decode GEMV inner loop.** `vec_dot_pq2_0_q8_1` already uses dp4a plus\n  `__byte_perm` unpacking; the decode floor is the 24 ms bandwidth wall, so the whole\n  remaining gap is ~5 ms, and it is not in that loop.\n- **Expecting a big prefill win from PQ2_0 tile tuning.** 483 t/s ceiling vs a 510 t/s\n  cross-check; the headroom is single-digit percent.\n- **Fusing the FWHT into the GEMM.** It would remove at most ~0.5 ms of launch latency\n  per decode step.\n\n## Where the remaining decode time must be (to be confirmed with ncu)\n\n24 ms of weight streaming against a ~29 ms measured token leaves roughly 5 ms. Candidates,\nin rough order of expected size:\n\n1. **KV cache traffic at depth.** 16 full-attention layers x 4 KV heads x 256 x 2 x 2 B =\n   64 KiB/token, which matches the model card exactly. At 32K depth that is 2 GiB read per\n   token (+5.8 ms, +29%); at 64K it is 4 GiB (+11.6 ms, +58%). A 4-bit KV cache\n   (~18 KiB/token) cuts most of that. This is a configuration change, not a kernel.\n2. **Launch count.** At 1.6 us per launch, a decode step with 1000-2000 launches spends\n   1.6-3.2 ms. Small ops (norms, rope, the 257 FWHT passes) each do less work than their\n   own launch cost, so the GPU starves there.\n3. **The linear-attention path.** 48 of 64 layers are gated delta net (`ssm-scan.cu`,\n   conv1d + a sequential scan), which is not a dp4a matmul and is therefore not covered by\n   the roofline above.\n\nThe per-kernel time table from `ncu` over one decode step will settle the split.\n\n\n# Decode profile: where the 34.45 ms per token goes\n\nMeasured with Nsight Systems on this machine. This closes the open question from\n`docs/roofline-rtx3060.md`, which had roughly 9 ms of each decode token unaccounted for\nafter weight streaming, the Hadamard passes and KV traffic.\n\n## Method and its one limitation\n\n```\nnsys profile --trace=cuda --sample=none --cpuctxsw=none \\\n  -o results/nsys-d8 ./bin/cuda/llama-bench -m <PQ2_0> -ngl 99 -fa on -p 0 -n 8 -d 0 -r 1\nnsys export --type sqlite -o results/nsys-d8.sqlite results/nsys-d8.nsys-rep\n```\n\nnsys 2023.4.4 against driver 595.91.07 (a ~2 year gap) **silently dropped most kernel\nrecords**: the trace's CUDA runtime span is 2690 ms while its kernel span is only 168 ms,\nso 2 complete decode passes were captured out of 64 requested. There is no warning in the\nlog. Do not read the totals as a full-run total.\n\nThe capture is still usable because the passes are identical at depth 0, and because the\nper-pass figure checks out independently: **68.9 ms of kernel time over 2 passes =\n34.45 ms/pass, against a measured 34.22 ms/token wall clock** (29.22 t/s from\n`llama-bench -p 0 -n 64 -d 0`). The pass count is fixed by `gated_delta_net` appearing\nexactly 48 times per pass (one per linear-attention layer), which is independent of the\nmatmul count.\n\n## The decomposition\n\n| Kernel | ms/token | share | calls/token |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| `mul_mat_vec_q` (ternary GEMV) | 28.45 | 82.6% | 361 |\n| `k_get_rows_float_vec` | 0.98 | 2.8% | 48 |\n| `gated_delta_net_cuda` | 0.92 | 2.7% | 48 |\n| `rms_norm_f32` | 0.80 | 2.3% | 209 |\n| `scale_f32` | 0.52 | 1.5% | 48 |\n| `quantize_q8_1` | 0.52 | 1.5% | 361 |\n| `fwht_cuda_block` (Hadamard) | 0.52 | 1.5% | 258 |\n| `mul_mat_vec_f` | 0.38 | 1.1% | 96 |\n| `cpy_scalar` | 0.28 | 0.8% | 112 |\n| `flash_attn_ext_f16` | 0.20 | 0.6% | 16 |\n| about ten more types | ~0.38 | 1.1% | |\n| **total** | **34.45** | 100% | ~1967 launches |\n\nCall counts per token are slightly under-counted (361 GEMV calls against the 401 rotated\nweights in the model) because the second captured pass is truncated. The time shares are\nunaffected to within a percent.\n\n## Three conclusions\n\n**1. Decode is not launch-bound.** 34.45 ms of GPU kernel time against 34.22 ms of wall\nclock means the GPU is saturated. With ~1967 launches per token at a measured 1.6 us each,\nlaunch enqueue is fully hidden behind execution. CUDA graphs, launch batching, and\nkernel-count reduction are therefore *not* worth pursuing on this workload.\n\n**2. The ternary GEMV is at 84.3% of the bandwidth roofline.** 28.45 ms to stream\n7.19 GiB is 253 GB/s effective, against the measured 300 GB/s streaming ceiling. The\nremaining 15.7% (~4.5 ms, 13% of the token) is the entire prize for GEMV micro-optimization,\nand the inner loop already uses `dp4a` with `__byte_perm` unpacking.\n\n**3. The non-GEMV 17.4% is fragmented.** No single kernel exceeds 2.8%, and the biggest\ngroup is normalisation and quantisation plumbing: `rms_norm_f32` (209 calls) +\n`scale_f32` (48) + `quantize_q8_1` (361) + `cpy_scalar` (112) = 2.12 ms/token across 730\nlaunches. These are memory-bound small kernels whose cost is dominated by launch latency\nrather than throughput; they are the one place where fusion could pay, because unlike the\nGEMV they are *not* bandwidth-saturated.\n\nNote that `quantize_q8_1` runs 361 times per token, once per GEMV, even though the\n`hadamard_memo` in `llama-graph.cpp` means many GEMVs share the same rotated activation.\nQuantising a shared activation once instead of per consumer is a concrete, bounded target.\n\n## What this rules out, and what it invites\n\n| Idea | Verdict |\n|---|---|\n| CUDA graphs / fewer launches | Ruled out: the GPU is saturated, launches are already hidden |\n| Rewriting the GEMV inner loop | At most 13%, and it is already `dp4a`-based |\n| Fusing the Hadamard pass | Inert: 0.52 ms/token total (1.5%) |\n| GEMV tuning for cache/occupancy | Worth one bounded attempt: 15.7% gap to roofline |\n| Fusing norm/quantise plumbing | The largest remaining non-GEMV target, ~2 ms/token across 730 launches |\n| Speculative decoding | **Done, and it dwarfs all of the above: 4.9-6.0x.** See ADR 0003 |\n\n\n# Confirmed profile: Bonsai 2 27B on the RTX 3060 12 GB\n\nThis is the configuration to run, with the measurement behind each choice. Every\nnumber is from a file under `results/`; predictions that measurement overturned are\nrecorded in `docs/decisions/`.\n\n## The profile\n\n```bash\nscripts/serve.sh                 # config/rt3060.env\n```\n\n| Setting | Value | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| pack | `PQ2_0` (7.19 GiB) | `PTQ1_0` decodes 31% slower (ADR 0001) |\n| `-ngl` | 99 | everything offloaded; weights are 6,872 MiB |\n| `-fa` | on | required by the model's attention path |\n| `-c` | 32768 (64K verified) | 64K fits at 11,732 MiB; ~74K is the f16 ceiling |\n| KV type | f16 | quantized KV is **slower** at every depth (ADR 0002) |\n| `-b` / `-ub` | 2048 / 512 | swept 512-4096: pp512 spans 487-494, all within noise |\n| `-np` | 1 | `-np 2` splits the pool to 16,384 tokens per slot and costs VRAM |\n| `--reasoning-budget` | 2048 | thinking dominates latency at 29 tok/s |\n| `reasoning_effort` | medium | documented as about as accurate at moderate limits |\n| `--spec-type` | `ngram-simple` | 4.9-6.0x when the output reuses the context, inert otherwise (ADR 0003) |\n| mmproj | off GPU | 0.63 GB, only needed for image input |\n\n## Operating profiles\n\nOne server serves both modes, because `reasoning_effort` is a per-request field.\n`ngram-simple` is harmless when it does not apply, so it stays on in both.\n\n| | Reasoning chat | Agentic / mechanical edits |\n|---|---|---|\n| `reasoning_effort` in the request | `medium` (server default) | **`none`** |\n| decode | 28.5 t/s | **146 t/s** |\n| prefill | 494 t/s | 494 t/s |\n| speculation | inert (0 drafts) | 279 drafts, 85% accepted |\n| when to use | hard problems, math, planning | reading and rewriting files, refactors, mechanical edits |\n\nThe mode difference is the single largest performance fact about this setup: **5x on\ndecode**, larger than every kernel-level opportunity combined. It is a quality trade, so\nit belongs to the caller, not to the server default - but a caller that does not send it\nfor mechanical work is leaving 5x on the table.\n\nTrap worth repeating: `thinking_budget_tokens: 0` does **not** disable thinking and\ntherefore does **not** unlock speculation. Only `reasoning_effort: \"none\"` does. See\n[ADR 0003](decisions/0003-ngram-speculation.md).\n\n## Measured performance\n\nBaseline, load average 1.9-2.9, `REPS=3`.\n\n| Phase | Result | Roofline | Files |\n|---|---:|---|---|\n| pp512 | **493.8 ± 2.5 t/s** | 483 (dp4a) / 510 (3070-scaled) | `results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164343.log` |\n| pp2048 | 491.3 t/s | - | same |\n| pp8192 | 474.9 t/s | - | same |\n| tg128 | **29.55 ± 0.13 t/s** | 42 t/s ceiling, 24.0 ms floor | `results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164528.log` |\n| tg128 @ d8192 | 28.04 t/s | - | same |\n| tg128 @ d32768 | **24.09 t/s** | - | same |\n\nIndependent confirmation of decode through a different path: `scripts/telemetry.sh`\nduring a live 300-token request computed **29.1 tok/s** from the server's own counters\nand showed the load transient (GPU 97% -> 12%, 175 W -> 46 W, 67 C -> 57 C)\n(`results/telemetry-20260927-171527.csv`).\n\n### Prefill is at the hardware ceiling\n\nThe card sustains 13.0e12 int8 MAC/s of `dp4a` (measured, `tools/microbench/roofline.cu`)\nand PP512 needs 13.75e12 MACs, so the ceiling is 483 t/s. The measurement lands 2% above\nthat, between it and the 510 t/s obtained by scaling the RTX 3070's community result.\nThroughput is flat from 512 to 8192 tokens, so this is throughput-limited, not\noverhead-limited. **There is no prefill tuning left in the `PQ2_0` path.**\n\n### Where decode time goes\n\nPer token at depth 0: **33.8 ms measured against a 24.0 ms weight-streaming floor.**\n\n| Component | Time | Source |\n|---|---:|---|\n| weight streaming (7.19 GiB at 300 GB/s) | 24.0 ms | measured bandwidth |\n| FWHT (257 Hadamard passes) | 0.54 ms | measured, 2.1 us each |\n| KV read at depth 32K | 7.7 ms | measured depth delta |\n| **unaccounted at depth 0** | **~9.3 ms** | remainder |\n\nThe unaccounted part is the one open question. `scripts/profile-nsys.sh` and\n`scripts/profile-ncu.sh` exist to identify it per kernel; neither has been run yet\n(nsys is not installed, and ncu needs root for hardware counters on this machine).\n\n## Measured 12 GB budget\n\nllama.cpp sees 11,904 MiB with the display attached (12,288 MiB total, ~384 MiB for the\ndesktop).\n\n| Component | Size |\n|---|---:|\n| weights (`PQ2_0`) | 6,872 MiB |\n| compute buffers | 736 MiB at ctx 32768, 764 at 65536 |\n| KV (f16) | 64 MiB per 1024 tokens |\n\nObserved totals: **9,656 MiB at ctx 32768** and **11,732 MiB at ctx 65536**, both\nverified to load and serve. That leaves about 556 MiB spare at 64K, putting the f16\ncontext ceiling near **74K tokens**.\n\n## Reproduction\n\n```bash\nscripts/setup.sh all                        # pinned binary + both packs, hash-checked\nscripts/roofline.sh                         # card ceilings\nscripts/bench.sh bin/cuda PQ2_0             # both phases, records machine state\nscripts/parity-check.sh bin/cuda build/llama-sm86/bin PQ2_0   # build equivalence\nscripts/serve.sh start && scripts/telemetry.sh\n```\n\n`scripts/parity-check.sh` passes: the sm_86 source build reproduces the pinned binary\nexactly (perplexity 5.8953 vs 5.8953, identical greedy tokens) on\n`results/parity-20260927-164848/`. Any future kernel change must keep that true.\n\n## Explored and rejected\n\n| Option | Result |\n|---|---|\n| `PTQ1_0` packing | prefill +4%, decode -24%, effective bandwidth 45% vs 71% |\n| 4-bit KV (`q4_0`/`q4_0`) | slower at every depth; -19% at 32K |\n| 4-bit K only (`q4_0`/`f16`) | worst of all: 17.36 t/s at depth 0 |\n| `-ub` 1024/2048, `-b` 4096 | within 1.5% of default, i.e. noise |\n| `-np` 2 | halves per-slot context to 16,384 and raises VRAM |\n| tuning `PQ2_0` prefill | at the dp4a roofline already |\n| CUDA graphs / launch batching | GPU is saturated; ~1967 launches/token are already hidden |\n| `ngram-cache` speculation | 2.19x vs 4.90x for `ngram-simple`, so not worth carrying |\n| speculation while thinking | 0 drafts at every thinking level; inert |\n\n## What would still move the needle\n\n1. **The GEMV's 15.7% gap to the bandwidth roofline** (~4.5 ms/token, 13% of decode). The\n   inner loop is already `dp4a`-based, so this is a bounded, hard target.\n2. **Fusing the normalisation/quantisation plumbing**: `rms_norm_f32` (209 calls),\n   `scale_f32` (48), `quantize_q8_1` (361) and `cpy_scalar` (112) cost ~2.1 ms/token\n   across 730 launches and are *not* bandwidth-saturated, unlike the GEMV. Quantising a\n   shared rotated activation once instead of once per consumer is the concrete version.\n3. **Tensor cores for prefill.** The only route past 483 t/s, and it needs a different\n   weight packing plus mma kernels: a re-architecture, not a tuning pass.\n4. **Beyond ~74K context.** Requires a quantized cache and therefore accepting the\n   measured speed penalty plus calibrating the mean-centering bias.\n\nSpeculative decoding was item 3 on this list before it was measured; it is now the\nconfiguration default and worth 4.9-6.0x, far more than items 1-2 combined\n([ADR 0003](decisions/0003-ngram-speculation.md)).\n\n\n# RTX 3060 roofline (measured)\n\nAll numbers below were measured on this machine, not taken from spec sheets or\ncommunity tables. They are the denominators used to judge whether a model-level\nresult is good or bad, and to decide where optimization effort can pay off.\n\n- GPU: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3060 12 GB (GA106, sm_86, 28 SMs, 1837 MHz sustained)\n- Driver 595.91.07, CUDA 12.4, `third_party/llama.cpp` at `adfffbe`\n- Harness: `tools/microbench/roofline.cu`, run via `scripts/roofline.sh`\n- Raw output: `results/roofline-*.txt`\n\n## Measured ceilings\n\n| Quantity | Measured | Reference | Note |\n|---|---:|---|---|\n| Streaming read bandwidth | **298 - 307 GB/s** | 360 GB/s (192-bit GDDR6 spec) | 83-85% of spec; the practical ceiling |\n| Empty kernel launch | **1.6 - 1.8 us** | - | rises to 2.9 us under CPU load (parallel build) |\n| Null-work launch (28x256) | **1.6 us** | - | launch cost is grid-size independent at this size |\n| dp4a (int8 4-way dot) | **26.0 TOPS(int8)** = 13.0e12 MAC/s | - | 8 independent chains |\n| FP32 FMA | **11.7 TFLOPS** = 5.84e12 FMA/s | 12.8 TFLOPS (28 SM x 128 lane x 2 x 1.837 GHz) | 91% of issue-limited peak |\n| FP16x2 FMA (`__hfma2`) | **13.1 TFLOPS** | - | only 1.12x FP32 |\n| FWHT N=1024, decode (5 blocks) | **2.07 us/call** | - | the model's rotation block size |\n| FWHT N=1024, decode (17 blocks) | **2.09 us/call** | - | launch-latency bound, not bandwidth |\n| FWHT N=1024, pp512 (2560 blocks) | **66 us** (318 GB/s) | 298-307 GB/s | at the bandwidth ceiling |\n| FWHT N=1024, pp512 (8704 blocks) | **240 us** (297 GB/s) | 298-307 GB/s | at the bandwidth ceiling |\n\n### The dp4a result that matters\n\ndp4a delivers **13.0e12 MAC/s against FP32's 5.84e12 FMA/s, i.e. 2.2x, not 4x.**\nGA106 has 128 FP32 lanes but only 64 INT32 lanes per SM, so the int8 advantage on\nthis card is roughly half of what a datacenter part gives. Any plan that assumes a\n4x int8 multiplier over-counts the prefill ceiling by about 2x.\n\n## Derived floors for Bonsai 2 27B\n\nModel facts taken from the GGUF header (`tools/gguf-inspect.py`): 64 blocks,\nembedding 5120, FFN 17408, 24 heads / 4 KV heads, key = value = 256,\n`full_attention_interval = 4` (16 full-attention + 48 linear-attention layers),\nHadamard block 1024, 401 rotated weight tensors.\n\n### Decode (bandwidth-bound)\n\nWeights are streamed once per token.\n\n| Pack | Weight bytes | Floor at 300 GB/s | Ceiling | Practical (82-85% eff.) |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| PTQ1_0 | 5.95 GB | 19.8 ms | 50 t/s | 40 - 43 t/s |\n| PQ2_0 | 7.21 GB | 24.0 ms | 42 t/s | 34 - 36 t/s |\n\n#### Confirmed by measurement\n\n`scripts/bench.sh bin/cuda PQ2_0` with `PHASES=tg`\n(`results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164528.log`):\n\n| Test | Measured t/s | ms/token | Efficiency vs the 24.0 ms floor |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| tg128 | 29.55 ± 0.13 | 33.8 | 71% |\n| tg128 @ d8192 | 28.04 ± 0.07 | 35.7 | 67% |\n| tg128 @ d32768 | 24.09 ± 0.07 | 41.5 | 58% |\n\n**The KV traffic model is confirmed.** The depth deltas against the 64 KiB/token figure:\n\n| Depth | Predicted KV read | Predicted added time | Measured delta | Error |\n|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| 8192 | 512 MiB | +1.79 ms | +1.9 ms | +6% |\n| 32768 | 2.0 GiB | +7.16 ms | +7.7 ms | +8% |\n\nAt 32K depth, KV is **19% of the decode token**; the ADR 0002 prediction that a 4-bit\ncache becomes worthwhile above 8-32K depth rests on a model that has now been checked\nagainst this card.\n\n#### The remaining decode gap\n\nAt depth 0, decode takes 33.8 ms against a 24.0 ms streaming floor, so **9.8 ms is\nneither weight streaming nor KV**. The RTX 3070 (same architecture, 448 GB/s) measured\n22.9 ms for the same model, which scales to 29 ms at this card's bandwidth: the 3060 is\nabout 5 ms worse than bandwidth scaling predicts, consistent with its 28 SMs and 3 MB L2\nhurting the non-matmul parts of the step.\n\nThat 9.8 ms is the real optimization target, and the candidate list is in the section\nbelow. `scripts/profile-ncu.sh` exists to identify it per kernel rather than by\nelimination.\n\n### Prefill (dp4a-bound)\n\nPP512 needs 2 x 26.9e9 x 512 = 27.5 TFLOP = 13.75e12 int8 MACs.\n\n```\n13.75e12 MAC / 13.0e12 MAC/s = 1.06 s  ->  483 t/s ceiling\n```\n\nCross-check: the RTX 3070 (46 SMs, same sm_86) measured PP512 = 838.9 t/s in the\ncommunity table. Scaled to 28 SMs that is 510 t/s. Two independent routes agree, so\n**PQ2_0 prefill is already at the dp4a roofline** and a tile-tuning effort can only\nrecover the residual 5-15%, not a multiple.\n\n#### Confirmed by measurement\n\n`scripts/bench.sh bin/cuda PQ2_0` with `PHASES=pp`\n(`results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164343.log`), load average 4.7:\n\n| Test | Measured t/s | Roofline prediction |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| pp128 | 460.1 ± 18.7 | - |\n| pp512 | 493.8 ± 2.5 | 483 (dp4a) / 510 (3070-scaled) |\n| pp2048 | 491.3 ± 0.5 | - |\n| pp8192 | 474.9 ± 0.2 | - |\n\nThe measurement lands between the two predictions, within 2% of the mean. Throughput is\nflat from 512 to 8192 tokens, so prefill is throughput-limited rather than\noverhead-limited, exactly as the dp4a model says.\n\n**Consequence: PQ2_0 prefill tuning is a dead end.** The only way to prefill materially\nfaster on this card is to stop doing int8 `dp4a`, for example by feeding the ternary\nweights to tensor cores, which needs a different weight packing and is a\nre-architecture rather than a tuning exercise.\n\nPTQ1_0 is a different story: on the RTX 3090 Ti the community measured PP512 = 805 for\nPTQ1_0 against 1552 for PQ2_0. Both packs perform the same number of int8 MACs, so\nPTQ1_0's prefill cannot be dp4a-bound; its dense-trit unpacking is instruction-issue\nbound and roughly doubles the cost. See ADR 0001.\n\n### FWHT (the Hadamard rotation) is not a bottleneck\n\nThe rotation is dispatched from inside `mul_mat` when the node carries\n`GGML_HINT_SRC0_IS_HADAMARD` (`ggml-cuda.cu:1822`), and `hadamard_memo` is keyed on\n`(activation, rot)` (`llama-graph.cpp:1556`), so weights that share an activation and\nrotation share one transform. The pass count is on the order of 257 per forward step.\n\n| Phase | Cost | Share |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| decode | 257 x ~2.1 us = **0.54 ms** | ~1.9% of a 29 ms token |\n| pp512 | ~0.5 ms | ~0.08% of a 0.6-1.1 s prefill |\n\nFWHT is at the bandwidth ceiling where it is bandwidth-bound, and launch-latency bound\nat decode. It is not where the remaining time is.\n\n## What this rules out\n\n- **Rewriting the decode GEMV inner loop.** `vec_dot_pq2_0_q8_1` already uses dp4a plus\n  `__byte_perm` unpacking; the decode floor is the 24 ms bandwidth wall, so the whole\n  remaining gap is ~5 ms, and it is not in that loop.\n- **Expecting a big prefill win from PQ2_0 tile tuning.** 483 t/s ceiling vs a 510 t/s\n  cross-check; the headroom is single-digit percent.\n- **Fusing the FWHT into the GEMM.** It would remove at most ~0.5 ms of launch latency\n  per decode step.\n\n## Where the remaining decode time must be (to be confirmed with ncu)\n\n24 ms of weight streaming against a ~29 ms measured token leaves roughly 5 ms. Candidates,\nin rough order of expected size:\n\n1. **KV cache traffic at depth.** 16 full-attention layers x 4 KV heads x 256 x 2 x 2 B =\n   64 KiB/token, which matches the model card exactly. At 32K depth that is 2 GiB read per\n   token (+5.8 ms, +29%); at 64K it is 4 GiB (+11.6 ms, +58%). A 4-bit KV cache\n   (~18 KiB/token) cuts most of that. This is a configuration change, not a kernel.\n2. **Launch count.** At 1.6 us per launch, a decode step with 1000-2000 launches spends\n   1.6-3.2 ms. Small ops (norms, rope, the 257 FWHT passes) each do less work than their\n   own launch cost, so the GPU starves there.\n3. **The linear-attention path.** 48 of 64 layers are gated delta net (`ssm-scan.cu`,\n   conv1d + a sequential scan), which is not a dp4a matmul and is therefore not covered by\n   the roofline above.\n\nThe per-kernel time table from `ncu` over one decode step will settle the split.\n\n\n# Decode profile: where the 34.45 ms per token goes\n\nMeasured with Nsight Systems on this machine. This closes the open question from\n`docs/roofline-rtx3060.md`, which had roughly 9 ms of each decode token unaccounted for\nafter weight streaming, the Hadamard passes and KV traffic.\n\n## Method and its one limitation\n\n```\nnsys profile --trace=cuda --sample=none --cpuctxsw=none \\\n  -o results/nsys-d8 ./bin/cuda/llama-bench -m <PQ2_0> -ngl 99 -fa on -p 0 -n 8 -d 0 -r 1\nnsys export --type sqlite -o results/nsys-d8.sqlite results/nsys-d8.nsys-rep\n```\n\nnsys 2023.4.4 against driver 595.91.07 (a ~2 year gap) **silently dropped most kernel\nrecords**: the trace's CUDA runtime span is 2690 ms while its kernel span is only 168 ms,\nso 2 complete decode passes were captured out of 64 requested. There is no warning in the\nlog. Do not read the totals as a full-run total.\n\nThe capture is still usable because the passes are identical at depth 0, and because the\nper-pass figure checks out independently: **68.9 ms of kernel time over 2 passes =\n34.45 ms/pass, against a measured 34.22 ms/token wall clock** (29.22 t/s from\n`llama-bench -p 0 -n 64 -d 0`). The pass count is fixed by `gated_delta_net` appearing\nexactly 48 times per pass (one per linear-attention layer), which is independent of the\nmatmul count.\n\n## The decomposition\n\n| Kernel | ms/token | share | calls/token |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| `mul_mat_vec_q` (ternary GEMV) | 28.45 | 82.6% | 361 |\n| `k_get_rows_float_vec` | 0.98 | 2.8% | 48 |\n| `gated_delta_net_cuda` | 0.92 | 2.7% | 48 |\n| `rms_norm_f32` | 0.80 | 2.3% | 209 |\n| `scale_f32` | 0.52 | 1.5% | 48 |\n| `quantize_q8_1` | 0.52 | 1.5% | 361 |\n| `fwht_cuda_block` (Hadamard) | 0.52 | 1.5% | 258 |\n| `mul_mat_vec_f` | 0.38 | 1.1% | 96 |\n| `cpy_scalar` | 0.28 | 0.8% | 112 |\n| `flash_attn_ext_f16` | 0.20 | 0.6% | 16 |\n| about ten more types | ~0.38 | 1.1% | |\n| **total** | **34.45** | 100% | ~1967 launches |\n\nCall counts per token are slightly under-counted (361 GEMV calls against the 401 rotated\nweights in the model) because the second captured pass is truncated. The time shares are\nunaffected to within a percent.\n\n## Three conclusions\n\n**1. Decode is not launch-bound.** 34.45 ms of GPU kernel time against 34.22 ms of wall\nclock means the GPU is saturated. With ~1967 launches per token at a measured 1.6 us each,\nlaunch enqueue is fully hidden behind execution. CUDA graphs, launch batching, and\nkernel-count reduction are therefore *not* worth pursuing on this workload.\n\n**2. The ternary GEMV is at 84.3% of the bandwidth roofline.** 28.45 ms to stream\n7.19 GiB is 253 GB/s effective, against the measured 300 GB/s streaming ceiling. The\nremaining 15.7% (~4.5 ms, 13% of the token) is the entire prize for GEMV micro-optimization,\nand the inner loop already uses `dp4a` with `__byte_perm` unpacking.\n\n**3. The non-GEMV 17.4% is fragmented.** No single kernel exceeds 2.8%, and the biggest\ngroup is normalisation and quantisation plumbing: `rms_norm_f32` (209 calls) +\n`scale_f32` (48) + `quantize_q8_1` (361) + `cpy_scalar` (112) = 2.12 ms/token across 730\nlaunches. These are memory-bound small kernels whose cost is dominated by launch latency\nrather than throughput; they are the one place where fusion could pay, because unlike the\nGEMV they are *not* bandwidth-saturated.\n\nNote that `quantize_q8_1` runs 361 times per token, once per GEMV, even though the\n`hadamard_memo` in `llama-graph.cpp` means many GEMVs share the same rotated activation.\nQuantising a shared activation once instead of per consumer is a concrete, bounded target.\n\n## What this rules out, and what it invites\n\n| Idea | Verdict |\n|---|---|\n| CUDA graphs / fewer launches | Ruled out: the GPU is saturated, launches are already hidden |\n| Rewriting the GEMV inner loop | At most 13%, and it is already `dp4a`-based |\n| Fusing the Hadamard pass | Inert: 0.52 ms/token total (1.5%) |\n| GEMV tuning for cache/occupancy | Worth one bounded attempt: 15.7% gap to roofline |\n| Fusing norm/quantise plumbing | The largest remaining non-GEMV target, ~2 ms/token across 730 launches |\n| Speculative decoding | **Done, and it dwarfs all of the above: 4.9-6.0x.** See ADR 0003 |\n\n\n# Confirmed profile: Bonsai 2 27B on the RTX 3060 12 GB\n\nThis is the configuration to run, with the measurement behind each choice. Every\nnumber is from a file under `results/`; predictions that measurement overturned are\nrecorded in `docs/decisions/`.\n\n## The profile\n\n```bash\nscripts/serve.sh                 # config/rt3060.env\n```\n\n| Setting | Value | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| pack | `PQ2_0` (7.19 GiB) | `PTQ1_0` decodes 31% slower (ADR 0001) |\n| `-ngl` | 99 | everything offloaded; weights are 6,872 MiB |\n| `-fa` | on | required by the model's attention path |\n| `-c` | 32768 (64K verified) | 64K fits at 11,732 MiB; ~74K is the f16 ceiling |\n| KV type | f16 | quantized KV is **slower** at every depth (ADR 0002) |\n| `-b` / `-ub` | 2048 / 512 | swept 512-4096: pp512 spans 487-494, all within noise |\n| `-np` | 1 | `-np 2` splits the pool to 16,384 tokens per slot and costs VRAM |\n| `--reasoning-budget` | 2048 | thinking dominates latency at 29 tok/s |\n| `reasoning_effort` | medium | documented as about as accurate at moderate limits |\n| `--spec-type` | `ngram-simple` | 4.9-6.0x when the output reuses the context, inert otherwise (ADR 0003) |\n| mmproj | off GPU | 0.63 GB, only needed for image input |\n\n## Operating profiles\n\nOne server serves both modes, because `reasoning_effort` is a per-request field.\n`ngram-simple` is harmless when it does not apply, so it stays on in both.\n\n| | Reasoning chat | Agentic / mechanical edits |\n|---|---|---|\n| `reasoning_effort` in the request | `medium` (server default) | **`none`** |\n| decode | 28.5 t/s | **146 t/s** |\n| prefill | 494 t/s | 494 t/s |\n| speculation | inert (0 drafts) | 279 drafts, 85% accepted |\n| when to use | hard problems, math, planning | reading and rewriting files, refactors, mechanical edits |\n\nThe mode difference is the single largest performance fact about this setup: **5x on\ndecode**, larger than every kernel-level opportunity combined. It is a quality trade, so\nit belongs to the caller, not to the server default - but a caller that does not send it\nfor mechanical work is leaving 5x on the table.\n\nTrap worth repeating: `thinking_budget_tokens: 0` does **not** disable thinking and\ntherefore does **not** unlock speculation. Only `reasoning_effort: \"none\"` does. See\n[ADR 0003](decisions/0003-ngram-speculation.md).\n\n## Measured performance\n\nBaseline, load average 1.9-2.9, `REPS=3`.\n\n| Phase | Result | Roofline | Files |\n|---|---:|---|---|\n| pp512 | **493.8 ± 2.5 t/s** | 483 (dp4a) / 510 (3070-scaled) | `results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164343.log` |\n| pp2048 | 491.3 t/s | - | same |\n| pp8192 | 474.9 t/s | - | same |\n| tg128 | **29.55 ± 0.13 t/s** | 42 t/s ceiling, 24.0 ms floor | `results/bench-cuda-PQ2_0-kvf16-20260927-164528.log` |\n| tg128 @ d8192 | 28.04 t/s | - | same |\n| tg128 @ d32768 | **24.09 t/s** | - | same |\n\nIndependent confirmation of decode through a different path: `scripts/telemetry.sh`\nduring a live 300-token request computed **29.1 tok/s** from the server's own counters\nand showed the load transient (GPU 97% -> 12%, 175 W -> 46 W, 67 C -> 57 C)\n(`results/telemetry-20260927-171527.csv`).\n\n### Prefill is at the hardware ceiling\n\nThe card sustains 13.0e12 int8 MAC/s of `dp4a` (measured, `tools/microbench/roofline.cu`)\nand PP512 needs 13.75e12 MACs, so the ceiling is 483 t/s. The measurement lands 2% above\nthat, between it and the 510 t/s obtained by scaling the RTX 3070's community result.\nThroughput is flat from 512 to 8192 tokens, so this is throughput-limited, not\noverhead-limited. **There is no prefill tuning left in the `PQ2_0` path.**\n\n### Where decode time goes\n\nPer token at depth 0: **33.8 ms measured against a 24.0 ms weight-streaming floor.**\n\n| Component | Time | Source |\n|---|---:|---|\n| weight streaming (7.19 GiB at 300 GB/s) | 24.0 ms | measured bandwidth |\n| FWHT (257 Hadamard passes) | 0.54 ms | measured, 2.1 us each |\n| KV read at depth 32K | 7.7 ms | measured depth delta |\n| **unaccounted at depth 0** | **~9.3 ms** | remainder |\n\nThe unaccounted part is the one open question. `scripts/profile-nsys.sh` and\n`scripts/profile-ncu.sh` exist to identify it per kernel; neither has been run yet\n(nsys is not installed, and ncu needs root for hardware counters on this machine).\n\n## Measured 12 GB budget\n\nllama.cpp sees 11,904 MiB with the display attached (12,288 MiB total, ~384 MiB for the\ndesktop).\n\n| Component | Size |\n|---|---:|\n| weights (`PQ2_0`) | 6,872 MiB |\n| compute buffers | 736 MiB at ctx 32768, 764 at 65536 |\n| KV (f16) | 64 MiB per 1024 tokens |\n\nObserved totals: **9,656 MiB at ctx 32768** and **11,732 MiB at ctx 65536**, both\nverified to load and serve. That leaves about 556 MiB spare at 64K, putting the f16\ncontext ceiling near **74K tokens**.\n\n## Reproduction\n\n```bash\nscripts/setup.sh all                        # pinned binary + both packs, hash-checked\nscripts/roofline.sh                         # card ceilings\nscripts/bench.sh bin/cuda PQ2_0             # both phases, records machine state\nscripts/parity-check.sh bin/cuda build/llama-sm86/bin PQ2_0   # build equivalence\nscripts/serve.sh start && scripts/telemetry.sh\n```\n\n`scripts/parity-check.sh` passes: the sm_86 source build reproduces the pinned binary\nexactly (perplexity 5.8953 vs 5.8953, identical greedy tokens) on\n`results/parity-20260927-164848/`. Any future kernel change must keep that true.\n\n## Explored and rejected\n\n| Option | Result |\n|---|---|\n| `PTQ1_0` packing | prefill +4%, decode -24%, effective bandwidth 45% vs 71% |\n| 4-bit KV (`q4_0`/`q4_0`) | slower at every depth; -19% at 32K |\n| 4-bit K only (`q4_0`/`f16`) | worst of all: 17.36 t/s at depth 0 |\n| `-ub` 1024/2048, `-b` 4096 | within 1.5% of default, i.e. noise |\n| `-np` 2 | halves per-slot context to 16,384 and raises VRAM |\n| tuning `PQ2_0` prefill | at the dp4a roofline already |\n| CUDA graphs / launch batching | GPU is saturated; ~1967 launches/token are already hidden |\n| `ngram-cache` speculation | 2.19x vs 4.90x for `ngram-simple`, so not worth carrying |\n| speculation while thinking | 0 drafts at every thinking level; inert |\n\n## What would still move the needle\n\n1. **The GEMV's 15.7% gap to the bandwidth roofline** (~4.5 ms/token, 13% of decode). The\n   inner loop is already `dp4a`-based, so this is a bounded, hard target.\n2. **Fusing the normalisation/quantisation plumbing**: `rms_norm_f32` (209 calls),\n   `scale_f32` (48), `quantize_q8_1` (361) and `cpy_scalar` (112) cost ~2.1 ms/token\n   across 730 launches and are *not* bandwidth-saturated, unlike the GEMV. Quantising a\n   shared rotated activation once instead of once per consumer is the concrete version.\n3. **Tensor cores for prefill.** The only route past 483 t/s, and it needs a different\n   weight packing plus mma kernels: a re-architecture, not a tuning pass.\n4. **Beyond ~74K context.** Requires a quantized cache and therefore accepting the\n   measured speed penalty plus calibrating the mean-centering bias.\n\nSpeculative decoding was item 3 on this list before it was measured; it is now the\nconfiguration default and worth 4.9-6.0x, far more than items 1-2 combined\n([ADR 0003](decisions/0003-ngram-speculation.md)).\n\n\nNEEDLE_START\nThe measured read bandwidth of this card is 298 to 307 gigabytes per second, and the\nternary GEMV achieves 84.3 percent of that ceiling. Prefill is bound by the int8 dp4a\nrate, which on this part delivers only 2.2 times the FP32 rate because GA106 has 64\ninteger lanes per SM against 128 FP32 lanes. Sixteen of the sixty four blocks are full\nattention and the remaining forty eight are gated delta net layers, so the KV cache\ngrows by exactly 65536 bytes per token.\nNEEDLE_END<|im_end|>\n<|im_start|>assistant\n<think>\n\n</think>\n\n","has_new_line":true,"truncated":false,"stop_type":"eos","stopping_word":"","tokens_cached":21263,"timings":{"cache_n":0,"prompt_n":21113,"prompt_ms":47720.669,"prompt_per_token_ms":2.2602505091649694,"prompt_per_second":442.4288351866986,"predicted_n":147,"predicted_ms":1442.439,"predicted_per_token_ms":9.879719178082192,"predicted_per_second":101.21745182985207,"draft_n":144,"draft_n_accepted":135}}}

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Measure n-gram speculative decoding against a plain baseline on this machine.
     3	#
     4	# The fork implements draft-model-free speculation (`--spec-type ngram-*`), which is
     5	# the only lever that changes the decode roofline: it verifies several tokens per
     6	# weight pass instead of one. How much it helps depends entirely on how much the
     7	# output overlaps the context, so the prompt file matters more than the flags.
     8	#
     9	# Usage:
    10	#   scripts/specbench.sh <prompt-file> [spec-type...]      # default: none ngram-mod
    11	#
    12	# Env:
    13	#   N=300        tokens to generate
    14	#   REASONING=off|on|auto   thinking mode (off is what makes copy tasks speculative)
    15	#
    16	# Writes one log per configuration under results/ and prints a comparison table.
    17	set -euo pipefail
    18	
    19	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    20	source "$ROOT/config/rt3060.env"
    21	
    22	PROMPT_FILE="${1:?usage: specbench.sh <prompt-file> [spec-type...]}"
    23	shift || true
    24	TYPES=("$@")
    25	[[ ${#TYPES[@]} -eq 0 ]] && TYPES=(none ngram-mod)
    26	
    27	N="${N:-300}"
    28	REASONING="${REASONING:-off}"
    29	MODEL="${MODEL:-$ROOT/models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-${PACK:-PQ2_0}.gguf}"
    30	BIN="${BIN_DIR:-$ROOT/bin/cuda}/llama-cli"
    31	STAMP="$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
    32	TAG="$(basename "$PROMPT_FILE" .txt)"
    33	
    34	mkdir -p "$ROOT/results"
    35	printf '%-14s %8s %8s %7s %7s %6s %6s\n' spec-type gen_t/s speedup runs drafts acc 'accept%' >&2
    36	
    37	BASE=""
    38	for st in "${TYPES[@]}"; do
    39	  LOG="$ROOT/results/spec-${TAG}-${st}-${STAMP}.log"
    40	  "$BIN" -m "$MODEL" -ngl 99 -fa on -st --no-warmup \
    41	    -n "$N" --temp 0 -f "$PROMPT_FILE" --spec-type "$st" -rea "$REASONING" --verbose \
    42	    > "$LOG" 2>&1 || true
    43	
    44	  # llama-cli with --verbose prints a perf block and, when speculation ran, a stats line.
    45	  # The perf line reads: "eval time = X ms / N tokens ( T ms per token, R tokens per second)".
    46	  # "prompt eval time" also contains the substring, so the last match is the generation one.
    47	  perf="$(grep -E "eval time =" "$LOG" | tail -1 || true)"
    48	  gts="$(printf '%s' "$perf" | sed -nE 's/.*, *([0-9.]+) tokens per second.*/\1/p')"
    49	  runs="$(printf '%s' "$perf" | grep -oE '/ *[0-9]+ (runs|tokens)' | grep -oE '[0-9]+' | head -1 || true)"
    50	  drafts=$(grep -oE "#gen drafts = *[0-9]+" "$LOG" | tail -1 | grep -oE "[0-9]+" || true)
    51	  acc=$(grep -oE "#acc drafts = *[0-9]+" "$LOG" | tail -1 | grep -oE "[0-9]+" || true)
    52	
    53	  gts="${gts:-0}"; runs="${runs:-0}"; drafts="${drafts:-0}"; acc="${acc:-0}"
    54	  if [[ "$st" == "none" || -z "$BASE" ]]; then BASE="$gts"; fi
    55	  sp="$(python3 -c "b=$BASE; g=$gts; print(f'{g/b:.2f}x' if b else 'n/a')" 2>/dev/null || echo n/a)"
    56	  pct="$(python3 -c "print(f'{100*$acc/$drafts:.0f}' if $drafts else '-')" 2>/dev/null || echo -)"
    57	  printf '%-14s %8s %8s %7s %7s %6s %6s\n' "$st" "$gts" "$sp" "$runs" "$drafts" "$acc" "$pct" >&2
    58	done
    59	
    60	echo >&2
