/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba scripts/quality-check.py | sed -n '1,270p'" in /home/penta/repos/eobonsai
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Quality check across reasoning modes, with machine-checkable answers.
     3	
     4	The reasoning-mode A/B (scripts/reasoning-ab.sh) measures speed only. Speed alone is not
     5	enough to decide a default: `reasoning_effort: "none"` was observed to cut a mechanical
     6	task from 334 generated tokens to 42, and nothing yet says whether that is concision or a
     7	broken answer. This script settles it by asking tasks whose answers can be scored without a
     8	judge.
     9	
    10	Three task families, each scored mechanically:
    11	
    12	  arithmetic   exact numeric answer, parsed and compared
    13	  format       an instruction with a checkable shape (line count, prefix, exclusions)
    14	  transform    a deterministic string/JSON transformation, compared to a computed key
    15	
    16	Usage:
    17	  scripts/serve.sh start
    18	  scripts/quality-check.py                 # all modes, all tasks
    19	  scripts/quality-check.py none medium     # selected modes
    20	"""
    21	import json
    22	import re
    23	import sys
    24	import time
    25	import urllib.error
    26	import urllib.request
    27	
    28	PORT = 8080
    29	URL = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}/v1/chat/completions"
    30	
    31	# ---------------------------------------------------------------- task definitions
    32	# Each returns (prompt, scorer). The scorer takes the assistant text and returns
    33	# (passed: bool, detail: str).
    34	
    35	
    36	def score_number(expected, extract=r"-?\d[\d,]*\.?\d*"):
    37	    """Compare the LAST number in the reply to the expected value.
    38	
    39	    The last number is the model's final answer; earlier ones may be intermediate steps.
    40	    Replies that bury the answer are caught here rather than silently passing.
    41	    """
    42	    def f(text):
    43	        nums = re.findall(extract, text.replace(",", ""))
    44	        if not nums:
    45	            return False, "no number found"
    46	        got = float(nums[-1])
    47	        return abs(got - expected) < 1e-6, f"expected {expected}, got {got}"
    48	    return f
    49	
    50	
    51	def score_exact_after(marker, expected):
    52	    def f(text):
    53	        idx = text.rfind(marker)
    54	        if idx < 0:
    55	            return False, f"marker {marker!r} not found"
    56	        tail = text[idx + len(marker):].strip().splitlines()
    57	        got = tail[0].strip() if tail else ""
    58	        return got == expected, f"expected {expected!r}, got {got!r}"
    59	    return f
    60	
    61	
    62	def score_shape(min_lines, forbid_words=(), require_lines=None):
    63	    def f(text):
    64	        body = text.strip()
    65	        lines = [l for l in body.splitlines() if l.strip()]
    66	        problems = []
    67	        if len(lines) < min_lines:
    68	            problems.append(f"{len(lines)} non-empty lines < {min_lines}")
    69	        if require_lines is not None and len(lines) != require_lines:
    70	            problems.append(f"{len(lines)} lines != {require_lines}")
    71	        for w in forbid_words:
    72	            if re.search(w, body, re.I):
    73	                problems.append(f"contains forbidden {w!r}")
    74	        return (not problems), ("ok" if not problems else "; ".join(problems))
    75	    return f
    76	
    77	
    78	def score_json_transform(expected_pairs):
    79	    def f(text):
    80	        m = re.search(r"\{.*\}", text, re.S)
    81	        if not m:
    82	            return False, "no JSON object found"
    83	        try:
    84	            got = json.loads(m.group(0))
    85	        except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
    86	            return False, f"invalid JSON: {e}"
    87	        problems = []
    88	        for k, v in expected_pairs.items():
    89	            if k not in got:
    90	                problems.append(f"missing key {k}")
    91	            elif str(got[k]).strip().lower() != str(v).strip().lower():
    92	                problems.append(f"{k}: expected {v}, got {got[k]}")
    93	        return (not problems), ("ok" if not problems else "; ".join(problems))
    94	    return f
    95	
    96	
    97	TASKS = [
    98	    # Arithmetic: the correct answer is a single number, so there is no judging.
    99	    # Verified by hand: weights 7.19 GiB = 7362.56 MiB; free = 11904 - 7362.56 - 760 =
   100	    # 3781.44 MiB; at 64 KiB (0.0625 MiB) per token that is 60503 tokens. Tolerance is
   101	    # generous because the model may round 7.19 GiB differently; scoring is absolute so
   102	    # 60503 and 60500 both pass while a wrong order of magnitude fails.
   103	    (
   104	        "arith_1",
   105	        "A GPU holds a 7.19 GiB model and a KV cache costing 64 KiB per token. "
   106	        "It reports 11904 MiB usable and compute buffers take 760 MiB. "
   107	        "How many tokens of context fit? Answer with the number alone.",
   108	        score_number(60503, r"\d[\d,]*"),
   109	    ),
   110	    (
   111	        "arith_2",
   112	        "Compute 847 * 29 + 1147. Reply with the final number only.",
   113	        score_number(847 * 29 + 1147),
   114	    ),
   115	    # 401*5120*2.13 bits = 4,373,146 bits = 546,643 bytes = 533.8 KiB. Scored in KiB so the
   116	    # answer is a meaningful integer rather than 0.00 in GiB.
   117	    (
   118	        "arith_3",
   119	        "A tensor has 401 rows of 5120 values, stored at 2.13 bits per value. "
   120	        "How many kibibytes is it? Round to the nearest whole number and reply with "
   121	        "the number only.",
   122	        score_number(534, r"\d[\d,]*"),
   123	    ),
   124	    # Format: the instruction has a checkable shape, so compliance is mechanical.
   125	    (
   126	        "format_1",
   127	        "List exactly 5 HTTP status codes, one per line, each line starting with the "
   128	        "three-digit code and a space, and nothing else. No explanations, no headers.",
   129	        score_shape(5, forbid_words=[r"^#"], require_lines=5),
   130	    ),
   131	    (
   132	        "format_2",
   133	        "Reply with exactly three lines. Line 1 is the word ALPHA, line 2 is BETA, "
   134	        "line 3 is GAMMA. Nothing else.",
   135	        score_shape(3, require_lines=3),
   136	    ),
   137	    # Transform: deterministic, comparable to a computed key.
   138	    (
   139	        "transform_1",
   140	        'Return only a JSON object with the keys "total", "mean" and "count" for the '
   141	        "numbers 4, 8, 15, 16, 23, 42. total and count are integers, mean is "
   142	        "rounded to two decimals.",
   143	        lambda t: score_json_transform(
   144	            {"total": 108, "mean": 18.0, "count": 6})(t),
   145	    ),
   146	]
   147	
   148	
   149	def ask(prompt, mode, max_tokens=1200):
   150	    body = {"messages": [{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
   151	            "max_tokens": max_tokens, "temperature": 0}
   152	    if mode != "server-default":
   153	        body["reasoning_effort"] = mode
   154	    req = urllib.request.Request(URL, data=json.dumps(body).encode(),
   155	                                headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
   156	    t0 = time.time()
   157	    d = json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=1800))
   158	    wall = time.time() - t0
   159	    msg = d["choices"][0]["message"]
   160	    return {
   161	        "text": (msg.get("content") or ""),
   162	        "think": len(msg.get("reasoning_content") or ""),
   163	        "wall": wall,
   164	        "tokens": d["timings"]["predicted_n"],
   165	        "drafts": d["timings"].get("draft_n", 0),
   166	    }
   167	
   168	
   169	def main():
   170	    modes = sys.argv[1:] or ["server-default", "none", "medium"]
   171	    rows = []
   172	    for name, prompt, scorer in TASKS:
   173	        for mode in modes:
   174	            try:
   175	                r = ask(prompt, mode)
   176	            except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
   177	                rows.append((name, mode, None, f"HTTP {e.code}", {}, ""))
   178	                continue
   179	            passed, detail = scorer(r["text"])
   180	            rows.append((name, mode, passed, detail, r, r["text"]))
   181	
   182	    print(f"{'task':12s} {'mode':15s} {'pass':5s} {'gen_t/s':>8s} {'wall_s':>7s} "
   183	          f"{'tok':>5s} {'drafts':>7s} {'think':>6s}  detail")
   184	    summary = {}
   185	    for name, mode, passed, detail, r, _ in rows:
   186	        if r:
   187	            gts = r["tokens"] / r["wall"] if r["wall"] else 0
   188	            print(f"{name:12s} {mode:15s} {('PASS' if passed else 'FAIL'):5s} "
   189	                  f"{gts:8.2f} {r['wall']:7.1f} {r['tokens']:5d} {r['drafts']:7d} "
   190	                  f"{r['think']:6d}  {detail[:52]}")
   191	        else:
   192	            print(f"{name:12s} {mode:15s} {'ERR':5s} {'-':>8} {'-':>7} {'-':>5} "
   193	                  f"{'-':>7} {'-':>6}  {detail}")
   194	        summary.setdefault(mode, []).append(1 if passed else 0)
   195	
   196	    print()
   197	    print("accuracy by mode:")
   198	    for mode, res in summary.items():
   199	        print(f"  {mode:15s} {sum(res)}/{len(res)} = {100*sum(res)/len(res):.0f}%")
   200	
   201	
   202	if __name__ == "__main__":
   203	    main()

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Read a GGUF header without loading tensor data.
     3	
     4	The header sits at the start of the file, so this works on a partially
     5	downloaded model. Prints the architecture parameters and, for Bonsai 2, the
     6	Hadamard rotation map: which weights are rotated tells us how many FWHT passes
     7	a forward step performs.
     8	
     9	Usage: python3 tools/gguf-inspect.py models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-PQ2_0.gguf
    10	"""
    11	import struct
    12	import sys
    13	
    14	# GGUF metadata value type ids
    15	U8, I8, U16, I16, U32, I32, F32, BOOL, STR, ARRAY, U64, I64, F64 = range(13)
    16	
    17	FIXED = {
    18	    U8: ("<B", 1), I8: ("<b", 1), U16: ("<H", 2), I16: ("<h", 2),
    19	    U32: ("<I", 4), I32: ("<i", 4), F32: ("<f", 4), BOOL: ("<?", 1),
    20	    U64: ("<Q", 8), I64: ("<q", 8), F64: ("<d", 8),
    21	}
    22	
    23	GGML_TYPE_NAMES = {
    24	    0: "F32", 1: "F16", 2: "Q4_0", 3: "Q4_1", 6: "Q5_0", 7: "Q5_1", 8: "Q8_0",
    25	    9: "Q8_1", 10: "Q2_K", 11: "Q3_K", 12: "Q4_K", 13: "Q5_K", 14: "Q6_K",
    26	    15: "Q8_K", 30: "BF16", 39: "Q1_0", 40: "Q2_0",
    27	    41: "PQ2_0", 42: "PTQ1_0",  # ids are fork-specific; verified against the file below
    28	}
    29	
    30	
    31	class Reader:
    32	    def __init__(self, fh):
    33	        self.fh = fh
    34	
    35	    def raw(self, n):
    36	        b = self.fh.read(n)
    37	        if len(b) != n:
    38	            raise EOFError(f"short read: wanted {n}, got {len(b)}")
    39	        return b
    40	
    41	    def unpack(self, fmt):
    42	        return struct.unpack(fmt, self.raw(struct.calcsize(fmt)))[0]
    43	
    44	    def string(self):
    45	        return self.raw(self.unpack("<Q")).decode("utf-8", "replace")
    46	
    47	    def value(self, vtype):
    48	        if vtype in FIXED:
    49	            return self.unpack(FIXED[vtype][0])
    50	        if vtype == STR:
    51	            return self.string()
    52	        if vtype == ARRAY:
    53	            etype = self.unpack("<I")
    54	            count = self.unpack("<Q")
    55	            # Skip only genuinely huge arrays (tokenizer vocab is ~250k strings).
    56	            if etype == STR and count > 10000:
    57	                for _ in range(count):
    58	                    self.string()
    59	                return [f"<{count} strings skipped>"]
    60	            items = [self.value(etype) for _ in range(count)]
    61	            return items
    62	        raise ValueError(f"unknown value type {vtype}")
    63	
    64	
    65	def main(path):
    66	    with open(path, "rb") as fh:
    67	        r = Reader(fh)
    68	        magic = r.raw(4)
    69	        if magic != b"GGUF":
    70	            sys.exit(f"not a GGUF file (magic={magic!r})")
    71	        version = r.unpack("<I")
    72	        n_tensors = r.unpack("<Q")
    73	        n_kv = r.unpack("<Q")
    74	        print(f"gguf version {version}, {n_tensors} tensors, {n_kv} kv pairs")
    75	
    76	        kv = {}
    77	        for _ in range(n_kv):
    78	            key = r.string()
    79	            kv[key] = r.value(r.unpack("<I"))
    80	
    81	        print("\n--- architecture ---")
    82	        for k in sorted(kv):
    83	            if k.startswith(("qwen35.", "prism.", "general.architecture",
    84	                             "general.size_label", "general.name")):
    85	                v = kv[k]
    86	                if isinstance(v, list) and len(v) > 8:
    87	                    v = f"[{len(v)} items] {v[:8]} ..."
    88	                print(f"{k:44s} = {v}")
    89	
    90	        print("\n--- tensors ---")
    91	        info = []
    92	        for _ in range(n_tensors):
    93	            name = r.string()
    94	            nd = r.unpack("<I")
    95	            ne = [r.unpack("<Q") for _ in range(nd)]
    96	            ttype = r.unpack("<I")
    97	            offset = r.unpack("<Q")
    98	            info.append((name, ne, ttype, offset))
    99	
   100	        from collections import Counter
   101	        print("type histogram:", dict(Counter(GGML_TYPE_NAMES.get(t, t) for _, _, t, _ in info)))
   102	
   103	        print("\nfirst 3 (name, ne, type):")
   104	        for name, ne, ttype, _ in info[:3]:
   105	            print(f"  {name:32s} {ne} {GGML_TYPE_NAMES.get(ttype, ttype)}")
   106	
   107	        # Layer-0 tensor census: one linear layer, one full-attention layer.
   108	        print("\nlayer 0 tensors:")
   109	        for name, ne, ttype, _ in info:
   110	            if name.startswith(("blk.0.", "blk.1.", "blk.2.", "blk.3.")):
   111	                print(f"  {name:34s} {str(ne):26s} {GGML_TYPE_NAMES.get(ttype, ttype)}")
   112	
   113	        rot = kv.get("prism.hadamard.weight_names")
   114	        inv = kv.get("prism.hadamard.inverse_weight_names")
   115	        if rot is not None:
   116	            print("\n--- hadamard ---")
   117	            print(f"block_size          = {kv.get('prism.hadamard.block_size')}")
   118	            print(f"axis                = {kv.get('prism.hadamard.axis')}")
   119	            print(f"sign_mode           = {kv.get('prism.hadamard.sign_mode')}")
   120	            print(f"rotated weights ({len(rot)}): {rot}")
   121	            print(f"inverse weights ({len(inv) if inv else 0}): {inv}")
   122	
   123	        # Count rotated weights per layer, split by layer kind. A linear-attention
   124	        # layer rotates attn_qkv/attn_gate/ssm_out; a full-attention layer rotates
   125	        # attn_q/attn_k/attn_v/attn_output. Both rotate the three FFN weights.
   126	        if rot:
   127	            per_layer = {}
   128	            outside = []
   129	            for name, _, _, _ in info:
   130	                if name not in rot:
   131	                    continue
   132	                if not name.startswith("blk."):
   133	                    outside.append(name)
   134	                    continue
   135	                parts = name.split(".")
   136	                per_layer.setdefault(parts[1], []).append(".".join(parts[2:]))
   137	            kinds = {}
   138	            for idx, names in per_layer.items():
   139	                key = "full_attn" if any(n.startswith("attn_q.") for n in names) else "linear_attn"
   140	                kinds.setdefault(key, {"layers": 0, "weights": sorted(names)})
   141	                kinds[key]["layers"] += 1
   142	            print(f"\nlayers = {len(per_layer)}")
   143	            for key, v in sorted(kinds.items()):
   144	                print(f"  {key:12s} layers={v['layers']:3d} rotated/weight={len(v['weights'])} {v['weights']}")
   145	            print(f"  outside blocks: {outside}")
   146	            total = sum(len(v) for v in per_layer.values()) + len(outside)
   147	            print(f"=> {total} rotated weight tensors per forward pass")
   148	
   149	
   150	if __name__ == "__main__":
   151	    main(sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-PQ2_0.gguf")

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Reasoning-mode comparison against a running server.
     3	#
     4	# Speculative decoding only fires when thinking is off, and the difference measured here is
     5	# ~5x, so the reasoning mode dominates every other performance choice in this project. This
     6	# script measures the trade honestly: the same prompts, several modes, with time, token
     7	# count and draft statistics for each.
     8	#
     9	# Usage:
    10	#   scripts/serve.sh start
    11	#   scripts/reasoning-ab.sh                    # built-in prompt set
    12	#   scripts/reasoning-ab.sh my-prompts.txt     # "=== name ===" delimited blocks
    13	#
    14	# It measures the served path, not llama-cli, because that is what clients use.
    15	set -euo pipefail
    16	
    17	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    18	source "$ROOT/config/rt3060.env"
    19	
    20	PORT="${PORT:-8080}"
    21	HOST="${HOST:-127.0.0.1}"
    22	STAMP="$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
    23	OUT="$ROOT/results/reasoning-ab-$STAMP.txt"
    24	PROMPT_FILE="${1:-}"
    25	mkdir -p "$ROOT/out" "$ROOT/results"
    26	
    27	# Prompts are passed to the driver through files, never through shell variables: a
    28	# multi-line prompt in a variable loses its newlines and silently changes the task.
    29	if [[ -z "$PROMPT_FILE" ]]; then
    30	  PROMPT_FILE="$ROOT/out/reasoning-ab-prompts.txt"
    31	  cat > "$PROMPT_FILE" <<'EOF'
    32	=== mechanical ===
    33	Given the note below, output it verbatim with every occurrence of "The" replaced by "THE".
    34	Output only the modified note, with no commentary.
    35	---
    36	The model runs on a single GPU. The KV cache grows with the context. The prefill phase
    37	is compute bound. The decode phase is bandwidth bound. The measured ceiling is the limit.
    38	===
    39	=== reasoning ===
    40	A 12 GB GPU holds a model whose weights take 7.19 GiB, and the KV cache costs 64 KiB per
    41	token. Compute how many tokens of context fit in the remaining memory if compute buffers
    42	take 760 MiB and the card reports 11904 MiB usable. Show the arithmetic, then state the
    43	answer in one line.
    44	===
    45	EOF
    46	fi
    47	
    48	cat > "$ROOT/out/_reasoning_ask.py" <<'PY'
    49	import json, sys, time, urllib.request, urllib.error
    50	mode, prompt_path, port = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]
    51	prompt = open(prompt_path).read().strip()
    52	body = {"messages": [{"role": "user", "content": prompt}], "max_tokens": 400, "temperature": 0}
    53	if mode != "server-default":
    54	    body["reasoning_effort"] = mode
    55	req = urllib.request.Request(f"http://127.0.0.1:{port}/v1/chat/completions",
    56	                             data=json.dumps(body).encode(),
    57	                             headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    58	t0 = time.time()
    59	try:
    60	    d = json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=1800))
    61	except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
    62	    print(f"{'ERR':>8} {'-':>8} {'-':>6} {'-':>7} {'-':>8} {e.code:>11}")
    63	    raise SystemExit
    64	wall = time.time() - t0
    65	t = d["timings"]
    66	msg = d["choices"][0]["message"]
    67	think = len(msg.get("reasoning_content") or "")
    68	print(f"{t['predicted_per_second']:8.2f} {wall:8.1f} {t['predicted_n']:6d} "
    69	      f"{t.get('draft_n', 0):7d} {t.get('draft_n_accepted', 0):8d} {think:11d}")
    70	PY
    71	
    72	# Emit "name<tab>path" pairs, one file per prompt so newlines survive.
    73	python3 - "$PROMPT_FILE" "$ROOT/out" <<'PY' > "$ROOT/out/_reasoning_prompts.tsv"
    74	import re, sys, pathlib
    75	text, outdir = open(sys.argv[1]).read(), pathlib.Path(sys.argv[2])
    76	blocks = [b.strip() for b in re.split(r"^===.*===\s*$", text, flags=re.M) if b.strip()]
    77	names = re.findall(r"^===\s*(.+?)\s*===\s*$", text, flags=re.M)
    78	for i, body in enumerate(blocks):
    79	    name = names[i] if i < len(names) else f"prompt{i+1}"
    80	    path = outdir / f"_reasoning_prompt_{i}.txt"
    81	    path.write_text(body)
    82	    print(f"{name}\t{path}")
    83	PY
    84	
    85	if [[ ! -s "$ROOT/out/_reasoning_prompts.tsv" ]]; then
    86	  echo "no prompts parsed from $PROMPT_FILE (expected '=== name ===' delimited blocks)" >&2
    87	  exit 1
    88	fi
    89	
    90	{
    91	  echo "# reasoning-mode A/B against the served path"
    92	  echo "# date: $(date -Iseconds)   server: http://$HOST:$PORT"
    93	  echo "# gpu: $(nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,memory.used,clocks.max.sm --format=csv,noheader)"
    94	  echo "# loadavg: $(cat /proc/loadavg)"
    95	  echo "# prompt file: ${PROMPT_FILE#$ROOT/}"
    96	  echo
    97	  printf '%-12s %-15s %8s %8s %6s %7s %8s %11s\n' \
    98	         prompt mode gen_t/s wall_s tokens drafts accepted think_chars
    99	} | tee "$OUT"
   100	
   101	while IFS=$'\t' read -r name path; do
   102	  for mode in server-default none low medium; do
   103	    row="$(python3 "$ROOT/out/_reasoning_ask.py" "$mode" "$path" "$PORT")"
   104	    printf '%-12s %-15s %s\n' "$name" "$mode" "$row" | tee -a "$OUT"
   105	  done
   106	  echo | tee -a "$OUT"
   107	done < "$ROOT/out/_reasoning_prompts.tsv"
   108	
   109	cat | tee -a "$OUT" <<'EOF'
   110	# Reading this table:
   111	#   think_chars > 0 -> the model reasoned, drafts are 0, so speculation was inert
   112	#   drafts > 0      -> speculation fired; that is where the ~5x comes from
   113	#   server-default  -> whatever config/rt3060.env passes as the chat-template default
   114	#   none is the only mode measured to disable thinking; low and medium still think
   115	EOF
   116	
   117	echo
   118	echo "saved: $OUT"

import importlib.util, collections
sp=importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('gi','tools/gguf-inspect.py'); m=importlib.util.module_from_spec(sp); sp.loader.exec_module(m)
p='models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-PQ2_0.gguf'
with open(p,'rb') as f:
 r=m.Reader(f); r.raw(4); r.unpack('<I'); nt=r.unpack('<Q'); nk=r.unpack('<Q')
 for _ in range(nk): r.string(); r.value(r.unpack('<I'))
 q=collections.defaultdict(lambda:[0,0]); allsz=0
 for _ in range(nt):
  name=r.string(); nd=r.unpack('<I'); ne=[r.unpack('<Q') for i in range(nd)]; typ=r.unpack('<I'); off=r.unpack('<Q')
  if typ==142:
   s=ne[0]*ne[1]*34//128
   q[tuple(ne)][0]+=1; q[tuple(ne)][1]+=s; allsz+=s
 for dims,(cnt,b) in sorted(q.items(),key=lambda kv:-kv[1][1]): print(dims,cnt,round(b/1e9,3),'GB',round(100*b/allsz,1),'%')
 print('PQ total',allsz/1e9, 'GB')
PY" in /home/penta/repos/eobonsai

/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba scripts/build.sh | sed -n '1,300p'" in /home/penta/repos/eobonsai
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Build the PrismML llama.cpp fork for this machine (RTX 3060, sm_86, CUDA 12.4).
     3	#
     4	# A source build is required for two reasons:
     5	#   1. kernel work needs a tree we can modify,
     6	#   2. the baseline for A/B must come from the same toolchain as the optimized build.
     7	#
     8	# The fork tree is pinned to the commit that the pinned release binary was built from,
     9	# so source and prebuilt results are comparable.
    10	set -euo pipefail
    11	
    12	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    13	SRC="$ROOT/third_party/llama.cpp"
    14	BUILD="$ROOT/build/llama-sm86"
    15	CUDA_ROOT="${CUDA_ROOT:-/usr/local/cuda-12.4}"
    16	FORK_COMMIT="adfffbe41b2cabcd51fff326ab045662265062bb"
    17	
    18	log() { printf '[build] %s\n' "$*" >&2; }
    19	
    20	[[ -d "$SRC/.git" ]] || { log "fork missing; run scripts/setup.sh then clone"; exit 1; }
    21	
    22	if [[ -d "$SRC/.git" ]] && ! git -C "$SRC" cat-file -e "$FORK_COMMIT^{commit}" 2>/dev/null; then
    23	  log "fetching pinned commit $FORK_COMMIT"
    24	  git -C "$SRC" fetch --depth 1 origin "$FORK_COMMIT"
    25	fi
    26	current="$(git -C "$SRC" rev-parse HEAD)"
    27	if [[ "$current" != "$FORK_COMMIT" ]]; then
    28	  log "checking out $FORK_COMMIT (was ${current:0:9})"
    29	  git -C "$SRC" checkout --detach "$FORK_COMMIT"
    30	fi
    31	
    32	# Apply the local patch overlay. The checkout stays disposable: patches/ is the source
    33	# of truth, so a fresh clone plus this loop reproduces the tuned build exactly.
    34	for p in "$ROOT"/patches/*.patch; do
    35	  [[ -e "$p" ]] || continue
    36	  if git -C "$SRC" apply --reverse --check "$p" 2>/dev/null; then
    37	    log "patch already applied: $(basename "$p")"
    38	  elif git -C "$SRC" apply "$p"; then
    39	    log "applied patch: $(basename "$p")"
    40	  else
    41	    log "ERROR: cannot apply $(basename "$p") - the pinned commit may have changed"
    42	    exit 1
    43	  fi
    44	done
    45	
    46	log "CUDA: $CUDA_ROOT"
    47	log "configure -> $BUILD"
    48	cmake -S "$SRC" -B "$BUILD" \
    49	  -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release \
    50	  -DGGML_CUDA=ON \
    51	  -DGGML_NATIVE=ON \
    52	  -DCMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=86 \
    53	  -DCMAKE_CUDA_COMPILER="$CUDA_ROOT/bin/nvcc" \
    54	  -DCUDAToolkit_ROOT="$CUDA_ROOT" \
    55	  -DLLAMA_CURL=OFF \
    56	  -DGGML_CUDA_FA_ALL_QUANTS=ON \
    57	  -DCMAKE_EXPORT_COMPILE_COMMANDS=ON
    58	
    59	log "building (this takes a while)"
    60	# llama-perplexity is part of the verification gate (scripts/parity-check.sh), so it is
    61	# built alongside the benchmark and serving binaries.
    62	cmake --build "$BUILD" --target llama-bench llama-cli llama-server llama-perplexity -j"$(nproc)"
    63	
    64	log "binaries: $BUILD/bin"
    65	ls -1 "$BUILD/bin" | head -20
    66	log "done"

/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba scripts/roofline.sh | sed -n '1,270p'" in /home/penta/repos/eobonsai
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Build and run the roofline microbenchmarks, saving raw output under results/.
     3	#
     4	# These are the measured denominators for every later claim about the model:
     5	# read bandwidth, launch overhead, dp4a peak, CUDA-core FMA, and FWHT cost.
     6	set -euo pipefail
     7	
     8	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
     9	CUDA_ROOT="${CUDA_ROOT:-/usr/local/cuda-12.4}"
    10	ARCH="${ARCH:-sm_86}"
    11	OUT="$ROOT/results/roofline-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S).txt"
    12	
    13	mkdir -p "$ROOT/out" "$ROOT/results"
    14	"$CUDA_ROOT/bin/nvcc" -O3 -arch="$ARCH" -o "$ROOT/out/roofline" "$ROOT/tools/microbench/roofline.cu"
    15	
    16	{
    17	  echo "### roofline microbenchmarks"
    18	  echo "date     : $(date -Iseconds)"
    19	  echo "arch     : $ARCH   cuda: $("$CUDA_ROOT/bin/nvcc" --version | tail -2 | head -1)"
    20	  echo "gpu      : $(nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,driver_version,clocks.max.sm,clocks.max.mem,memory.total --format=csv,noheader)"
    21	  echo
    22	  "$ROOT/out/roofline"
    23	} | tee "$OUT"
    24	
    25	echo
    26	echo "saved: $OUT"

/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba scripts/spec-summary.py | sed -n '1,290p'" in /home/penta/repos/eobonsai
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Extract a committable summary from the verbose speculative-decoding run logs.
     3	
     4	The verbose llama-cli logs are large (about 1 MB each) and are not tracked; this pulls
     5	out the numbers that docs and ADRs cite, into one small table.
     6	
     7	Usage: python3 scripts/spec-summary.py [results-dir]
     8	"""
     9	import csv
    10	import glob
    11	import os
    12	import re
    13	import sys
    14	
    15	SPEC = re.compile(r"(none|ngram-[a-z0-9-]+)")
    16	EVAL = re.compile(
    17	    r"eval time = *([0-9.]+) ms / *([0-9]+) tokens \( *[0-9.]+ ms per token, *([0-9.]+) tokens per second\)"
    18	)
    19	PROMPT = re.compile(r"prompt eval time = *[0-9.]+ ms / *([0-9]+) tokens")
    20	
    21	
    22	def parse(path):
    23	    text = open(path, errors="replace").read()
    24	    evals = EVAL.findall(text)
    25	    if not evals:
    26	        return None
    27	    _, gen_tokens, gen_tps = evals[-1]
    28	    prompts = PROMPT.findall(text)
    29	    draft = re.search(r"#gen drafts = *([0-9]+)", text)
    30	    acc = re.search(r"#acc drafts = *([0-9]+)", text)
    31	    drafts = int(draft.group(1)) if draft else 0
    32	    accepted = int(acc.group(1)) if acc else 0
    33	
    34	    stem = os.path.basename(path).removeprefix("spec-").removesuffix(".log")
    35	    stem = re.sub(r"-\d{8}-\d{6}$", "", stem)          # drop the timestamp
    36	    specs = SPEC.findall(stem)
    37	    spec = specs[-1] if specs else "?"
    38	    workload = stem[: stem.rfind(spec)].rstrip("-") if spec in stem else stem
    39	    # llama-cli marks the thinking trace, so its presence tells us which mode ran.
    40	    thinking = "on" if ("[Start thinking]" in text or "Reasoning effort is set to" in text) else "off"
    41	    return {
    42	        "workload": workload or "?",
    43	        "spec_type": spec,
    44	        "thinking": thinking,
    45	        "prompt_tokens": int(prompts[-1]) if prompts else 0,
    46	        "gen_tokens": int(gen_tokens),
    47	        "gen_t_per_s": float(gen_tps),
    48	        "drafts": drafts,
    49	        "accepted": accepted,
    50	        "accept_pct": f"{100 * accepted / drafts:.0f}%" if drafts else "-",
    51	    }
    52	
    53	
    54	def main():
    55	    root = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "results"
    56	    rows = [r for r in (parse(p) for p in sorted(glob.glob(f"{root}/spec-*.log"))) if r]
    57	    out = f"{root}/spec-summary-20260927.txt"
    58	    with open(out, "w", newline="") as fh:
    59	        fh.write("# n-gram speculative decoding, extracted (llama-cli, temp 0, REASONING=off)\n")
    60	        fh.write("# source: verbose logs from scripts/specbench.sh and the depth runs.\n")
    61	        fh.write("# Those logs are large and regenerable, so they are not tracked; this table is.\n")
    62	        w = csv.DictWriter(fh, fieldnames=list(rows[0].keys()))
    63	        w.writeheader()
    64	        for r in sorted(rows, key=lambda r: (r["workload"], r["spec_type"])):
    65	            w.writerow(r)
    66	    print(open(out).read())
    67	
    68	
    69	if __name__ == "__main__":
    70	    main()

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# Session Handoff Protocol
     2	
     3	Protocol for handing off long-running work between Claude sessions via GitHub issues.
     4	
     5	## Premises
     6	
     7	- **Scope**: long-running workstreams that span multiple sessions and possibly multiple interfaces (claude.ai ↔ Claude Code).
     8	- **Medium**: GitHub issue with `session-handoff` label.
     9	- **Granularity**: one issue per workstream. Not one per session.
    10	- **Avoid destructive history loss**: the issue body holds current state and is overwritten on each session. History accumulates in a pinned comment titled "Session log", append-only.
    11	
    12	## Sender procedure (end of session)
    13	
    14	1. Commit and push outstanding work. Make the working tree clean, or record the dirty state explicitly.
    15	2. Overwrite the issue body using `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/handoff.md` format.
    16	   - **Snapshot**: branch + latest commit SHA + timestamp.
    17	   - **Next action**: granular enough that the next session can begin immediately.
    18	   - **Failed approaches**: record anything tried and abandoned. Skipping this is the most common cause of duplicated effort.
    19	3. Append this session's outcome to the pinned "Session log" comment as a single dated paragraph.
    20	4. Reference the issue number in the final message.
    21	
    22	## Receiver procedure (start of session)
    23	
    24	1. List open issues labelled `session-handoff` and identify the relevant workstream.
    25	2. Read the issue body. Internalise Snapshot, Next action, Failed approaches.
    26	3. Verify the Snapshot matches reality.
    27	   - Compare `git log -1 origin/<branch>` to the recorded commit SHA.
    28	   - Check working tree state if claimed clean.
    29	4. If drift exists, report to the user before acting (e.g., "issue records `<sha-A>`, but origin is now `<sha-B>` with these changes: ...") and request guidance.
    30	5. If no drift, read the **Next action** aloud to the user and confirm before executing.
    31	6. Execute. Verify against the **Verification** field. Then follow the sender procedure to update the issue.
    32	
    33	## Promotion rule
    34	
    35	When a Decision in the issue is referenced in 2+ later sessions, promote it to an ADR under `docs/decisions/*.md`. Replace the entry in the issue body with a link to the ADR. This keeps the issue body bounded and prevents knowledge from being lost when the issue is closed.
    36	
    37	## SSOT precedence
    38	
    39	When sources conflict:
    40	
    41	1. `CLAUDE.md` (project-wide invariants)
    42	2. `.claude/rules/` (path-scoped)
    43	3. ADR in `docs/decisions/` (settled judgements)
    44	4. Handoff issue body (current workstream state)
    45	
    46	The handoff issue is the most volatile. If it conflicts with `CLAUDE.md`, the latter wins.
    47	
    48	## Anti-patterns
    49	
    50	- Treating the body as a chat log. Use comments — especially the pinned "Session log" — for history.
    51	- Inlining large logs or code dumps. Use file path + SHA + line-range pointers instead.
    52	- Ending a session with no concrete Next action.
    53	- Updating the title without updating the body.
    54	- Closing the issue without migrating Decisions to an ADR — knowledge is lost when the closed issue is no longer surfaced.
    55	- Concurrent edits from claude.ai and Claude Code on the same issue. If both interfaces are active, designate one as writer and the other as reader.
    56	
    57	## Parallel workstreams
    58	
    59	Multiple concurrent issues are fine. Express dependencies via GitHub issue refs (`#NN`). If the dependency graph becomes complex, evaluate dedicated trackers (e.g., `gastownhall/beads`) — but for solo-plus-AI workflows, GitHub issues at this granularity are usually sufficient.

static bool test_prompt(llama_context * ctx, int n_prompt, int n_batch, int n_threads) {
    llama_set_n_threads(ctx, n_threads, n_threads);

    const llama_model * model   = llama_get_model(ctx);
    const llama_vocab * vocab   = llama_model_get_vocab(model);
    const int32_t       n_vocab = llama_vocab_n_tokens(vocab);

    std::vector<llama_token> tokens(n_batch);

    int n_processed = 0;

    while (n_processed < n_prompt) {
        int n_tokens = std::min(n_prompt - n_processed, n_batch);
        tokens[0]    = n_processed == 0 && llama_vocab_get_add_bos(vocab) ? llama_vocab_bos(vocab) : std::rand() % n_vocab;
        for (int i = 1; i < n_tokens; i++) {
            tokens[i] = std::rand() % n_vocab;
        }
        int res = llama_decode(ctx, llama_batch_get_one(tokens.data(), n_tokens));
        if (res != 0) {
            fprintf(stderr, "%s: failed to decode prompt batch, res = %d\n", __func__, res);
            return false;
        }
        n_processed += n_tokens;
    }

    llama_synchronize(ctx);
    return true;
}

    uint32_t n_sampling_nodes_max = 0;
    for (const auto & [seq_id, sampler] : sampling.samplers) {
        const uint32_t n_nodes = llama_sampler_backend_n_nodes(sampler);
        n_sampling_nodes += n_nodes;
        if (cparams.n_outputs_max_per_seq > 1) {
            n_sampling_nodes_max = std::max(n_sampling_nodes_max, n_nodes);
        }
    }

    const uint32_t n_sampling_outputs_max = std::min<uint64_t>(
            std::min(n_tokens, cparams.n_outputs_max),
            (uint64_t) cparams.n_seq_max * cparams.n_outputs_max_per_seq);

    res += n_sampling_nodes;
    if (n_sampling_outputs_max > 1) {
        res += (n_sampling_outputs_max - 1) * n_sampling_nodes_max;
    }
    return res;
}

llm_graph_result * llama_context::get_gf_res_reserve() const {
    return static_cast<llm_graph_result *>(gf_res_reserve.get());
}

// pack sampler outputs into as few sequences as possible before using sequences without samplers
static void ubatch_prepare_reserve(
              llama_ubatch                            & ubatch,
              uint32_t                                  n_outputs,
        const std::map<llama_seq_id, llama_sampler *> & samplers,
              uint32_t                                  n_outputs_max_per_seq) {
    const uint32_t n_seqs       = ubatch.n_seqs;
    const uint32_t n_seq_tokens = ubatch.n_seq_tokens;

    for (uint32_t s = 0; s < n_seqs; ++s) {
        for (uint32_t t = 0; t < n_seq_tokens; ++t) {
            const uint32_t i = s * n_seq_tokens + t;
            ubatch.n_seq_id[i] = 1;
            ubatch.seq_id[i] = &ubatch.seq_id_unq[s];
        }
    }

    // sequences with a sampler that fit in this ubatch
    std::vector<uint32_t> sampler_seqs;
    std::vector<bool> has_sampler(n_seqs, false);
    for (const auto & entry : samplers) {
        const llama_seq_id seq_id = entry.first;
        if (seq_id < 0 || (uint32_t) seq_id >= n_seqs) {
            continue;
        }

        sampler_seqs.push_back(seq_id);
        has_sampler[seq_id] = true;
    }

    uint32_t n_outputs_set = 0;

    const uint32_t n_outputs_per_seq = std::min(n_seq_tokens, n_outputs_max_per_seq);
    for (uint32_t s : sampler_seqs) {
        if (n_outputs_set >= n_outputs) {
            break;
        }

        for (uint32_t t = 0; t < n_outputs_per_seq && n_outputs_set < n_outputs; ++t) {
            ubatch.output[s * n_seq_tokens + t] = true;
            ++n_outputs_set;
        }
    }

    // use sequences without samplers for any remaining outputs
    for (uint32_t t = 0; t < n_seq_tokens && n_outputs_set < n_outputs; ++t) {
        for (uint32_t s = 0; s < n_seqs && n_outputs_set < n_outputs; ++s) {
            if (has_sampler[s]) {
                continue;
            }

            ubatch.output[s * n_seq_tokens + t] = true;
            ++n_outputs_set;
        }
    }
}

        uint32_t n_tokens, uint32_t n_seqs, uint32_t n_outputs, const llama_memory_context_i * mctx, bool split_only, size_t * sizes) {
    LLAMA_LOG_DEBUG("%s: reserving a graph for ubatch with n_tokens = %4u, n_seqs = %2u, n_outputs = %4u\n", __func__, n_tokens, n_seqs, n_outputs);
    GGML_ASSERT(n_outputs >= 1);

    if (n_tokens % n_seqs != 0) {
        n_tokens = ((n_tokens + (n_seqs - 1)) / n_seqs) * n_seqs; // round to next multiple of n_seqs
        LLAMA_LOG_DEBUG("%s: making n_tokens a multiple of n_seqs - n_tokens = %u, n_seqs = %u, n_outputs = %u\n", __func__, n_tokens, n_seqs, n_outputs);

//
// interface implementation
//

struct llama_batch llama_batch_get_one(
             llama_token * tokens,
                 int32_t   n_tokens) {
    return {
        /*n_tokens =*/ n_tokens,
        /*tokens   =*/ tokens,
        /*embd     =*/ nullptr,
        /*pos      =*/ nullptr,
        /*n_seq_id =*/ nullptr,
        /*seq_id   =*/ nullptr,
        /*logits   =*/ nullptr,
    };
}

struct llama_batch llama_batch_init(int32_t n_tokens_alloc, int32_t embd, int32_t n_seq_max) {
    llama_batch batch = {
        /*n_tokens =*/ 0,
        /*tokens   =*/ nullptr,
        /*embd     =*/ nullptr,
        /*pos      =*/ nullptr,
        /*n_seq_id =*/ nullptr,
        /*seq_id   =*/ nullptr,
        /*logits   =*/ nullptr,
    };

    if (embd) {
        batch.embd = (float *) malloc(sizeof(float) * n_tokens_alloc * embd);
    } else {
        batch.token = (llama_token *) malloc(sizeof(llama_token) * n_tokens_alloc);
    }

    batch.pos      = (llama_pos *)     malloc(sizeof(llama_pos)      * n_tokens_alloc);
    batch.n_seq_id = (int32_t *)       malloc(sizeof(int32_t)        * n_tokens_alloc);
    batch.seq_id   = (llama_seq_id **) malloc(sizeof(llama_seq_id *) * (n_tokens_alloc + 1));
    for (int i = 0; i < n_tokens_alloc; ++i) {

exec
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '243,297p' third_party/llama.cpp/src/models/qwen35.cpp" in /home/penta/repos/eobonsai
 succeeded in 0ms:
            if (cparams.capture_layer_idx[c] == il) {
                ggml_tensor * cap = cur;
                if (cparams.embeddings_nextn_masked && inp_out_ids) {
                    cap = ggml_get_rows(ctx0, cap, inp_out_ids);
                }
                cb(cap, "h_capture", il);
                h_capture[c] = cap;
            }
        }
    }
    cur = inpL;
    // multi-layer hidden-state tap: concatenate captured layers along dim0 into a
    // single [n_capture * n_embd, n_outputs] tensor for one bulk host copy.
    if (cparams.n_capture_layers > 0) {
        ggml_tensor * cap = h_capture[0];
        GGML_ASSERT(cap && "capture layer 0 was not produced (index out of executed range?)");
        for (uint32_t c = 1; c < cparams.n_capture_layers; ++c) {
            GGML_ASSERT(h_capture[c] && "a requested capture layer was not produced");
            cap = ggml_concat(ctx0, cap, h_capture[c], 0);
        }
        cb(cap, "h_capture_cat", -1);
        res->t_h_capture = cap;

        // The capture concat chain is a side-branch off the per-layer outputs,
        // not reachable by traversing backward from the logits tensor expanded
        // below -- without this it's built but never added to gf, so the
        // scheduler never visits or backend-assigns it (ggml_set_output() alone
        // marks intent, it doesn't add the node to the graph).
        ggml_build_forward_expand(gf, cap);
    }

    cur = build_norm(cur, model.output_norm, nullptr, LLM_NORM_RMS, -1);

    cb(cur, "h_nextn", -1);
    res->t_h_nextn = cur;

    if ((!cparams.embeddings_nextn_masked || cparams.n_capture_layers > 0) && inp_out_ids) {
        cur = ggml_get_rows(ctx0, cur, inp_out_ids);
    }

    cb(cur, "result_norm", -1);
    res->t_embd = cur;

    // LM head
    cur = build_lora_mm(model.output, cur, model.output_s);

    cb(cur, "result_output", -1);
    res->t_logits = cur;

    ggml_build_forward_expand(gf, cur);
}

            pos[i] = p0[seq_id];

            // update the starting position for all sequences that are assigned to the this token

                const llama_seq_id seq_id = batch.seq_id[i][s];

                p0[seq_id] = pos[i] + 1;
            }
        }

        batch.pos = pos.data();
    }

    if (!batch.logits) {
        if (output_all) {
            // return the output for all tokens
            output.resize(batch.n_tokens, true);
        } else {
            // return the output only for the last token
            output.resize(batch.n_tokens, false);
            output[output.size() - 1] = true;
        }

        bool warn = false;

        for (int32_t i = 0; i < batch.n_tokens; ++i) {
            if (batch.logits[i] == 0) {
                warn = true;
            }
        }

// Best FlashAttention kernel for a specific GPU:
enum best_fattn_kernel {
    BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE    =   0,
    BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_TILE    = 200,
    BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_VEC     = 100,
    BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_MMA_F16 = 400,
};

static bool ggml_cuda_fattn_kv_type_supported(ggml_type type) {
    switch (type) {
        case GGML_TYPE_F32:
        case GGML_TYPE_F16:
            return true;
        case GGML_TYPE_Q4_1:
        case GGML_TYPE_Q5_0:
        case GGML_TYPE_Q5_1:
#ifndef GGML_CUDA_FA_ALL_QUANTS
            return false;
#endif // GGML_CUDA_FA_ALL_QUANTS
        case GGML_TYPE_Q4_0:
        case GGML_TYPE_Q8_0:
        case GGML_TYPE_BF16:
            return true;
        default:
            return false;
    }
}

static best_fattn_kernel ggml_cuda_get_best_fattn_kernel(const int device, const ggml_tensor * dst) {
#ifndef FLASH_ATTN_AVAILABLE
    GGML_UNUSED(device); GGML_UNUSED(dst);
    return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
#endif// FLASH_ATTN_AVAILABLE

    const ggml_tensor * KQV   = dst;
    const ggml_tensor * Q     = dst->src[0];
    const ggml_tensor * K     = dst->src[1];
    const ggml_tensor * V     = dst->src[2];
    const ggml_tensor * mask  = dst->src[3];

    const int gqa_ratio = Q->ne[2] / K->ne[2];
    GGML_ASSERT(Q->ne[2] % K->ne[2] == 0);

    float max_bias = 0.0f;
    memcpy(&max_bias, (const float *) KQV->op_params + 1, sizeof(float));

    // The effective batch size for the kernel can be increased by gqa_ratio.
    // The kernel versions without this optimization are also used for ALiBi, if there is no mask, or if the KV cache is not padded,
    bool gqa_opt_applies = gqa_ratio >= 2 && mask && max_bias == 0.0f && K->ne[1] % FATTN_KQ_STRIDE == 0;
    for (const ggml_tensor * t : {Q, K, V, mask}) {
        if (t == nullptr || ggml_is_quantized(t->type)) {
            continue;
        }
        for (size_t i = 1; i < GGML_MAX_DIMS; ++i) {
            if (t->nb[i] % 16 != 0) {
                gqa_opt_applies = false;
                break;
            }
        }
    }

    const int cc = ggml_cuda_info().devices[device].cc;

    switch (K->ne[0]) {
        case  40:
        case  64:
        case  72:
        case  80:
        case  96:
        case 128:
        case 112:
        case 256:
            if (V->ne[0] != K->ne[0]) {
                return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
            }
            break;
        case 192:
            if (V->ne[0] != 128 || !gqa_opt_applies) {
                return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
            }
            if (gqa_ratio % 8 != 0) {
                return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
            }
            break;
        case 320:
            if (V->ne[0] != 256 || !gqa_opt_applies) {
                return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
            }
            if (gqa_ratio % 32 != 0) {
                return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
            }
            break;
        case 512:
            if (V->ne[0] != K->ne[0]) {
                return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
            }
            if (!gqa_opt_applies) {
                return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
            }
            break;
        case 576:
            if (V->ne[0] != 512) {
                return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
            }
            if (!gqa_opt_applies) {
                return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
            }
            break;
        default:
            return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
    }

#ifndef GGML_CUDA_FA_ALL_QUANTS
    if (K->type != V->type) {
        return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
    }
#endif // GGML_CUDA_FA_ALL_QUANTS

    if (!ggml_cuda_fattn_kv_type_supported(K->type) || !ggml_cuda_fattn_kv_type_supported(V->type)) {
        return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
    }

    if (mask && mask->ne[2] != 1) {
        return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_NONE;
    }

    // For small batch sizes the vector kernel may be preferable over the kernels optimized for large batch sizes:
    // 192 satisfies % 64 == 0 but has no vec instance (DKQ != DV); force it onto the MMA path.
    const bool can_use_vector_kernel = Q->ne[0] <= 256 && Q->ne[0] % 64 == 0 && Q->ne[0] != 192 && K->ne[1] % FATTN_KQ_STRIDE == 0;

    // If Turing tensor cores are available, use them:
    if (turing_mma_available(cc) && Q->ne[0] != 40 && Q->ne[0] != 72) {
        if (can_use_vector_kernel) {
            if (!ggml_is_quantized(K->type) && !ggml_is_quantized(V->type)) {
                if (cc >= GGML_CUDA_CC_ADA_LOVELACE && Q->ne[1] == 1 && Q->ne[3] == 1 && !(gqa_ratio > 4 && K->ne[1] >= 8192)) {
                    return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_VEC;

exec
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '465,535p' third_party/llama.cpp/ggml/src/ggml-cuda/fattn.cu" in /home/penta/repos/eobonsai
 succeeded in 0ms:
                    return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_VEC;
                }
            } else {
                if (cc >= GGML_CUDA_CC_ADA_LOVELACE) {
                    if (Q->ne[1] <= 2) {
                        return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_VEC;
                    }
                } else {
                    if (Q->ne[1] == 1) {
                        return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_VEC;
                    }
                }
            }
            if (!gqa_opt_applies && Q->ne[1] == 1) {
                return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_VEC;
            }
        }
        return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_MMA_F16;
    }

    const int ncols2_max = Q->ne[0] == 320 ? 32 : ((Q->ne[0] == 576 || Q->ne[0] == 192) ? 16 : 8);
    int gqa_ratio_eff = 1;
    while (gqa_ratio % (2*gqa_ratio_eff) == 0 && gqa_ratio_eff < ncols2_max) {
        gqa_ratio_eff *= 2;
    }

    if (volta_mma_available(cc) && Q->ne[0] != 40 && Q->ne[0] != 72) {
        if (can_use_vector_kernel && Q->ne[1] * gqa_ratio_eff <= 2) {
            return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_VEC;
        }
        if (Q->ne[1] * gqa_ratio_eff <= 16) {
            return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_TILE; // On Volta tensor cores are only faster for sufficiently large matrices.
        }
        return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_MMA_F16;
    }

    // AMD MFMA needs a certain minimum batch size to outscale the tile kernel for large head sizes.
    if ((amd_mfma_available(cc) && Q->ne[0] <= 256) && Q->ne[0] != 40 && Q->ne[0] != 72) {
        if ((Q->ne[0] <= 64 && Q->ne[1] * gqa_ratio_eff > 8)) {
            return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_MMA_F16;
        }
        if ((Q->ne[0] <= 128 && Q->ne[1] * gqa_ratio_eff > 16)) {
            return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_MMA_F16;
        }
        if ((Q->ne[0] <= 256 && Q->ne[1] * gqa_ratio_eff > 64)) {
            return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_MMA_F16;
        }
    }

    // AMD WMMA is always faster than the tile kernel if the full tile width of 16 can be utilized.
    if ((amd_wmma_available(cc) && gqa_opt_applies && Q->ne[0] <= 128) && Q->ne[0] != 40 && Q->ne[0] != 72 && Q->ne[1] * gqa_ratio_eff > 8) {
        return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_MMA_F16;
    }

    // If there are no tensor cores available, use the generic tile kernel:
    if (can_use_vector_kernel) {
        if (!ggml_is_quantized(K->type) && !ggml_is_quantized(V->type)) {
            if (Q->ne[1] == 1) {
                if (!gqa_opt_applies) {
                    return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_VEC;
                }
            }
        } else {
            if (Q->ne[1] <= 2) {
                return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_VEC;
            }
        }
    }
    return BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_TILE;
}


codex
The prefill ceiling also needs correction: the roofline calculation charges every one of the model’s 26.9B parameters to every prompt token. The local GGUF and fork code show the embedding is a lookup and the output head is computed only for requested output rows. That raises the simple `dp4a` ceiling from about 483 to about 534 t/s. I’m checking what this changes in the final verdict; it is an upper bound, not a measured tuning gain.

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Correctness gate: two builds must produce the same numbers on this model.
     3	#
     4	# Any kernel change must pass this before its speedup counts. A faster kernel that
     5	# changes the output is worthless, and on a rotated-basis ternary model a broken
     6	# transform can still produce fluent-looking text, so text comparison alone is not
     7	# enough.
     8	#
     9	# Two checks:
    10	#   1. perplexity on a fixed tracked corpus (sensitive to numerics)
    11	#   2. greedy generation with a fixed seed (catches token-level divergence)
    12	#
    13	# Usage:
    14	#   scripts/parity-check.sh bin/cuda build/llama-sm86/bin
    15	#   scripts/parity-check.sh bin/cuda build/llama-sm86/bin PTQ1_0
    16	#
    17	# Exit status: 0 when both checks agree within tolerance, 1 otherwise.
    18	set -euo pipefail
    19	
    20	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    21	A_DIR="${1:?usage: parity-check.sh <bin-dir-a> <bin-dir-b> [PQ2_0|PTQ1_0]}"
    22	B_DIR="${2:?usage: parity-check.sh <bin-dir-a> <bin-dir-b> [PQ2_0|PTQ1_0]}"
    23	PACK="${3:-PQ2_0}"
    24	
    25	MODEL="$ROOT/models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-${PACK}.gguf"
    26	# A tracked file, so the corpus cannot drift out of the repository.
    27	CORPUS="$ROOT/docs/roofline-rtx3060.md"
    28	COMMON=(-m "$MODEL" -ngl 99 -fa on -c 512)
    29	PROMPT="The capital of France is"
    30	OUTDIR="$ROOT/results/parity-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
    31	
    32	[[ -f "$MODEL" ]]  || { echo "model not found: $MODEL" >&2; exit 1; }
    33	[[ -f "$CORPUS" ]] || { echo "corpus not found: $CORPUS" >&2; exit 1; }
    34	for tool in llama-perplexity llama-cli; do
    35	  for dir in "$A_DIR" "$B_DIR"; do
    36	    [[ -x "$ROOT/$dir/$tool" ]] || { echo "missing $ROOT/$dir/$tool" >&2; exit 1; }
    37	  done
    38	done
    39	mkdir -p "$OUTDIR"
    40	
    41	# stdin is closed for every invocation: llama-cli's single-turn mode still reads stdin,
    42	# and a script run without a terminal would otherwise block there forever.
    43	ppl() { # ppl <bin-dir> <tag>
    44	  local dir="$1" tag="$2"
    45	  "$ROOT/$dir/llama-perplexity" "${COMMON[@]}" -f "$CORPUS" < /dev/null 2>&1 |
    46	    tee "$OUTDIR/ppl-$tag.log" |
    47	    grep -E "Final estimate" | tail -1 |
    48	    sed -E 's/.*PPL = *([0-9.]+).*/\1/'
    49	}
    50	
    51	greedy() { # greedy <bin-dir> <tag>
    52	  local dir="$1" tag="$2"
    53	  # temperature 0 makes sampling deterministic; the seed fixes the RNG path anyway.
    54	  "$ROOT/$dir/llama-cli" "${COMMON[@]}" -st --no-warmup -n 48 \
    55	    --temp 0 --seed 1234 -p "$PROMPT" < /dev/null > "$OUTDIR/greedy-$tag.log" 2>&1 || true
    56	  # Strip the timing banner and the prompt echo so only generated text is compared.
    57	  sed -n '/^The capital of France is/,$p' "$OUTDIR/greedy-$tag.log" | head -40
    58	}
    59	
    60	echo "### parity check: $A_DIR  vs  $B_DIR   pack=$PACK"
    61	echo "corpus: ${CORPUS#$ROOT/}  ($(stat -c %s "$CORPUS") bytes)"
    62	echo
    63	
    64	echo "--- perplexity"
    65	PPL_A="$(ppl "$A_DIR" a)"; PPL_B="$(ppl "$B_DIR" b)"
    66	echo "  $A_DIR -> $PPL_A"
    67	echo "  $B_DIR -> $PPL_B"
    68	
    69	echo
    70	echo "--- greedy tokens (-n 48, temp 0, seed 1234)"
    71	greedy "$A_DIR" a > "$OUTDIR/greedy-a.txt"
    72	greedy "$B_DIR" b > "$OUTDIR/greedy-b.txt"
    73	if diff -q "$OUTDIR/greedy-a.txt" "$OUTDIR/greedy-b.txt" >/dev/null; then
    74	  echo "  identical"
    75	  TOKENS_OK=1
    76	else
    77	  echo "  DIVERGENT:"
    78	  diff "$OUTDIR/greedy-a.txt" "$OUTDIR/greedy-b.txt" | head -10 | sed 's/^/    /'
    79	  TOKENS_OK=0
    80	fi
    81	
    82	echo
    83	# Perplexity is a mean over tokens, so tiny fp differences show as small deltas.
    84	# 0.1% is far below any real numerical breakage, which shows as whole points.
    85	if [[ -z "$PPL_A" || -z "$PPL_B" ]]; then
    86	  echo "FAIL: could not parse perplexity (see $OUTDIR)"
    87	  exit 1
    88	fi
    89	DELTA="$(python3 -c "a,b=$PPL_A,$PPL_B; print(f'{abs(a-b)/a*100:.4f}')")"
    90	echo "ppl delta: ${DELTA}%   tokens: $([[ $TOKENS_OK == 1 ]] && echo match || echo differ)"
    91	if python3 -c "import sys; sys.exit(0 if $DELTA < 0.1 else 1)"; then
    92	  echo "PASS"
    93	  echo "logs: $OUTDIR"
    94	  exit 0
    95	else
    96	  echo "FAIL: perplexity delta >= 0.1%"
    97	  echo "logs: $OUTDIR"
    98	  exit 1
    99	fi

     2	# Sample GPU and server telemetry while the model is serving.

     4	# The point is to see throughput and memory under real load, not at the start:
     5	# the counters in /metrics are monotonic, so the useful number is the delta
     6	# between samples.
     7	#
     8	# Usage:
     9	#   scripts/serve.sh start
    10	#   scripts/telemetry.sh                 # 10 samples, 3 s apart, prints a table
    11	#   scripts/telemetry.sh 5 60            # 60 samples, 5 s apart
    12	#
    13	# Output is appended to a CSV under results/ as well as printed.
    14	set -euo pipefail
    15	
    16	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    17	source "$ROOT/config/rt3060.env"
    18	
    19	INTERVAL="${1:-3}"
    20	COUNT="${2:-10}"
    21	PORT="${PORT:-8080}"
    22	HOST="${HOST:-127.0.0.1}"
    23	
    24	CSV="$ROOT/results/telemetry-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S).csv"
    25	
    26	metric() { # metric <name>
    27	  curl -s "http://$HOST:$PORT/metrics" 2>/dev/null |
    28	    awk -v k="llamacpp:$1" '$1 == k {print $2; exit}' || echo ""
    29	}
    30	
    31	echo "sampling every ${INTERVAL}s x ${COUNT}; server at http://$HOST:$PORT" >&2
    32	echo "time,vram_mib,gpu_util_pct,power_w,temp_c,sm_mhz,pred_tokens,prompt_tokens,gen_tps,prompt_tps" > "$CSV"
    33	
    34	prev_tok=""; prev_prompt=""; prev_t=""
    35	printf '%8s %9s %6s %7s %6s %8s %8s %9s\n' time VRAM_MiB GPU% W C tok/s prompt_t/s >&2
    36	
    37	for _ in $(seq 1 "$COUNT"); do
    38	  now="$(date +%H:%M:%S)"
    39	  read -r vram util power temp sm <<<"$(nvidia-smi \
    40	      --query-gpu=memory.used,utilization.gpu,power.draw,temperature.gpu,clocks.current.sm \
    41	      --format=csv,noheader,nounits | tr -d ',')"
    42	
    43	  tok="$(metric tokens_predicted_total)"
    44	  prompt="$(metric prompt_tokens_total)"
    45	  psec="$(metric tokens_predicted_seconds_total)"
    46	  prsec="$(metric prompt_seconds_total)"
    47	
    48	  gen_tps=""; prompt_tps=""
    49	  if [[ -n "$prev_tok" && -n "$tok" && "$tok" != "$prev_tok" ]]; then
    50	    gen_tps="$(python3 -c "print(f'{($tok-$prev_tok)/max($psec-${prev_psec:-0},1e-9):.1f}')" 2>/dev/null || echo "")"
    51	  fi
    52	  if [[ -n "$prompt" && -n "$prsec" ]]; then
    53	    prompt_tps="$(python3 -c "print(f'{$prompt/max($prsec,1e-9):.1f}')" 2>/dev/null || echo "")"
    54	  fi
    55	
    56	  echo "$now,$vram,$util,$power,$temp,$sm,${tok:-},${prompt:-},${gen_tps:-},${prompt_tps:-}" >> "$CSV"
    57	  printf '%8s %9s %6s %7s %6s %8s %9s\n' "$now" "$vram" "$util" "$power" "$temp" "${gen_tps:--}" "${prompt_tps:--}" >&2
    58	
    59	  prev_tok="${tok:-}"; prev_psec="${psec:-0}"; prev_prompt="${prompt:-}"
    60	  sleep "$INTERVAL"
    61	done
    62	
    63	echo >&2
    64	echo "csv: $CSV" >&2

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Fetch the pinned PrismML llama.cpp build and the Bonsai 2 27B GGUF packs.
     3	#
     4	# Everything is pinned: the fork release tag, the tarball SHA-256 (from the GitHub
     5	# release API), and the model repo. Re-running is safe and resumable.
     6	#
     7	# Usage:
     8	#   scripts/setup.sh all            # binaries + every pack (default)
     9	#   scripts/setup.sh binaries       # CUDA binary only
    10	#   scripts/setup.sh model PQ2_0    # one pack only
    11	#   scripts/setup.sh mmproj         # vision projector only
    12	set -euo pipefail
    13	
    14	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    15	BIN_DIR="$ROOT/bin/cuda"
    16	MODEL_DIR="$ROOT/models/bonsai2"
    17	mkdir -p "$BIN_DIR" "$MODEL_DIR"
    18	
    19	# --- pinned artifacts -------------------------------------------------------
    20	FORK_TAG="prism-b10743-adfffbe"
    21	FORK_URL="https://github.com/PrismML-Eng/llama.cpp/releases/download/${FORK_TAG}"
    22	BIN_ASSET="llama-${FORK_TAG}-bin-linux-cuda-12.4-x64.tar.gz"
    23	BIN_SHA256="fef4c7e8d83ff261d89809c1b302fe5f826adc1a74e13654b67ec056fbc1c639"
    24	
    25	MODEL_REPO="prism-ml/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-gguf"
    26	MODEL_BASE="https://huggingface.co/${MODEL_REPO}/resolve/main"
    27	
    28	# pack -> expected size in bytes (from the HF API; 0 = do not check)
    29	declare -A PACK_SIZE=(
    30	  [PQ2_0]=7206168928
    31	  [PTQ1_0]=5946648928
    32	  [mmproj-Q8_0]=0
    33	)
    34	
    35	log() { printf '[setup] %s\n' "$*" >&2; }
    36	die() { printf '[setup] ERROR: %s\n' "$*" >&2; exit 1; }
    37	
    38	fetch() { # fetch <url> <dest>
    39	  local url="$1" dest="$2"
    40	  if [[ -s "$dest" ]]; then
    41	    log "exists, skipping: $(basename "$dest")"
    42	    return 0
    43	  fi
    44	  log "downloading $(basename "$dest")"
    45	  curl -fL --retry 5 --retry-delay 5 -C - -o "$dest" "$url"
    46	}
    47	
    48	get_binaries() {
    49	  local tar="$BIN_DIR/$BIN_ASSET"
    50	  if [[ ! -x "$BIN_DIR/llama-bench" ]]; then
    51	    fetch "$FORK_URL/$BIN_ASSET" "$tar"
    52	    log "verifying SHA-256"
    53	    echo "$BIN_SHA256  $tar" | sha256sum -c - \
    54	      || die "tarball hash mismatch — refusing to extract"
    55	    log "extracting"
    56	    tar -xzf "$tar" -C "$BIN_DIR" --strip-components=1
    57	    rm -f "$tar"
    58	  fi
    59	  [[ -x "$BIN_DIR/llama-bench" ]] || die "llama-bench missing after extract"
    60	  log "binaries ready: $BIN_DIR"
    61	}
    62	
    63	get_pack() { # get_pack <PQ2_0|PTQ1_0>
    64	  local pack="$1"
    65	  local file="Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-${pack}.gguf"
    66	  local dest="$MODEL_DIR/$file"
    67	  local expect="${PACK_SIZE[$pack]:-0}"
    68	  fetch "$MODEL_BASE/$file" "$dest"
    69	  if [[ "$expect" != 0 ]]; then
    70	    local actual; actual=$(stat -c %s "$dest")
    71	    [[ "$actual" == "$expect" ]] \
    72	      || die "$file size $actual != expected $expect (truncated? re-run to resume)"
    73	  fi
    74	  log "model ready: $dest"
    75	}
    76	
    77	get_mmproj() {
    78	  local file="Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-mmproj-Q8_0.gguf"
    79	  fetch "$MODEL_BASE/$file" "$MODEL_DIR/$file"
    80	  log "vision projector ready (optional; ~0.63 GB)"
    81	}
    82	
    83	record_hashes() {
    84	  ( cd "$MODEL_DIR" && sha256sum ./*.gguf > SHA256SUMS 2>/dev/null ) || true
    85	  log "wrote $MODEL_DIR/SHA256SUMS"
    86	}
    87	
    88	case "${1:-all}" in
    89	  binaries) get_binaries ;;
    90	  model)    get_pack "${2:?usage: setup.sh model PQ2_0|PTQ1_0}" ; record_hashes ;;
    91	  mmproj)   get_mmproj ; record_hashes ;;
    92	  all)
    93	    get_binaries
    94	    get_pack PQ2_0
    95	    get_pack PTQ1_0
    96	    record_hashes
    97	    ;;
    98	  *) die "unknown target '$1' (all|binaries|model <pack>|mmproj)" ;;
    99	esac
   100	log "done"

    if (!batch.logits) {
        if (output_all) {
            // return the output for all tokens
            output.resize(batch.n_tokens, true);
        } else {

            output.resize(batch.n_tokens, false);

                warn = true;

        if (warn) {
            LLAMA_LOG_WARN("%s: embeddings required but some input tokens were not marked as outputs -> overriding\n", __func__);

            batch.logits = output.data();

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Correctness gate for speculative decoding: greedy output must be unchanged.
     3	#
     4	# n-gram speculation verifies drafted tokens against the target model, so with greedy
     5	# decoding it should be exact rather than approximate. This checks that claim on this
     6	# model, because a 5x speedup that silently alters output is worthless.
     7	#
     8	# Usage: scripts/spec-parity.sh <prompt-file> [spec-type]     # default ngram-simple
     9	#
    10	# Exit status: 0 if the generated text is identical, 1 otherwise.
    11	set -euo pipefail
    12	
    13	ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
    14	source "$ROOT/config/rt3060.env"
    15	
    16	PROMPT_FILE="${1:?usage: spec-parity.sh <prompt-file> [spec-type]}"
    17	SPEC="${2:-ngram-simple}"
    18	N="${N:-64}"
    19	MODEL="${MODEL:-$ROOT/models/bonsai2/Ternary-Bonsai-2-27B-${PACK:-PQ2_0}.gguf}"
    20	BIN="${BIN_DIR:-$ROOT/bin/cuda}/llama-cli"
    21	TAG="$(basename "$PROMPT_FILE" .txt)"
    22	STAMP="$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
    23	OUT="$ROOT/results/spec-parity-${TAG}-${STAMP}.txt"
    24	
    25	run() { # run <spec-type> <dest>
    26	  "$BIN" -m "$MODEL" -ngl 99 -fa on -st --no-warmup -n "$N" --temp 0 \
    27	    -f "$PROMPT_FILE" --spec-type "$1" -rea off > "$2" 2>&1 || true
    28	}
    29	
    30	{
    31	  echo "### spec parity: $SPEC vs none   prompt=$TAG   n=$N   temp=0"
    32	  echo "date: $(date -Iseconds)"
    33	  echo "model: $(basename "$MODEL")"
    34	} | tee "$OUT"
    35	
    36	run none "$OUT.none"
    37	run "$SPEC" "$OUT.spec"
    38	
    39	python3 - "$OUT.none" "$OUT.spec" >> "$OUT" <<'PY'
    40	import re, sys
    41	def clean(p):
    42	    t = open(p, errors="replace").read().split("Exiting...")[0]
    43	    # Drop banner and timing lines: those are environment, not generated text.
    44	    t = re.sub(r"^(load_backend:|ggml_cuda_init:|  Device 0:|\[ Prompt:).*$", "", t, flags=re.M)
    45	    t = re.sub(r"^\s*$", "", t, flags=re.M)
    46	    return t.strip()
    47	a, b = clean(sys.argv[1]), clean(sys.argv[2])
    48	if a == b:
    49	    print(f"RESULT: PASS - identical generated text ({len(a.splitlines())} lines)")
    50	    sys.exit(0)
    51	la, lb = a.splitlines(), b.splitlines()
    52	diff = sum(1 for x, y in zip(la, lb) if x != y) + abs(len(la) - len(lb))
    53	print(f"RESULT: FAIL - {diff} differing lines (none={len(la)}, {len(lb)})")
    54	for i, (x, y) in enumerate(zip(la, lb)):
    55	    if x != y:
    56	        print(f"  line {i}: {x[:60]!r} != {y[:60]!r}")
    57	sys.exit(1)
    58	PY
    59	STATUS=$?
    60	
    61	echo
    62	tail -1 "$OUT"
    63	echo "artifact: $OUT"
    64	exit "$STATUS"

## Verdict

**No measured result supports another large GPU-kernel speedup.** The author has, however, left one potentially large *end-to-end* optimization untested: preserving the prefix cache through **actual file-edit turns**. The categorical claims that prefill and the entire GEMV are “closed” go further than their evidence supports.

### 1. Remaining optimization

**Keep changing file content late in the prompt so earlier tokens remain cacheable.** The cache experiment appended two short questions to an unchanged 21K-token context; it did not edit text inside that context ([test driver](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/out/cache-test.py:14)). It measured 47.743 s of fresh prefill versus 0.416 s on a cached turn ([result](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/results/prefix-cache-20260927.txt:4)). Inference: if an agent rewrites a file near the start of its prompt, much of that 47.7 s can return on every turn. A prompt layout that keeps stable material first and appends the current file state or diff could save **up to roughly 47 s per otherwise invalidated 21K turn**. That is conditional: the repository has no cache-hit, latency, and edit-correctness A/B for a real editing loop. Old file versions retained in context also need to be handled explicitly.

For kernel work, the plausible gains are small. The author already identified norm and quantization fusion; its **2.12 ms/token is an absolute ceiling for removing that entire group**, about 6% of the profiled token, not a predicted gain ([decomposition](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/decode-profile.md:68)). A measured `ffn_down` shape runs at 241.8 GB/s; lifting all 1.515 GB of those weights to 275 GB/s would save an *idealized* **0.76 ms/token**, about 2% of served decode ([counter sample](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/results/ncu-served-gemv.csv), [weight shapes](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/gemv-split-rootcause.md:29)). Neither is an established speedup. I would not commission a GEMV rewrite on these numbers.

### 2. What is incorrectly closed

- **The 483 t/s prefill “roofline” uses the wrong operation count.** It charges all 26.9B parameters to every prompt token ([calculation](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/roofline-rtx3060.md:91)). I verified in the local GGUF that the embedding and output tensors each contain 1.271B weights. The embedding is a lookup, and the fork requests output for only the last token of an ordinary batch ([batch behavior](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/third_party/llama.cpp/src/llama-batch.cpp:119), [output-head graph](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/third_party/llama.cpp/src/models/qwen35.cpp:274)). Removing those two per-token matrix multiplies raises the simple `dp4a` bound to about **534 t/s**. The measured 493.8 t/s is roughly 92% of that bound, not above a hard 483 t/s ceiling. Other work consumes the gap, so this does **not** establish an 8% tile-tuning win.

- **“No GEMV kernel-side headroom” is unproved for the whole model.** ADR 0006 profiles six launches and calls the 17408-row shape saturated ([ADR](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/decisions/0006-gemv-no-headroom.md:33)). My census of the local GGUF puts that fast `ffn_gate/up` shape at **42.5% of PQ weight bytes**. The other sampled shapes are below 275 GB/s. The old 24% prize was indeed a bad comparison; replacing it with “nothing to recover” is another unjustified extrapolation. It does not imply a large recoverable gap.

- **“Thinking on means zero speculation” is false.** The later served-path result records `medium` producing **96 drafts, 51 accepted**, with reasoning present; `low` also drafts ([raw summary](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/results/reasoning-ab-20260927-235000.txt:7)). Drafts can fire during an answer after thinking. The data do not show a large whole-request benefit, but the zero-draft rule and the blanket dismissal of `low` are wrong.

- **The benchmark-shape diagnosis is right; its “lower bound” corollary is not.** The document says `llama-bench tg` understates served throughput ([claim](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/gemv-benchmark-artifact.md:44)). Its 29.55 t/s baseline is actually **higher** than the 28.8 t/s served-shape CLI baseline ([served result](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/results/bench-bin-PQ2_0-kf16vf16-20260927-234156.log:12)). Per-kernel bandwidth cannot establish end-to-end ordering.

The Hadamard result and the neutral served-shape nwarps result survive this review. CUDA graphs are visibly reused by the server, but the quoted **5.3% benefit was toggled in `llama-bench`**, so its magnitude on served requests remains unmeasured ([ADR 0005](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/decisions/0005-cuda-graphs-active.md:15)).

### 3. Evidence and method defects

- **The decisive GEMV counters are absent from the committed evidence trail.** `.gitignore` excludes `results/ncu-*.csv`, including the six-launch CSV used by ADR 0006 ([ignore rule](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/.gitignore:30)). The committed closure is a transcription. Moreover, the 275 GB/s reference uses 200,000 rows in an ordinary CUDA run ([microbenchmark](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/tools/microbench/gemv.cu:130)); the GEMV figure comes from Nsight Compute. [NVIDIA’s profiling guide](https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/) documents replay, default cache flushing, clock control, and kernel serialization. Without matched controls, **277.7/275 = 101%** is not a precise closure test.

- **The 82.6% GEMV breakdown is still quoted as though it describes serving.** Its Nsight Systems capture was truncated and used the subsequently discredited `llama-bench` decode shape ([method](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/decode-profile.md:7), [shape correction](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/gemv-benchmark-artifact.md:19)). It is a useful ranking for that capture, not a measured served-token budget.

- **The correctness gate can pass divergent greedy output.** It records `TOKENS_OK`, but its final PASS condition tests only perplexity ([script](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/scripts/parity-check.sh:70), [PASS condition](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/scripts/parity-check.sh:91)). The reported parity runs may have matched; the gate itself does not enforce the project’s required check.

- **The reasoning quality score is weaker than its label.** The two format scorers check line counts but do not check the requested status codes or `ALPHA/BETA/GAMMA` content ([scorers and tasks](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/scripts/quality-check.py:62)). The arithmetic error under `none` is real ([result](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/results/quality-by-mode-20260928.txt:5)); “5/6 versus 6/6” does not establish edit quality or general safety for mechanical work.

The cached-turn **2.9 s** in the profile combines prompt timing from short non-copying replies with decode timing from a separate copying task; it is a projection, not an observed complete turn ([cache result](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/results/prefix-cache-20260927.txt:4), [profile](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/rt3060-profile.md:50)).

**Practical conclusion:** test cache retention on representative edited-file conversations first. Treat further GPU tuning as a bounded, low-single-digit-percent possibility until a complete served-shape A/B demonstrates more.

- **The 483 t/s prefill “roofline” uses the wrong operation count.** It charges all 26.9B parameters to every prompt token ([calculation](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/roofline-rtx3060.md:91)). I verified in the local GGUF that the embedding and output tensors each contain 1.271B weights. The embedding is a lookup, and the fork requests output for only the last token of an ordinary batch ([batch behavior](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/third_party/llama.cpp/src/llama-batch.cpp:119), [output-head graph](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/third_party/llama.cpp/src/models/qwen35.cpp:274)). Removing those two per-token matrix multiplies raises the simple `dp4a` bound to about **534 t/s**. The measured 493.8 t/s is roughly 92% of that bound, not above a hard 483 t/s ceiling. Other work consumes the gap, so this does **not** establish an 8% tile-tuning win.

- **“No GEMV kernel-side headroom” is unproved for the whole model.** ADR 0006 profiles six launches and calls the 17408-row shape saturated ([ADR](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/decisions/0006-gemv-no-headroom.md:33)). My census of the local GGUF puts that fast `ffn_gate/up` shape at **42.5% of PQ weight bytes**. The other sampled shapes are below 275 GB/s. The old 24% prize was indeed a bad comparison; replacing it with “nothing to recover” is another unjustified extrapolation. It does not imply a large recoverable gap.

- **“Thinking on means zero speculation” is false.** The later served-path result records `medium` producing **96 drafts, 51 accepted**, with reasoning present; `low` also drafts ([raw summary](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/results/reasoning-ab-20260927-235000.txt:7)). Drafts can fire during an answer after thinking. The data do not show a large whole-request benefit, but the zero-draft rule and the blanket dismissal of `low` are wrong.

- **The benchmark-shape diagnosis is right; its “lower bound” corollary is not.** The document says `llama-bench tg` understates served throughput ([claim](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/gemv-benchmark-artifact.md:44)). Its 29.55 t/s baseline is actually **higher** than the 28.8 t/s served-shape CLI baseline ([served result](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/results/bench-bin-PQ2_0-kf16vf16-20260927-234156.log:12)). Per-kernel bandwidth cannot establish end-to-end ordering.

The Hadamard result and the neutral served-shape nwarps result survive this review. CUDA graphs are visibly reused by the server, but the quoted **5.3% benefit was toggled in `llama-bench`**, so its magnitude on served requests remains unmeasured ([ADR 0005](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/decisions/0005-cuda-graphs-active.md:15)).

### 3. Evidence and method defects

- **The decisive GEMV counters are absent from the committed evidence trail.** `.gitignore` excludes `results/ncu-*.csv`, including the six-launch CSV used by ADR 0006 ([ignore rule](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/.gitignore:30)). The committed closure is a transcription. Moreover, the 275 GB/s reference uses 200,000 rows in an ordinary CUDA run ([microbenchmark](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/tools/microbench/gemv.cu:130)); the GEMV figure comes from Nsight Compute. [NVIDIA’s profiling guide](https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/) documents replay, default cache flushing, clock control, and kernel serialization. Without matched controls, **277.7/275 = 101%** is not a precise closure test.

- **The 82.6% GEMV breakdown is still quoted as though it describes serving.** Its Nsight Systems capture was truncated and used the subsequently discredited `llama-bench` decode shape ([method](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/decode-profile.md:7), [shape correction](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/gemv-benchmark-artifact.md:19)). It is a useful ranking for that capture, not a measured served-token budget.

- **The correctness gate can pass divergent greedy output.** It records `TOKENS_OK`, but its final PASS condition tests only perplexity ([script](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/scripts/parity-check.sh:70), [PASS condition](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/scripts/parity-check.sh:91)). The reported parity runs may have matched; the gate itself does not enforce the project’s required check.

- **The reasoning quality score is weaker than its label.** The two format scorers check line counts but do not check the requested status codes or `ALPHA/BETA/GAMMA` content ([scorers and tasks](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/scripts/quality-check.py:62)). The arithmetic error under `none` is real ([result](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/results/quality-by-mode-20260928.txt:5)); “5/6 versus 6/6” does not establish edit quality or general safety for mechanical work.

The cached-turn **2.9 s** in the profile combines prompt timing from short non-copying replies with decode timing from a separate copying task; it is a projection, not an observed complete turn ([cache result](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/results/prefix-cache-20260927.txt:4), [profile](/home/penta/repos/eobonsai/docs/rt3060-profile.md:50)).

**Practical conclusion:** test cache retention on representative edited-file conversations first. Treat further GPU tuning as a bounded, low-single-digit-percent possibility until a complete served-shape A/B demonstrates more.
